# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2006

## Introduction by Dravida dāsa

This year marks the fortieth anniversary of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s founding of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, and in their homages to him many devotees looked back on these four decades of history. One of the prominent themes that appear as devotees survey ISKCON past and present is the abiding presence of Śrīla Prabhupāda in His books and other instructions, in the institution itself, and most of all in the hearts of his sincere followers. As always at this time of year, devotees are overwhelmed with gratitude and pray for the blessings of Śrīla Prabhupāda to perform there sometimes difficult services in a way that will please him and the Lord.

In several places around the world devotees are struggling to build temples, and in some they have just completed them. All these devotees thank Śrīla Prabhupāda for the inspiration to overcome the daunting difficulties involved in such an endeavor. Here is how the devotee who wrote the offering on behalf of the Budapest *yātrā* expressed his appreciation to Śrīla Prabhupāda for being the guiding and inspiring force behind the construction of the new temple there:

Such wonderful arrangements are possible only by your mercy. As you always gave the right solution to your disciples and showed the right way, you made the necessary arrangements for us, too. So in a wonderful way you made our faith deeper in you, Kṛṣṇa, and in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You were the master who accomplished this project; we were only unworthy and useless tools in your work. When people come and see this beautiful building, gaze at the Deities standing on Their wonderful altar, and enjoy the nice atmosphere, it is all your glory.

As every year now, several offerings remembered dear departed Vaiṣṇavas, and how they showed all of us examples of undying faith in Śrīla Prabhupāda till the end. Here is an excerpt from Rādhānātha Swami’s offering:

Earlier this year . . . Stoka Kṛṣṇa Prabhu was also taken from us. He was only a boy in his early thirties. When he was unexpectedly diagnosed with incurable, swift-acting cancer, his wife was pregnant with his first child. As he lay paralyzed from the waist down and emaciated with pain, he became the father of a beautiful daughter. Everything was soon to be stripped away from him. Still he always smiled in joy, grateful for whatever came into his life. His words still ring in my heart:

Why should I be disturbed by dying at such a young age. I am grateful.

Why was I allowed to associate with devotees for even a moment? I did not deserve it.

Why was I ever blessed to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission for even a minute? I am totally unqualified.

Why was I allowed to chant Kṛṣṇa’s names even once? I am such an offender. Yet still in this lifetime I was given all these blessings.

Why was I given the supreme benediction of being the servant of the servant of the devo-tees? I am so fallen, I did not deserve such a blessing.

Then with sincere tears in his eyes he poured out his heart:

I feel nothing but gratitude and joy that in this short lifetime I received so much of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s causeless mercy.

Such extraordinary and heartrending expressions of love for Śrīla Prabhupāda will forever shine as ideals for his followers as they approach “the final exam.”

From Togo to Tyumen to Tallahassee, devotees have made this year’s Vyasa-puja book a grand chorus of gratitude and glorification of the great general in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Draviḍa Dāsa  
Vyāsa-pūjā book editor

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Gaurāṅga Kiśora Dāsa

Vyāsa-pūjā is the day we honor the Lord’s pure representative, the transparent medium for His teachings and mercy. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja explains in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that he knows his spiritual master (Nityānanda Prabhu) to be the servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, but he also knows Him to be a plenary expansion of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus Śrī Nityānanda Balarāma is the original manifestation of the guru and also a direct plenary expansion of the Lord. As Śrī Balarāma eternally serves Śrī Kṛṣṇa in so many ways, and as Nityānanda Balarāma eternally serves Śrī Krsna Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the guru eternally serves Lord Kṛṣṇa and gives spiritual strength (*bala*) to his surrendered disciples. Indeed, the guru is a manifestation of Śrī Balarāma, as Śrīla Prabhupāda states in his comments on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.14.28:

Balarāma is the protector of the devotees of the Lord. By His divine grace only one can approach the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and thus Śrī Balarāma is the mercy incarnation of the Lord, manifested as the spiritual master, the savior of the pure devotees.

The nature of the Lord is that He manifests his compassion through His dearmost devotees. Therefore the guru is the embodiment and reservoir of the Lord’s mercy. The guru is the means by which the Lord expresses his love and compassion for the conditioned souls. It is said in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 1.45) that by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa coming through the guru the disciple is delivered. The guru is the means by which the conditioned souls can become connected to Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa in his original transcendental form is far beyond the reach of the conditioned souls’ senses and mind. But in His form as the guru He is accessible to the conditioned souls.

So the essence of Vyāsa-pūjā is expressing gratitude for the Supreme Lord’s causeless mercy descending through His transparent medium, Śrī Gurudeva. When Śrī Balarāmajī entered the assembly of Romaharṣaṇa Sūta, the latter did not properly respect Him, and for this offense Śrī Balarāma touched Romaharṣaṇa with a blade of grass and killed him. This same Balarāma, in his *avatāra* as Śrī Nityānanda, took a blade of grass between his teeth, approached the most proud and ignorant sinners, and begged them to accept the mercy of the Lord by chanting His holy name, worshiping Him, and following His instructions. This is the mood of Śrī Guru.

Locana Dāsa Ṭhākura has described Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu as supremely merciful, the crest jewel of all *avatāras.* They are the most munificent, for they freely distributed love of God without considering who was fit or unfit to receive it. The most famous recipients of Their mercy were the two wretched sinners Jagāi and Mādhāi.

And in the modern age it was His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda who revealed the full extent of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi’s mercy. The two Lords delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi, but Śrīla Prabhupāda was personally empowered by Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi to deliver millions of Jagāis and Mādhāis. He was that great personality whom Caitanya Mahāprabhu deputed to be the *senapati,* the general in command of the *saṅkīrtana* army. Caitanya Mahāprabhu revealed the extent of His mercy through Śrīla Prabhupāda by freely distributing to everyone what had never before been made available. Śrīla Prabhupāda distributed the most rare treasure, the pure nectar of love of God, as if it were water. On this day we honor him as the pure representative of Vyāsa, the manifestation of Lord Baladeva, and the personification of the Lord’s mercy on the fallen souls.

O Lord,  
Seeing me suffering miseries and fears,  
You cried an ocean with your compassionate tears.  
That ocean was sentient and full of love;  
It is your mercy descending from above.  
It now has form and travels this earth;  
He is my eternal lord, birth after birth.  
I was a cave-dweller in a world of shadows,  
Chained and shackled, being dragged to the gallows.  
What I thought was reality was only illusion,  
Happiness a mirage — what a great confusion!  
He has opened my eyes and shown me the way;  
My debt to him I can never repay.  
By his example I am continually learning,  
All by divine grace — I am so undeserving.  
He has given this gift without expectation of return;  
This sublime mercy I did nothing to earn.  
Personally I have no hope of attaining perfection;  
I can only hope he glances mercifully in my direction.

Gaurāṅga Kiśora Dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

This year, 2006, is the fortieth anniversary of ISKCON, the society you crafted to “check the imbalance of values in life and to achieve real unity and peace in the world.”

There are many peace missions in the world, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Many of them are active in Washington, D.C., where I live. They come in a variety of sizes and shapes. Some are international, some are local; some are public, some private; some are conservative, some liberal; some achieve significant results, others do not. Yet all are flawed because they fail to understand the source of peace. They do not know, or propagate, that Śrī Kṛṣṇa is “the ultimate beneficiary,”“the Supreme Lord of all,” and “the well-wisher of all living entities.”

That mission was given to ISKCON. You envisioned that your society would do great things. You told us early on that your time on earth was limited. Therefore, you created a society to carry on your important work after you departed from this physical world.

Forty is an age when men and women take stock of themselves and their lives. They often go through (yet another) identity crisis, seeking the essential as their time races by. ISKCON is also seeking to clarify its identity, seeking to refocus its cooperative energy, seeking to understand where it can best effect change in a turbulent world.

Please continue to guide us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as we seek to “work cooperatively” in showing our love for you. We all want to go our separate ways. It appears so much easier. Rather than surrendering to your society and its all-too-human leadership, it is tempting to place my individualized interpretation of your wishes first and leave the troubles of community living far behind.

But I am your servant, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That means I must become the servant of your wishes, the servant of your vision, the servant of your order, and the servant of your servants. I must remain the servant of your International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

I am greatly flawed, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It gives me much pain to see my shortcomings, my mistakes, and my inability to serve you and your mission properly. Yet I must try to serve and not abandon your order.

Please bless your servants, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Bless us to understand that service to you *means* sustaining the instrument you crafted “to bring real unity and peace” into the world. If there are flaws in your ISKCON, let me work to repair them. If there is disharmony, let me humble myself to find common ground. If there is lack of faith, let me become faithful. If there is lack of leadership, let me teach by faithful following.

Please bless your ISKCON on its fortieth anniversary, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Bless us with faith in your order, faith that by serving your mission in a cooperative spirit, all good will be done.

Your aspiring servant,  
Anuttama Dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Periodically, discussions arise about whether or not Śrīla Prabhupāda was perfect. To some this means, Was he omniscient? Did he know all things past, present, and future? At the other end of the spectrum, there are those who say that while sitting on the *vyāsāsana* and quoting *śāstra* Śrīla Prabhupāda may have been perfect, but outside of that, he was influenced by a Victorian/Bengali worldview.

Neither of these arguments interests me. As for omniscience, when asked about this in Los Angeles in 1970, Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “I know what Kṛṣṇa wants me to know.” As for cultural conditioning, there is no doubt that Śrīla Prabhupāda was a “Vaikuṇṭha man.” He was not limited to any particular time or place.

I have to say that, to my vision, Śrīla Prabhupāda was completely perfect.

His ability to cut to the essence was perfect. When asked why the death rate was so high in India, he countered, “The death rate is the same everywhere: 100%.”

When foolishly asked, “Is the world flat?” he answered, “Everywhere I walk it is,” and then added with a chuckle, “except for New Vrindāban.”

Someone challenged, “I say we have evolved from monkeys,” to which Śrīla Prabhupāda countered, “Where is the question of evolution? I say you are still monkeys.”

A man said, “Sooner or later, I will surrender to Kṛṣṇa.”Śrīla Prabhupāda advised, “Better sooner. Do you know you have a later?”

He was perfect in his compassion. When one of his *sannyāsīs* saw a poor woman picking through the trash, he turned to Śrīla Prabhupāda and said, “Sometimes I feel so sorry for these people.”Śrīla Prabhupāda looked at him with a gaze laden with mercy and questioned, “Why just sometimes?”

A man shouted at Śrīla Prabhupāda during a *darśana* and had to be hustled out of the room. The devotees were quite unnerved and began to speculate as to the offender’s future. Would he be sent to some hellish planet? Would he be born in a decrepit condition here on earth? How would he become free of his offense? After the discussion went back and forth among the devotees for some time, Śrīla Prabhupāda looked up and offered, “We could forgive him,” an option no one else had thought of.

As Śrīla Prabhupāda prepared to give an arrival address, he looked out over the audience of college students, hippies, factory workers, inner city street kids, Indian families, and even a few motorcyclists dressed in full leather regalia. He said to himself, softly but with determination, “Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet are big enough for everyone.”

He was perfect in humility. While he explained the Vedic system of four *varṇas* and four *āśramas,* an agitated communist interrupted him, challenging, “And I suppose you think you are first class?”Śrīla Prabhupāda stopped him cold by answering with deep conviction: “No, I am fifth class. I am a servant of all the other classes.”

He asked that a picture of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura be hung behind his *darśana* desk. When the devotees came in to do so, he was sitting there working. They began to back out of the room, saying they would return later. He instructed them to go ahead with the job, but they protested, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are afraid we might bump into you in the process.” With complete innocence he replied, “What? For service, you can stand on my head.”

He was perfect in bravery. A morning walk in Kolkata took him past the massive Victoria Memorial, towering two hundred feet above the park. He related how as a young boy, when it was being built, he scaled the scaffolding to the very top. A devotee offered, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, you must have been very brave.” He replied, “Yes, and I am still brave. Otherwise, how did I come to America all alone?”

He was perfect as a disciple. His personal assistant was so impressed by a powerful series of evening lectures Śrīla Prabhupāda was giving in London that while offering the daily massage he felt like he was getting a world champion ready for a prize fight. With youthful enthusiasm he was going on and on with praise, when suddenly Śrīla Prabhupāda slapped his hand on the mat and said firmly, “No. I am simply the servant of my guru mahārāja.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda gave a small glimpse into his perfect love and dedication to his guru mahārāja in the following excerpt from his concluding words to the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta:*

Although according to material vision His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda passed away from this material world on the last day of December, 1936, I still consider His Divine Grace to be always present with me by his *vāṇī,* his words. . . . I think that His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words. It is to be admitted that whatever translation work I have done is through the inspiration of my spiritual master, because personally I am most insignificant and incompetent to do this materially impossible work. I do not think myself a very learned scholar, but I have full faith in the service of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. If there is any credit to my activities of translating, it is all due to His Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was perfect in his surrender to the order of his guru mahārāja. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura instructed Śrīla Prabhupāda, “Go to the West!” and it became Śrīla Prabhupāda’s life’s mission. Others told him that no one beyond the shores of India would take up the teachings of Lord Caitanya. He was cautioned that it would be freezing cold and that he would have great difficulty finding vegetarian ingredients for cooking. “Then let me starve,” was his response. He was warned, “You will die of a heart attack.” Yet he still left the sweet shelter of Vṛndāvana and arrived in the land of the *mlecchas,* alone and unassisted. “When I got off the boat, I did not know whether to turn left or right.”

He was perfect in his disappearance from this world. Voltaire, the infamous atheist, called for a priest in his last hours. Many of those who preached that it is all Brahman have left this world hooked up to machines and begging for a few more days.

In contrast, Śrīla Prabhupāda never wavered and never faltered. When the devotees begged him to stop traveling and rest, he replied, “I want the benediction to fight like Arjuna until my last breath. If a warrior dies on the battlefield, is it not glorious?” In perfect appreciation of and reciprocation with his mood of selfless devotion, Kṛṣṇa allowed Śrīla Prabhupāda to leave this world in Vṛndāvana, giving the world his Bhaktivedanta purports until nearly his last breath.

He was perfect in his instruction. Confirming his empowerment directly by Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrīla Prabhupāda managed to present the path of the Six Gosvāmīs in such a way that even the most fallen could take it up. At the same time, the highest *līlās* and most intimate understandings lie embedded in his Bhaktivedanta purports, to be revealed as the heart becomes purified.

The simple truth is that the more I surrender to following each and every one of his instructions, the more I realize how perfect they are. They are perfect for happiness now, perfect for happiness in the future, and perfect for offering the best service to all humankind. There is no way I can ever properly express my indebtedness and gratitude toward the most perfect spiritual master and friend, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

His most insignificant servant,  
Badrinārāyaṇ Dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Today we are beginning the Durban Ratha-yātrā festival, which will be held over four days on the Durban beachfront. Thirteen *sannyāsīs* have come from all over the world to participate and give their association to the public and to the hundreds of devotees who have gathered from all over the country. By the time the festival finishes, everyone will be deeply spiritually enriched and surcharged, and in this way they and the whole movement here will have progressed because of the association they will have received.

You said, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you created ISKCON so that people could get the association of devotees and in this way take part in hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa. You said:

The purport is that one cannot live outside the society of devotees; one must live in the association of devotees, where there is constant chanting and hearing of the glories of the Lord. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is started for this purpose, so that hundreds of ISKCON centers may give people a chance to hear and chant, to accept the spiritual master, and to disassociate themselves from persons who are materially interested, for in this way one can make solid advancement in going back home, back to Godhead. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.23, purport)

You have entered the hearts of your sincere followers, and your presence is reflected in their personalities and activities. Through their association we get yours, and in this way transformation occurs.

We see your attachment to the holy names reflected in the hearts of so many of your disciples, who more and more love to chant. In a morning walk you once said:

Quality \[in chanting\], no quality—it is not your position to understand. When the quality comes, there is no force. You will have a taste for chanting. You will desire at that time, “Why sixteen rounds? Why not sixteen *thousand* rounds?” That is quality. That is quality. \[Now\] it is by force. You’ll not do it; therefore at least sixteen rounds. But when you come to the quality, you will feel yourself, “Why sixteen? Why not sixteen *thousand*?” That is quality, automatically. Just like Haridāsa Ṭhākura was doing. He was not forced to do. Even Caitanya Mahāprabhu, He requested, “Now you are old enough. You can reduce.” So he refused, “No. Up to the end of my life I shall go on.” That is quality.

With years of practice in chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, many of your followers are advancing and are happy to chant all the time. In this way they reflect your natural quality of attachment for chanting, and it spreads from them to others.

Your preaching and purports are so profound, reflecting the association you receive from your spiritual master and from the previous *ācāryas*. As your disciples progress, they reflect this in their own preaching and writing, and thus everything becomes deeper and more potent. One of your disciples told us that you wanted your disciples to write commentaries on your purports. You expect us to continue your “family tradition” by presenting transcendental literature.

You told one disciple, “You should write. This is your first business. Go on writing. We require many, many articles about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. So you should devote yourself to writing.” Now we see the BBT has published the *Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta,* with purports by one of your disciples, and they are worthy of being presented under the insignia of your book trust. Your profundity in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is being reflected in your disciples’ hearts.

You are so humorous, without becoming frivolous, and your humor always brings out a Kṛṣṇa conscious point of great value. Your disciples also reflect this. One of your disciples told me, “In the relationship between spiritual master and disciple there is mercy, and there is a burden. The disciple gets the mercy, and the spiritual master gets the burden!” When I heard this I laughed, but then I appreciated what a wise observation it was. Your own disciple was reflecting some of the depth of your transcendental sense of humor.

There are so many examples one could give of so many of your innumerable qualities, and how they are being reflected in your sincere followers. These are just a few. The principle is the same as that spoken of by Lord Brahmā in *Brahma-saṁhitā:*

*bhāsvān yathāśma-sakaleṣu nijeṣu tejaḥ*  
*svīyaṁ kiyat prakaṭayaty api tadvad atra*  
*brahmā ya eṣa jagad-aṇḍa-vidhāna-kartā*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi*

**“I adore the primeval Lord, Govinda, from whom the separated subjective portion Brahmā receives his power for the regulation of the mundane world, just as the sun manifests a portion of his own light in all the effulgent gems that bear such names as** ***sūrya-kānta*****.” (*****Brahma-saṁhitā*** **5.49)**

We know that you remain like the sun in relation to the *sūrya-kānta* gem, which just reflects a little of the sun’s effulgence. But still even those small reflections are glorious and help to light up this dark material world. I remember how astonished I was when I saw you dance in the London Ratha-yātrā in 1973. During our festival in Durban your disciples and other followers will also dance and chant in ecstasy, following in your footsteps, and this will also affect the hearts of many onlookers. As a result, some will become devotees themselves in due course.

I pray that some of the attachment your sincere followers have for you will also become reflected in my heart.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

As my spiritual master you always reside in my heart and you are always aware of what I am doing. Yet for my own purification I need to express my love for you and tell you how I have been serving your divine lotus feet, especially on this very special day of your divine appearance.

You always told us that we must think big and plan big in order to achieve something worthwhile for Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and we are His eternal servants; therefore whatever we do must be befitting His absolute position. As His servants, we simply have to desire to please Him, and then all that we need will be provided by His divine arrangement. If we can maintain that mood, then all our sincere endeavors to serve Him will undoubtedly meet with glorious success. I am so fortunate that you revealed this most wonderful secret to me and inspired me to do something worthwhile for Your Divine Grace.

During our first meeting you asked me to translate your books into Bengali. When that project was completed in 1996, I embarked on producing a television serial on Your Divine Grace’s life for the Indian National Television channel. When that project was completed in 2003, you brought me to the holy land of Ujjain to develop a mega-project for Your Divine Grace on three hundred acres of land donated by the Madhya Pradesh Government.

The first stage of this project consisted of building a ten-thousand-square-foot marble temple in the most prestigious part of the city, along with an *āśrama* for the devotees, a twenty-eight-room guesthouse, a restaurant, and an auditorium.

This temple was completed in less than a year, and the inauguration took place on February 10, 2006, on Nityānanda-trayodaśī, as planned. About two thousand devotees came from all over the world, and the occasion was a grand success. The festival was graced by many *sannyāsīs,* dignitaries, and celebrities. It was considered the greatest event in Ujjain in recent years.

The temple was carved out of sparkling white marble, and the deities — Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana, Lalitā-Viśākhā, Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, and Gaura-Nitāi — have manifested their extraordinary beauty. Many devotees commented that they have never seen such beautiful deities before.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for bringing me to this very special place. I never thought of building a temple and getting involved in worshiping the Deities. As a *sannyāsī,* I used to think that traveling and preaching was my main occupation. However, now I am getting the taste of developing a direct relationship with the Lord of my heart. Please continue to guide me toward His lotus feet and allow me develop my loving relationship with Him. It is only by your mercy that I can win the favor of the Transcendental Couple. I do not know what devotional service actually means, but I know that some day, because of Your Divine Grace, it will be revealed to me. Until then, please allow me to remain in ISKCON, in the association of your dedicated devotees, as a speck of dust at your divine lotus feet.

Your aspiring servant,  
Bhakti Chāru Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet! All glories to you!

**It’s All Your Doing**

Your descent to the U.S.A.  
Was a draw to pull us away  
From the culture of the gun —  
All the trigger-happy fun.  
You set our fingers to bead and string,  
Gave us a song that we could sing,  
A different tune, a different drum,  
An event to which all would come.  
We turned down fish, fowl, and cow;  
Our palate knows better now;  
*Prasād* is not just food,  
But a product of a devotional mood.  
The notion that “I am Christian or Jew,  
I’m a Buddhist or I’m a Hindu”—  
Reason tells I’m none of the above,  
But a spirit in Kṛṣṇa’s love.  
Desires were many-branched,  
Like splinters deeply entrenched.  
You pulled out those rocks ’n’ rolls  
To offer real rhythm for us souls.  
It’s a makeover — a reversal of thoughts;  
It’s an untying of prejudiced knots.  
Hindu myths are now mysteries  
Of genuine chronicled histories.  
Idols are now icons,  
Rights are now wrongs,  
We even denied denim pants,  
For in *dhotīs* and saris we now dance.  
You revived ancient and lost arts,  
Events such as the Festival of the Carts.  
For us they are with us to stay;  
For the mainstream it’s a short distance away.  
You direct us where pilgrims go,  
To lands sacred that *ṛṣis* know.  
Bhārat soil has become our attraction,  
An annual flight, a migratory action.  
The word “service” has now a different meaning,  
Even the toilet is attractive at the time of cleaning.  
For us followers, the sun always rises,  
For you gave and continue to give endless surprises.

Your humble servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I humbly offer my obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In this year’s Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I am writing a few lines from a small island country called Barbados in the Caribbean, about three hours and twenty minutes away by plane from Miami.

This island is extremely beautiful. Here sugarcane grows profusely and the breadfruit trees decorate the island graciously. The little birds are singing beautiful prayer music at 4:40 am. I also feel the pulse of your divine mission even in this remote corner of the globe. There are about 300,000 people on this island, and a few sincere souls are already practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness here.

Yesterday I addressed the students and the faculty members at the University of the West Indies, Barbados, on the topic “Science and Spirituality in Understanding Life’s Value and Meaning — a Vedāntic Perspective” for about one and a half hours. The audience was very receptive and inquisitive, and the students and professors alike showed great interest in this topic. I explained the meaning of science, scientific exploration, and the science of God in the light of Your Divine Grace’s instructions and explanations.

Many interesting questions were raised from the audience. In this connection, Your Divine Grace taught me how to use the available modern scientific knowledge to corroborate the conclusions of the teachings of genuine scriptures. This is the heart of your divine instruction on scientific preaching. Your Divine Grace never instructed us to undertake rigorous material scientific research work. Rather, you emphatically stated on several occasions that whatever scientific knowledge we already learned was sufficient and that we should use that knowledge to support the validity of the teachings of *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. That is why we all left rigorous scientific research work after joining your divine mission — in order not to grind the brain for the mundane so-called scientific discovery instead of devotional service. Therefore, Your Divine Grace mercifully instructed me that we do not need to undertake any rigorous research. Research has already been done by expert sages and saintly people, and the results are already available in the scriptures. We have to be expert in how to collect whatever scientific knowledge is available and try to support the scriptural conclusions. For the benefit of all, Your Divine Grace has wonderfully explained this understanding in the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.22:

*idaṁ hi puṁsas tapasaḥśrutasya vā*  
*sviṣṭasya sūktasya ca buddhi-dattayoḥ*  
*avicyuto ’rthaḥ kavibhir nirūpito*  
*yad-uttamaśloka-guṇānuvarṇanam*

**“Learned circles have positively concluded that the infallible purpose of the advancement of knowledge, namely austerities, study of the** ***Vedas,*** **sacrifice, chanting of hymns, and charity, culminates in the transcendental descriptions of the Lord, who is defined in choice poetry.”**

Human intellect is developed for advancement of learning in art, science, philosophy, physics, chemistry, psychology, economics, politics, etc. By culture of such knowledge the human society can attain perfection of life. This perfection of life culminates in the realization of the Supreme Being, Viṣṇu. The *śruti* therefore directs that those who are actually advanced in learning should aspire for the service of Lord Viṣṇu. Unfortunately, persons who are enamored by the external beauty of *viṣṇu-māyā* do not understand that culmination of perfection or self-realization depends on Viṣṇu. *Viṣṇu-māyā* means sense enjoyment, which is transient and miserable. Those who are entrapped by *viṣṇu-māyā* utilize advancement of knowledge for sense enjoyment. Śrī Nārada Muni has explained that all paraphernalia of the cosmic universe is but an emanation from the Lord out of His different energies because the Lord has set in motion, by His inconceivable energy, the actions and reactions of the created manifestation. They have come to be out of His energy, they rest on His energy, and after annihilation they merge into Him. Nothing is, therefore, different from Him, but at the same time the Lord is always different from them.

When advancement of knowledge is applied in the service of the Lord, the whole process becomes absolute. The Personality of Godhead and His transcendental name, fame, glory, etc., are all nondifferent from Him. Therefore, all the sages and devotees of the Lord have recommended that the subject matter of art, science, philosophy, physics, chemistry, psychology, and all other branches of knowledge should be wholly and solely applied in the service of the Lord. Art, literature, poetry, painting, etc., may be used in glorifying the Lord. The fiction writers, poets, and celebrated litterateurs are generally engaged in writing of sensuous subjects, but if they turn towards the service of the Lord they can describe the transcendental pastimes of the Lord. Vālmīki was a great poet, and similarly Vyāsadeva is a great writer, and both of them have absolutely engaged themselves in delineating the transcendental activities of the Lord and by doing so have become immortal. Similarly, science and philosophy also should be applied in the service of the Lord. There is no use presenting dry speculative theories for sense gratification. Philosophy and science should be engaged to establish the glory of the Lord. Advanced people are eager to understand the Absolute Truth through the medium of science, and therefore a great scientist should endeavor to prove the existence of the Lord on a scientific basis. Similarly, philosophical speculations should be utilized to establish the Supreme Truth as sentient and all-powerful. Similarly, all other branches of knowledge should always be engaged in the service of the Lord. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* also the same is affirmed. All “knowledge” not engaged in the service of the Lord is but nescience. Real utilization of advanced knowledge is to establish the glories of the Lord, and that is the real import. Scientific knowledge engaged in the service of the Lord and all similar activities are all factually *hari-kīrtana,* or glorification of the Lord.

Since your divine movement is expanding widely, I feel a great need to understand and apply properly your divine teachings for the benefit of all the sincere seekers of spiritual knowledge as taught by Your Divine Grace, especially in the field of scientific presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When people misinterpret your divine teachings and instructions, it can cause many difficulties, and it may mislead many innocent seekers of truth. Therefore, it may not serve the purpose of your divine mission. Such a trend in our society can only be corrected by understanding and following the direct instructions of Your Divine Grace.

I am directly appealing to Your Divine Grace for your kind mercy to all of us so that we can all properly understand your divine instructions directly. Without this understanding, when some individuals misinterpret your divine instruction, it will cause great disturbance and the truth may be lost. This danger is there.

Recently, a major scientific conference was held at U.C. Berkeley, honoring Prof. Charles Townes’ 90th birthday. Charles is one of the most prominent living scientists of the world who speaks in public about God and supports that God is a person. He is known as one the greatest physicists of our time. He received the Nobel Prize in 1964 for discovering laser and maser. He was also awarded the Templeton Prize last year for his contribution in promoting science and religion dialog. He gave the keynote address in the Second World Congress for the Synthesis of Science and Religion, held at Kolkata in January 1997, which was organized by the Bhaktivedanta Institute as a part of the Centennial Appearance Celebration of Your Divine Grace. He and his wife and a few other scientists also visited Śrī Māyāpur *dhāma* just after the congress. They were extremely impressed by the magnitude of Your Divine Grace’s spiritual organization and your contribution to the world for the welfare of humanity by the propagation of the divine culture of ŚrīGaurāṅga’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

The conference was quite significant in the sense that it had some spiritual content for a serious discussion among the participants in a major scientific conference. Some of the topics under the major theme “Amazing Light: Visions for Discovery,” were “The Ocean of Truth — Exploring the Great Unknowns in Physics and Cosmology”; “The ‘Big Picture’ — Exploring Questions on the Boundaries of Science”; “Is Life Based on the Laws of Physics?”; “Consciousness and Free Will: Special Interdisciplinary Panel Discussion.” For the first time, over a thousand scientists, including twenty Nobel laureates in physics from all over the world, gathered at the prestigious Zellerbach Hall. This was a major historic conference. The Bhaktivedanta Institute (Kolkata branch) was invited to participate in this conference, and a few of us did. For the first time in a major scientific conference, the topic of consciousness and free will was seriously discussed. This is the topic that Charles himself put up for a serious discussion. His opinion is well known among leading world scientists. In fact, he strongly supports the role of spirituality in both scientific and religious works. The panelists included physicists, neuroscientists, theologians, and philosophers. In a letter Your Divine Grace wrote: “When the scientist, philosopher, \[and\] medical man will get the opportunity to study systematically what is God, what are His energies, what is my relationship, how to approach Him, then only there will be paradise.” I can only internally feel that now Your Divine Grace would be very pleased with such a development in the scientific community. The interest is growing significantly among the prominent scientists of the world regarding the dialogue between scientific knowledge and spiritual wisdom, with the aim of understanding the deeper meaning of human life and the nature of the ultimate reality.

At this critical juncture, the contribution of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as taught by Your Divine Grace is getting more and more significant. For the pleasure of Your Divine Grace, I would like to report that within the last three years the Bhaktivedanta Institute (Kolkata) has been recognized as one of the global LSI units and awarded annually for its excellent works by the Metanexus Institute, USA, which promotes worldwide the dialogue of science and religion for the welfare of humanity.

On this most auspicious day of the divine appearance of Your Divine Grace, I humbly beg for your divine mercy and guidance at every moment.

Your Divine Grace’s most insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

**Vṛndāvana one evening**

I felt you were close,  
Thought of your lotus feet,  
More soft than a rose.  
Time is passing on,  
Memories fade away,  
Trying to serve your mission,  
Forever you will stay.  
“Cooperate with each other,”  
You commanded us all,  
“Then your love will be shown”—  
Are we hearing your call?  
Kindly help us, O master,  
Or else we go astray.  
In an hour of need,  
We helplessly pray.  
“Chant and be happy”—  
So simple and sublime.  
You taught us everything,  
Compassionate and divine.  
Feeling your separation,  
Will your mercy be shown?  
Taking shelter in your service,  
We will never be alone.

Your insignificant humble servant,  
A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! How wonderful it would be to again sit in your physical presence, at your lotus feet.

In the ’60s, when ISKCON was first incorporated, I and others dreamed the entire world would be chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa within our lifetime. We dreamed that all ISKCON members would be pure devotees within ten years! We were unable to predict the tremendous and various hardships we would have to endure simply to remain as practitioners, serving within your Society.

Now in 2006, we, your disciples, are no longer the “boys and girls” of the ’60s and ’70s. We are nearly senior citizens—old men and old ladies. Yet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you hardly envisioned us then as boys or girls, nor would you now see us as old men and ladies, for a person’s consciousness determines his vision.

I’ve heard you were once asked in an airport what you saw as you viewed the frenetic men and women on the go. “So many flapping dead bodies” was your reply. And you also saw the world of ISKCON, your Society, from the viewpoint of eternity, envisioning another ISKCON in Goloka Vṛndāvana, the spiritual world.

Of course, when either this world or ISKCON is envisioned from a more temporally limited platform, the practical platform of day-to-day living, a different vision appears. The material world may look extreme in its appeal, and ISKCON extreme in its faults.

Noble as ISKCON’S mission may be, how should we view those faults? Should that platform be ignored? How should we overcome the difficulties inherent in cooperating with the many varieties of others within an institution existing in Kali-yuga, the Iron Age of quarrel and discontent? Should we attempt to correct those faults? Should we tolerate them? Neither? Both?

Clearly, we should dutifully do our best to correct what we see as incorrect, but we should also try our best to tolerate the difficulties these faults inflict upon us—for the purpose of pleasing you and assisting you in accomplishing your mission: “It is said that great personalities almost always accept voluntary suffering because of the suffering of the people in general. This is considered the highest method of worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is present in everyone’s heart.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.7.44)

In letters to your disciples, you offer the method of attaining such an exalted level of tolerance: offenseless chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy names.

All of you are coming from backgrounds of bad habits, so to make progress in Krishna Consciousness we must overlook the faults of others. You must be patient with your husband. Chanting offenselessly is the remedy for all of these diseases. \[Letter to Vidarbha-kanyā Devī Dāsī, 19 August 1974\]

and

The difference between a devotee and a nondevotee is this, just like the bee and the fly: the bee always is attracted by the honey and flies go to the open sores. So the devotee is only attracted by the good qualities in other people and does not see their faults. He is friend to everyone and in this way he finds his life sublime. Of course we are not ourselves perfect, so sometimes there may be something, and we may lose our enthusiasm to do something—but these things come and go and you should not bother very much with them. Just be attached to Krishna and try always to think how to please Him, and He will personally rescue His devotee from all kinds of unfavorable situations. Even we may not like to do the work, if we work very hard for Krishna soon we lose our attachment and become satisfied just to be serving Krishna, and as long as at least 16 offenseless rounds daily are being chanted, know it for certain that our anxieties will disappear very soon. \[Letter to Badrinārāyaṇ Dāsa, 18 November 1971)

Despite ISKCON’S imperfections, Śrīla Prabhupāda, its mission is great, for ISKCON’S mission is your mission: to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness (*kṛṣṇa-bhakti-rasa-bhāvitā*—the soul’s absorption in the mellows of executing devotional service to Kṛṣṇa) throughout the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, after having offered you the above noble-sounding words, I admit my own dependence on your mercy to even spiritually survive, what to speak of being of assistance to you. In Manila in 1972, as you, Jagattāriṇī Mātājī, and I sat alone in a room in the Hotel Intercontinental awaiting the start of your lecture, you requested: “My desire is that all living beings in the universe go back to Godhead. You please help me with this desire.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have offered you my life. Though I am weak, faulty, yet proud, my deepest desire is nevertheless to serve you. Surely I test your tolerance. Mercifully forgive my many faults and offenses and kindly inspire and engage me as you desire. Please award me the ability to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* attentively and without offense.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Aspiring actually to be your servant,  
Bhūrijana Dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your appearance and activities, which are both fully transcendental. By meditating on your appearance and activities, one can transcend the path of birth and death.

The spiritual master is described as “the eternal father” of the disciple. The veracity of this statement is shown by your interactions with us, your disciples. Your relationship as eternal father is not simply some metaphorical poetic statement but a reality. In fact, you are more fatherly than any father of this world. Here are some examples of your superfatherly actions (I am sure there are many more):

1.Even before the birth of a child, the father arranges the home so that the child will have a comfortable, nurturing environment in which to grow up. In the same way, you have arranged for a society of devotees to care for us, even before we are old enough to speak intelligibly about spiritual subject matters. So many wonderful Vaiṣṇavas, who have accompanied you as your assistants, care for us in your society with real love and affection.

2.Upon the birth of the child, the father arranges for an appropriate name to be given to him, a name that gives him his identity and at the same time connects him to his predecessors. In the same way, you have named us. This name gives us the identity of a servant of a servant of a servant of Kṛṣṇa, and connects us to our wonderful disciplic family, which begins with the Supreme Lord Himself and His associates in Goloka.

3.The father also arranges for the maintenance and protection of his children. You have arranged to give us the most wonderful food (*prasādam*) and are always protecting us with your guidance and with the guidance of your faithful followers. Even physically, your loving family cares for us and protects us.

4.To secure a bright future for the child, the father arranges for him to be educated in relevant knowledge. You have personally written so many books, given so many classes, and shown us your personal example in order to educate us, not only in spiritual subjects but also in how to deal with this world. You have also given us many expert teachers to help us imbibe this knowledge.

5.The father trains the child and mentors him so that the child can take up the father’s occupation. You have arranged for our training so that we can take up the activity that will most please you, the spreading of love of Godhead.

6.Then the father arranges for the child to have a specific occupation in a specific locale. You have given us our services according to our psychophysical nature, so that we will be most effective in helping the conditioned souls regain their lost relation with Kṛṣṇa.

7.Even after the child takes on a specific occupation, the father is always there to guide him. I find that continually I need to be praying to you for guidance. Whenever I have to make a decision, I ask myself, “What would Śrīla Prabhupāda want me to do in this circumstance?”

8.A material father no longer protects the child after he has grown up, but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to protect us in every way.

9.You continue to act as the intermediary between us and Kṛṣṇa. Because of your purity and transparency, our experience of Kṛṣṇa is direct.

10.More than an ordinary father, you are with us at the time of death, and even beyond. You never neglect us. Even if we have to take birth again in this material realm, you appear and guide us on the path, life after life.

11.Then, when we finally reach the ultimate abode of the Lord, Goloka, you are there eternally to engage us in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Their associates. Simultaneously, you eternally engage us in the service of the Pañca-tattva and their associates.

How can we ever repay your love for us? The love that you have for us is boundless and causeless. That love can never be repaid. I pray that I will always be engaged in repaying that love, wherever it may take me, as long as I always have your service and association.

Your eternal son-servant,  
Bīr Krishna Dās Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

True disciples, no matter how advanced, always sees themselves as perpetual students of their guru. Even Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Lord Himself in the role of a devotee, vividly demonstrated how He was never beyond the guidance and jurisdiction of His spiritual master. Humbly aspiring to follow the path set by Śrī Gaura, I seek to praise your constant necessity for my life and beg for your increased presence. In tribute to our eternal connection and active relationship, allow me to walk alongside you in the morning and submit a preaching analysis for your judgment.

There is a toxic mentality raging in the wealthiest nations of the world, the so-called “established market democracies,” and I wonder what you would say about it.

In the first world, besides the monomaniacal focus on economic affairs, today’s dedicated servants of the senses share another virulent affliction that impedes our preaching.

Trendy it is for global humankind to malign whatever seems religious in any traditional sense. Even in India now, young professionals are starting to catch this Western bug: “Excuse me, but I’m into spirituality—not religion.” That means toss out anything smacking of authoritativeness, discipline, and sense control. The progressive notion is that real spirituality doesn’t burden the contemporary truth-seeker with rules, regulations, teachers, scriptures, temples, and so forth. The divine just amazingly springs spontaneously from anyone’s bosom, every heart, in a way unique to each individual.

*Paramparās* and guru-disciple relationships are the chains of autocracy. Especially scriptural dependency, they tell us, imprisons our mind with archaic dogmas. “Why should genuine spirituality entomb itself in certain texts, practices, and teachers? What truly informed and world-experienced person would accept such a warped vision? Only a fundamentalist, a creationist, a terrorist!”

Global humankind adamantly declares it has moved on. “Watch us soar unabashedly in the free skies of random self-enlightenment and self-justification.” The sad fact: what is deemed as true divinity, religion, and now yoga has become whatever to whomever.

Among today’s do-it-yourself, free-style sages, even their disdain for authoritative scripture does not match their utter abhorrence of religious institutions. “Why oh why would you want to belong to one of those! Are you in the Dark Ages?” The logic goes: religions are regressive and restrictive, and therefore religious institutions are even worse. Only the weak and misguided would ever participate enthusiastically in organized religion.

As your disciple surveying the current wasteland known as the Information Age, I pause to reflect on the unavoidable, inarguable spiritual reality. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you struggled so intensely, at such an advanced age, to give us *śāstra, paramparā, sādhana,* and an international institution. Oh, how easy it would be to bow to the storms of the moment and back away from these Bhaktivedanta gems. My question: how to acknowledge, in a timely way, the particular diseased mentality of the day while offering the timeless cure? Relevancy in our outreach, yes, but certainly in the process we do not want to risk any of our precious inheritance.

In your glorification of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you closed triumphantly:

The oldest of all  
But in new dress  
Miracle done  
Your Divine Grace.

My inadequate attempt to glorify you can end with only a pitiful, desperate cry: Śrīla Prabhupāda, I need you now more than ever before.

Trying to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmrita Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

“When Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya what is considered to be the most painful existence, Rāmānanda Rāya replied that separation from a pure devotee constitutes the most painful existence.” (*Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* Conclusion)

Events that have taken place over the past year have helped me to more clearly realize the words of Rāmānanda Rāya. An operation and weakened nervous system have prevented me from traveling and visiting your disciples and grand-disciples. Bereft of their association, I have suffered great pain.

But you have taught us that that pain is not without purpose. It brings us closer to those we are separated from and increases our attachment to them. We grow to appreciate the unique qualities of each and every devotee. That appreciation in turn brings us closer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I have no idea what is in store for me over the next year. I assume you would like me to continue to travel and preach. In that case, I need to fully regain my health and strength.

Then again, maybe you have some other service for me to perform. Either way, my desire is to surrender to your plan.

My only prayer is to become your surrendered servant and execute your will in this lifetime and in all future lifetimes.

Your servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Trees**

Śrīla Prabhupāda had to struggle very hard to build the Juhu temple. The first struggle was to get the land, the next struggle was to get the permission to build the temple, and the last struggle was to actually build the temple and install the Deities. And we had worked very hard to get the permission, but Indian permits often include many conditions. So although they give permission, the permission is not actually valid until one fulfills the conditions.

One of the conditions was that we had to have an internal access road of a certain width according to the length of the road. And it just so happened that right where the access road was supposed to go, there were some palm trees. Once, when Śrīla Prabhupāda came to Juhu after being away for some time, he noticed that one or two of the trees had been cut down, and he was very concerned—I would even say upset. He asked, “Why did you cut down the trees?” We thought we had the perfect answer: We had to build the temple, and to get the permission for the temple we had to cut down the trees in the way of the road. Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “No, you go to the municipality and tell them it’s against our religion to cut down trees.” So we had no choice, and we did it. And of course the municipal officers objected.

But one thing that really struck me about how concerned Śrīla Prabhupāda was for the trees was that the next time he came back to Juhu after a world tour, practically the first thing he said was “I was in Tehran, and I have seen they have trees in the middle of the streets. So we can also have trees in the middle of our street.” In other words, it was not so strange. So on Śrīla Prabhupāda’s order, we held our ground with the municipality, and in the end they gave us permission to keep the last tree that we had thought was blocking the road. And even today you can see that tree, which Śrīla Prabhupāda protected, still standing there with asphalt all around it.

So I am thinking that if Śrīla Prabhupāda has so much concern and care and, I would even say, love for a tree, then what to speak of us—how much care and love he must have for us. And it’s just because of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s care and mercy and protection that we are able to stand in Kṛṣṇa consciousness—no matter what else is around us.

All glory to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Girirāj Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet, which are giving shelter to unlimited conditioned souls.

You have no need for our eulogy or service. You are self-sufficient. It is only because of your causeless mercy that you give us the opportunity to glorify you for our purification.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa and inseparable from the Supreme Lord. Thoughts of your divine pastimes and instructions remind us of the Supreme Lord—which is the real goal of human life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have offered suffering humanity a way to live peacefully by practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the short span of twelve years you established Kṛṣṇa conscious centers around the world, published countless books, and converted thousands of *mlecchas* into devotees of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You had firm faith in the instructions of your spiritual master, and hence you became the instrument in realizing Lord Caitanya’s prediction: “In as many towns and villages as there are on the surface of the earth, My holy name will be preached.”

It is because of your purity and spiritual potency that new temples are being built, old ones are being maintained, books are being distributed, and more and more people are taking to the chanting of the *mahā-mantra*. Your glories are unlimited. Hearing and reading about your transcendental pastimes purify us and gives us the opportunity to further associate with you.

As you took the trouble to go to remote corners of the world to preach, your followers are continuing to do the same. Recently I was able to visit Pakistan to do a tiny service in this area. We have just opened a center in Tanzania—a Muslim country that would not even register our society for over ten years.

You emphasized the importance of preaching in your books, letters, *darśanas,* etc. By your personal example you demonstrated how one can preach till the end of one’s life. You explained that purity is the force which will increase our ecstasy to preach. Purity can be obtained only by strictly following your instructions—no shortcuts. Lord Caitanya wanted Kṛṣṇa consciousness to be distributed to everyone, without restriction.

You explained:

- Preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the greatest missionary activity.
- Preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the best welfare activity.
- The Lord is pleased by one who preaches.
- Preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is real mercy.
- Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement is meant for making people happy.
- Distribution of transcendental literature benefits human society more than any other welfare activity.
- Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the remedy that can bring world peace.
- Spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness renders eternal benefit to human society.

On this auspicious day, this insignificant servant of yours would like to beg you for your causeless mercy so that I may be able to help expand your *saṅkīrtana* movement. I also pray that I may always remain true to your transcendental instructions and be eternally engaged in your service.

Your insignificant servant,  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and prostrated obeisances in the *cintāmaṇi* dust of your lotus feet.

I continue to discuss the twenty-six qualities of a pure Vaiṣṇava and try to describe in my inadequate words, and to the degree that my inability allows me, how they pertain to you.

The next quality is *mṛdu:* mild, gentle or soft-hearted. Many of the twenty-six qualities are similar, and many are interdependent. Certainly your mildness was a result of your magnanimity and natural peacefulness, coming from being fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa. Because you demonstrated so much mercy, even though we deserved to be chastised, your main concern was to make the devotees feel enthusiastic to continue in their service, and you would smile and encourage us.

Sometimes Kṛṣṇa is described as *mṛdu-smita,* possessing a soft smile. Certainly your smile was a bath of relief. Just like Kṛṣṇa, a Vaiṣṇava can be like a thunderbolt or soft like a rose. You demonstrated both, as necessary, but soft-heartedness is the natural condition of a devotee. You comment in your *Bhagavad-gītā* about how Arjuna’s compassion, although improperly exhibited on the battlefield, is an innate quality of a devotee. Your soft-heartedness has kept most of us in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Especially in my case, with so much false ego, I could not withstand too much correction, but rather looked for praise.

The soothing effect of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is compared to the soft moonlight. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 13.5, you comment:

Similarly, the present verse also describes that by the illumination of all the moons, brightened by the reflection of the Kṛṣṇa sun, or by the grace of all the devotees of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the entire world will be illuminated, despite the darkness of Kali-yuga. Only the devotees of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu can dissipate the darkness of Kali-yuga, the ignorance of the population of this age. No one else can do so. We therefore wish that all the devotees of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement may reflect the supreme sun and thus dissipate the darkness of the entire world.

Thus, my humble prayer in this offering is that you give me the strength, intelligence, and ability to reflect one small beam of your divine *mṛdu* effulgence to assist you in soothing the chronic condition imposed by material existence and Kali-yuga.

Your most unworthy servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

At this time every year, we gather our thoughts and our communities to honor our spiritual leader, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Because our impurity blocks our vision of Kṛṣṇa in the heart, Kṛṣṇa sends his pure devotee as the external, visible form of the Supersoul, to guide us on the path back to Godhead.

As we mature and advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, our appreciation for, and devotion to, Śrīla Prabhupāda naturally grow in proportion. Ultimately it is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s wish that we mature to the point of being able, as he was, to simultaneously preserve and adapt the great tradition that he so wisely and faithfully revealed to us. We must preserve Kṛṣṇa consciousness as it is. Otherwise, we betray the spiritual science. Yet we must present it in a relevant way, so that it lives and breathes as humanity’s *yuga-dharma*.

Only through Śrīla Prabhupāda’s great mercy will we successfully grasp and implement his deep will to his satisfaction. On this day especially, let us pray for that specific mercy.

His servant,  
Hridayānanda Dās Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear spiritual father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

Forty years ago, in 1966, you registered ISKCON in New York and formally started the revolution that is The International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Now we remember you and want to insure that all the tireless efforts and sacrifices you made to start and spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will continue on to the end of the Golden Age of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, ten thousand years from now, if not longer. The whole world is deeply indebted to Your Divine Grace, and one day they will realize it. You have made the greatest sacrifice in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world in twelve years.

Your Divine Grace expressed that the way we can show our love for you is to cooperate to serve Your Divine Grace. That is my humble desire — to do whatever I can to please Your Divine Grace, and to that end we all try to cooperate with each other in your devotional service.

In our desire to develop Māyāpur Dhāma to fulfill your instructions, we are facing obstacles. We need your mercy to overcome these obstacles so that your vision for Māyāpur can soon be manifested for the world to see and be blessed.

By Your Divine Grace’s mercy, the congregational development is expanding around the world, and more and more devotees are practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness from their homes. They are also helping the temples in many ways and preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness from their homes as well. We look forward that by Your Divine Grace’s mercy the whole world will soon be filled with devotees practicing *bhakti-yoga* from their homes by following your instructions and vision. Their enthusiasm is a source of inspiration. Please shower your blessings upon them.

This year I am spending more time in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, Bangladesh, and East India to fulfill the instructions you gave me for these areas. I need your causeless mercy to make progress and achieve success in all you have asked me to achieve in this lifetime. Bless me that I can do more than what I was asked to achieve.

Only by Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s grace can one survive the strong attacks of the illusory energy of the Lord. That is why I am ever dependent on your mercy to always remain securely and safely fixed at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

I hope this finds you in good health.

Your servant,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Although I daily try to describe your glories whenever I speak or write, every year when I try to write a formal offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā, my mind becomes blank. One might say it is stunned by the unlimited nature of what you have given to me and to the universe, and by my limited ability and the space available. Already volumes of books have been published, and many more are in various stages of production, so what can I offer? I wish that I could sincerely and unconditionally offer my life, body, mind, and soul, but still my false ego is dragging me down. So many great personalities are glorifying you within your ISKCON movement, and there are also many others outside — scholars and the like — who cannot help but appreciate your great qualities and your contribution to society.

And the *Vedas* are also glorifying you. *Guru-tattva* is inconceivable and can be experienced through unmotivated service, not through mundane scholarship or anything like that.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 1.44) Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja describes the essential truth of the spiritual master, and you purports make it clear:

*yadyapi āmāra guru—caitanyera dāsa*  
*tathāpi jāniye āmi tāṅhāra prakāśa*

**Although I know that my spiritual master is a servitor of Śrī Caitanya, I know Him also as a plenary manifestation of the Lord.**

Every living entity is essentially a servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and the spiritual master is also His servant. Still, the spiritual master is a direct manifestation of the Lord. With this conviction, a disciple can advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa because he is a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa.

Lord Nityānanda, who is Balarāma Himself, the first direct manifestation or expansion of Kṛṣṇa, is the original spiritual master. He helps Lord Kṛṣṇa in His pastimes, and He is a servant of the Lord.

Every living entity is eternally a servant of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya; therefore the spiritual master cannot be other than a servant of Lord Caitanya. The spiritual master’s eternal occupation is to expand the service of the Lord by training disciples in a service attitude. A spiritual master never poses as the Supreme Lord Himself; he is considered a representative of the Lord. The revealed scriptures prohibit one’s pretending to be God, but a bona fide spiritual master is a most faithful and confidential servant of the Lord and therefore deserves as much respect as Kṛṣṇa.

As I read this passage, the following line stood out: “The spiritual master’s eternal occupation is to expand the service of the Lord by training disciples in a service attitude.”

Of course, you have done that, and through your books and disciples you are still doing it. So many devotees are infected with your service attitude and are pushing on your movement in all corners of the world. I always hanker for the association of your inspired servants and pray that I might also become infected.

*tato duḥsaṅgam utsṛjya satsu sajjeta buddhi-mān*  
*santa evāsya chindanti mano-vyāsaṅgam uktibhiḥ*

**An intelligent person should therefore reject all bad association and instead take up the association of saintly devotees, whose words cut off the excessive attachment of one’s mind. (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.26.27)**

Your servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. May your glories spread throughout all the universes, both spiritual and material.

In order to be eternally attached to serving you, we have to realize that Kṛṣṇa’s mercy on us can only come through you, and that your every activity, word, and thought is that mercy.

Some of the so-called “established religions” of the world have a principle called *ex cathedra,* on the basis of which the representative of God is to be taken as such only when he speaks from the pulpit (*cathedra*), repeating the words of God. In any other situation and when speaking anything else, he is to be taken as an ordinary mortal, and his words or activities have to be considered ordinary, i.e., material.

This insidious tendency may be trying to infiltrate your movement.

The idea of your so-called “material nature” is a trick of Yogamāyā to delude and turn away the insincere, all the while making them feel comfortable in their offensive conviction that they know which of your teachings and exemplary activities to accept and which to reject.

This misguided tendency mainly manifests in two aberrations: the first one is accepting what could be called “a philosophized Śrīla Prabhupāda,” an idea that prompts one to judge what is “spiritual” and what is “material” in your life and teachings — in other words, what is acceptable and what is not; the second aberration is the artificial opposite of the first one, namely, the blind generalization in which one levels out all your teachings and activities and indiscriminately accepts all your words, actions, and aspects of your life as equally significant for everyone at all times.

The first aberration leads to criticism of the spiritual master, pride, over-intelligence, drying up, and philosophical deviations.

The second one leads to sentimentalism, Hinduism, fanaticism, material expectations, and, eventually, frustration and loss of faith in the spiritual master.

The real protection from the above-mentioned dangers is given by Kṛṣṇa in the *śāstra:*

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27)**

Implicit faith in the spiritual master implies the discernment that will enable us to see all your words and deeds on the transcendental platform, all the while being able to recognize how we should please you, that is, which instructions, priorities, and practical directions we should take up at what time and in what circumstance in order to become your Kṛṣṇa conscious, eternal disciples.

When Kṛṣṇa was present on earth, Yogamāyā made sure that the unqualified could not ascertain the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s real identity or understand His *janma* and *karma.* Yogamāyā induced such persons to attribute to the Lord’s identity, birth, and *līlā* a material or semidivine nature, while in reality they are all transcendental.

The following verse indicates that when an elevated devotee like yourself descends, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the material nature also plays no role:

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ vibho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni svāntaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*

**“My Lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage.”**

The *janma* and *karma* of the spiritual master are but another example of the Lord’s *acintya-śakti* working.

Some may argue that the above is valid for the *principle* of the spiritual master, but his body, particular culture, tastes, etc., are external and therefore material.

In a letter you sent to Mālatī Devī Dāsī in 1968, you wrote:

In the absolute world there is no distinction as me, or he, and I. Krishna and His representative is the same. Just like Krishna can be present simultaneously in millions of places. Similarly, the Spiritual Master also can be present wherever the disciple wants. A Spiritual Master is the principle, not the body. Just like a television can be seen in thousands of places by the principle of relay monitoring.

But then why do the *śāstras* tell us that as long as the spiritual master is present we should serve his *vapu,* his body? Is there a contradiction?

Not at all.

As long as we are able to understand that the particular *vapu* that the spiritual master has taken (with all its unique characteristics) is significant because of its being the instrument through which he can show his mercy to us by accepting our devotion, our service to his *vapu* is transcendental, recognized by Kṛṣṇa.

The moment we *confine* the personality of the spiritual master to a material bodily concept based on nationality, caste, culture, etc., independent of the *principle,* then we are no longer serving the spiritual master but rather our offensive mentality.

You confirmed that even the diseased condition of your body during the last days was a means to increase your disciples’ attachment to you by engaging them in heartrending service.

The same goes for all other services to you.

I recall serving lunch to you once in Māyāpur. Pālikā Devī Dāsī was the cook, and she had advised me to serve with particular care, among other preparations, some small *pakorās, because you liked them very much*.

I was so attached and happy to be able to serve you something you particularly liked!

Thank you for having a predilection for *kachaurīs* and *pakorās,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We should not think that this *principle* of the spiritual master is an impersonal one, that the differences are only due to the bodies, and that we can avail ourselves of his association from “any-body,” at our discretion.

Even though you can reach us in any condition and through anybody, you are a specific eternal associate of the Personality of Godhead, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That specificity is perceived both in the service rendered to you while you were present in your *vapu* form, as well as in the liberated stage.

When asked if once liberated we would recognize and rejoin you to serve you, the answer you gave was unmistakably affirmative.

Those who are blessed with the realization of your *specific* oneness with and difference from the *principle* of the original spiritual master, the Personality of Godhead, will find you and serve you eternally in the spiritual world.

Please bless us that we may never fall, due to envy, into the pitfall of misunderstanding your true position.

Please protect the preachers and leaders of your movement from the unadvised, unlawful desire to merge with and become the *principle* of the spiritual master, and bless us with the attachment to serve the most wonderful manifestation of that principle — you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

With the invaluable contribution of your servants at the Mediterranean BBT, we have undertaken a project to print a book similar to *Darśana* dedicated to Your Divine Grace, with the aim of broadcasting the unparalleled sweetness and beauty of your appearance among us, thereby increasing the feelings of separation of your loving devotees.

Hoping that some day you’ll be pleased with me and will take me back to your lotus feet, I remain

Your property and servant,  
Madhusevita Dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please let me offer you my humble obeisances again and again.

You are the beacon of life for this distraught Age of Kali.
