Like Prahlāda Mahārāja, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were not interested in personal liberation; rather, you were resilient in your determination to deliver ordinary people by freeing them from illusion — unhappiness — and giving them pure devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, the highest happiness.

To those materially diseased San Francisco hippies of the 1967 era, you proclaimed, “I have brought only one medicine. Please take it.” Whereupon you picked up a pair of *karatālas* and began *kīrtana.*

To your spiritually blinded young disciples who lamented when very few guests showed up at our first nicely arranged engagement, you said, “Oh? You did not see? Lord Brahmā was there. And Nārada Muni.” (No, alas, we did not see.)

About a sweetly innocent young disciple who had pleased you by her question, you said, “Just see how this young girl wants Kṛṣṇa!” (And to her you said, “If you want, Kṛṣṇa will kiss you!”)

You said nothing one cool foggy Ratha-yātrā morning in London, circa ’72, when you were offered your *āsana* on the royal *ratha* cart. Instead, you raised your arms and began to dance, which brought out the sun, and you then removed your heavy black woolen coat and placed it on my outstretched arms, dancing all the while. You never sat on the *ratha* that day, dancing the entire procession. When we arrived at Trafalgar Square in ecstasy, no one saw that the wooden structure leading to the stage was shaky. With your coat shifted to one arm, I offered you the other, and you accepted by placing your lotus hand on my forearm, whereupon we walked together to the raised platform. It was over in a moment of utter perfection.

My arms are still reaching out to receive your coat, waiting for your gentle touch to guide me back home, back to Godhead, the ultimate platform. I lament my inability to rise above the selfish bodily concept of life.

With folded palms, falling flat at your lotus feet, I beg you to continue to accept me, your fallen disciple, for yet another year in your service,  
Mālatī Dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

My offering this year is not about airing a grievance. It’s about apprising you of the state of play. It’s directed not exactly to you as we knew you, but as you perceive us today. Written from the perspective of a retiree living in sort of self-imposed exile, these words attempt to highlight some of your profundities, and the wisdom needed to manage a global society.

In a letter to Kīrtanānanda Swami dated Oct 18, 1973, you wrote: “They cannot come into agreement with varieties, but if we keep Kṛṣṇa in the center, then there will be agreement in varieties. This is called unity in diversity.” In this letter you also said, “If we fight on account of diversity, then it is simply the material platform. Please try to maintain the philosophy of unity in diversity.” And, in the same letter, you wrote, “Be very careful to maintain unity in diversity.” You sent copies of the above letter to Karandhara Dāsa and Mohanānanda Dāsa.

This may sound like harping on a three-word phrase, but it was Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja who said, “Essential truth spoken concisely is true eloquence.” I know this offering is far from eloquent and should probably be trashed. But duty calls.

Finding and encouraging diversity within unity is a key challenge in administering a worldwide entity. In your letter to Kīrtanānanda Swami, you also mentioned the Aesop fable about a group of sticks being unbreakable when bundled together but fragile when separate. This is a brilliant argument for unity.

In the letter, you were disturbed about disagreements and altercations taking place within ISKCON. In other letters and conversations you talked about leaders discussing things “threadbare” (anyone reading this can look up that word in the VedaBase, but remember that the VedaBase is not all-inclusive), intimating that those holding opposing viewpoints could reach a higher understanding through sincere discussion.

The inevitable institutional dichotomy of conservatives versus liberals can be looked upon as creative tension. Each person may have to see the other’s point of view. As former US Secretary of Defense Robert S. McNamara once remarked, we have to learn how to “empathize with the enemy.” It’s not that McNamara should be considered one of our authorities, or that ISKCON’s conservatives and liberals are enemies, but that people like McNamara have *something* to offer.

We learn about music, computers, book production, motor vehicles, temple construction, study habits, health, and hospitality from many nondevotional gurus, just as we (following the lead of the wandering *brāhmaṇa* in the Eleventh Canto) can learn from the earth, air, sky, water, fire, moon, sun, pigeon, and python.

Therefore we sometimes learn from your “worldly side.” In addition to being the spiritual impetus behind ISKCON, you were an international CEO, making difficult and complex legal and financial decisions relating to many different world cultures. And you *loved* each of us with all your heart. Even that love can be regarded as a form of “psychological” perfection.

But management and leadership were among your fortes. We could say that these two qualities stemmed directly from your spiritual purity, but we can also regard them as separate skills. You were fond of saying that you started out with only forty rupees and just a few years later were controlling millions of dollars. Is that not entrepreneurship par excellence?

ISKCON faces great challenges. For ISKCON to be everything you wanted it to be is no easy task. It’s a challenge that takes lots of tolerance, ingenuity, creativity, innovation, original and lateral thinking, mind twisting, and the courage to be different if and when required.

If management aphorisms are to be meditated upon, then the “diversity in unity” principle has got to be high on the list.

Some nation-states — like the US (please pardon the ethnocentricity) — have achieved this to a degree. An example is the US federal government versus the US state governments. These two divisions of one government generally work together well, even though they sometimes have radically different agendas. On the other hand, Singapore, though very autocratic, is almost crime-free, and Bhutan, though its government is authoritarian, is considered a well-managed country. But these are relatively small regions. Countries with larger populations generally don’t fit into the well-managed category.

ISKCON’s alternative dispute resolution groups (ADR) are still finding out about managing difference (diversity writ large). It’s said that during the 2005–2006 lunar year, the ADR and its minions adjudicated hundreds of cases worldwide. The ADR’s key personnel are learners.

And management, like many things, involves a lot of learning. Today you can get a post-graduate degree in management. In management courses, training and assessment are key ingredients. Some management specialists say that success lies not in bettering competitors but in making them irrelevant.

If ISKCON is to be an educational institution, eternal learning and humility are by-words.

Of course, this is very theoretical, and where the rubber hits the road, things aren’t easy. But who says attaining the spiritual world is a facile ambition?

Mukunda Goswami

## Niranjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

While reading the *Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* I came across an example and two quotes befitting the mood for which I’m aspiring while writing this offering to you.

In total humility, Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī expresses his unworthiness for the topmost instructions he received from Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu as follows:

By Your grace I have appreciated the conclusions which You have taught me, but I am so low that I cannot even touch a drop of the ocean of Your instructions. Thus if You want me, who am nothing but a lame man, to dance, then please give me Your benediction by placing Your feet on my head.

In your commentary on the above quote, you have written:

Thus Sanātana Gosvāmī prayed for the Lord’s confirmation that His teachings would actually evolve in his heart by His grace. Otherwise Sanātana knew that there was no possibility of his being able to describe the Lord’s teachings. The purport of this is that the *ācāryas* (spiritual masters) are authorized by higher authorities. Instruction alone cannot make one an expert. Unless one is blessed by the spiritual master, or the *ācārya,* such teachings cannot become fully manifest. Therefore one should seek the mercy of the spiritual master so that the instructions of the spiritual master can develop within oneself. After receiving the prayers of Sanātana Gosvāmī, Lord Caitanya placed His feet on the head of Sanātana and gave him His benedictions so that all His instructions would develop fully.

There is no doubt in my mind that you want me to “dance.” You want everyone to dance, and the wealth of instructions you are giving in your books, lectures, letters, and conversations, as well as those you are revealing in and through the hearts of your sincere followers, is tangible proof that you want us to distribute that which you are purely giving to us.

Within your teachings are all the conclusions of the entire *paramparā,* beginning from Lord Kṛṣṇa and extending down through Lord Brahmā, Nārada Muni, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Madhva, Mādhavendra Purī, Īśvara Purī, Lord Caitanya, ŚrīRūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī, Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa, Jagannātha Dāsa Bābājī, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Śrīla Gaura Kiśora Dāsa Bābājī, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. All the conclusions given by them are descending through you.

You have been empowered in the same way that Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī was. As Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī describes:

*śrī-rūpa-hṛdaye prabhu śakti sañcārilā*  
*sarva-tattva-nirūpaṇe ‘pravīṇa’ karilā*

**“By entering the heart of Rūpa Gosvāmī, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu empowered him to ascertain properly the conclusions of all truths. He made him an experienced devotee whose decisions correctly agreed with the verdicts of the disciplic succession. Thus ŚrīRūpa Gosvāmī was personally empowered by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **19.117)**

We can understand that you were empowered within your heart in the same way. When Tamāl Krishna Goswami expressed his appreciation to you for your books, you began to cry and said, “I am only child. But I have tried to please my guru mahārāja. I have not written. It is Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa and my guru mahārāja.”

And in your concluding words to your translation and commentary for *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* you expressed this deep realization as follows:

I think that His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words. As it is said in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, tene brahma hṛdā ya ādi-kavaye*. Spiritual inspiration comes from within the heart, wherein the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in His Paramātmā feature, is always sitting with all His devotees and associates. It is to be admitted that whatever translation work I have done is through the inspiration of my spiritual master, because personally I am most insignificant and incompetent to do this materially impossible work. I do not think myself a very learned scholar, but I have full faith in the service of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. If there is any credit to my activities of translating, it is all due to His Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to become fully qualified to represent you. But not only am I spiritually lame, I am spiritually deaf, dumb, and blind too! Without your mercy, I am destined to remain spiritually bankrupt.

But if there is anything of worth I can claim as my own, it is this desire that your teachings will continue to evolve within my heart. I do not want to simply memorize your instructions. I want, and pray, that you will expand them within my heart as you guide me through the entire process.

My heart belongs to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I therefore beg you not only to place your lotus feet on my head but to firmly plant those two lotus feet within the core of my heart.

On this most auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this material world, I want to reconfirm again and again that you are the lord of my heart. If you want me to dance, then, as you have said in your prayer to Lord Kṛṣṇa, “make me dance, make me dance, O lord, make me dance as you like.”

Your eternally indebted servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear master,

While fulfilling my duties and holding many responsibilities for your satisfaction, and offering myself onto your lotus feet, I realize how, despite so many obstacles and apparently unsolvable problems, the remembrance of your instructions is like an invigorating draft of fresh air replenishing my strength to continue.

Your personal examples of determination in overcoming great challenges for spreading Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message are never-ending sources of inspiration for me and all other members of ISKCON.

You gave us practical examples of how important it is to protect our *siddhānta,* the philosophical conclusion of the Brahma-Mādhva-Gauḍīya Sampradāya. On the other hand, you taught us to use proper discretion, intelligence, and common sense in adapting the message according to time, place, and circumstance. By doing so, ISKCON will remain the vanguard, the revolutionary movement Your Divine Grace created.

I beg for your eternal mercy and protection so that I may continue my humble attempt to execute your plan, the divine mission of spreading Śrī Caitanyadeva’s message throughout human society.

Your eternal servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Long ago in New York City, a place for me that’s not so pretty,  
I was dazed and alone, no direction home,  
Lost in the dark, sleeping in the park,  
Wandering the streets, with no one to meet,  
Aimless and sad, I might have gone mad.  
Then my ears heard a loud *kīrtan* in the crowd.  
With *kartāls* and drums were blissful, dancing monks.  
One gave me a book and said, “Just have a look!  
Come to our program.” I said, “Can’t. Going to London.”  
He said, “Visit us there. You’ll be welcome for sure.”  
So I boarded the plane. In a few days I came  
To ISKCON, Bury Place, with a hesitant face.  
But the door opened wide — “Do please come inside.  
Downstairs for *prasād*. No need to be sad.  
Now it’s *saṅkīrtan* time. To enlighten the blind  
We’ll chant on the street to the *mṛdaṅga* beat.  
And when we come back Prabhupāda will speak.  
You can learn about God from the purest of all.”  
When I heard Prabhupāda’s song I knew I belonged  
At his soft lotus feet, so pure and so sweet.  
“What a great invitation to reject degradation.  
Let me jump on this train back to Godhead again,  
And remain forever with the *saṅkīrtan* beggars.  
Then by Prabhupāda’s grace I’ll be saved from disgrace,  
Engaged by our master, serving Kṛṣṇa ever after.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your worthless but eternally grateful servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rādhānātha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances.

Pleasing you is the principle that keeps thousands of devotees unified all over the world. Our aspiration to please you is the sustenance of our spiritual lives, the source of our empowerment, and our sacred connection to Lord Kṛṣṇa. I pray that the determination to please you may forever be my life and soul.

Last summer you blessed me with the undeserved fortune of rendering service to His Holiness Bhakti Tīrtha Mahārāja (your beloved Ghanaśyāma) during his last weeks with us. Witnessing his absolute dedication to your mission deeply affected my heart. In life or death he wanted nothing but to please you. The process of dying has the effect of cutting away all externals and revealing the core of a man’s heart. In those last weeks, I observed Bhakti Tīrtha Mahārāja’s one-pointed faithfulness to you. Whatever he did, even his preparation for going back to Godhead, was only to please you. His steadfast chanting of at least sixteen rounds each day in such difficult circumstances was only to honor his sacred promise to you. His amazing will to extend himself in the service of the devotees was simply impelled by his desire to be an instrument of your mercy. The words he spoke while laying on his deathbed, while chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy names, will forever echo in my heart:

Amazing, how fortunate we are! We are tasting the nectar of the holy name, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, and the association of his devotees. I would not change my place for anything in the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for blessing him and all who know him with your causeless mercy.

Earlier this year another dedicated soul, Stoka Kṛṣṇa Prabhu, was also taken from us. He was only a boy in his early thirties. When he was unexpectedly diagnosed with incurable, swift-acting cancer, his wife was pregnant with his first child. As he lay paralyzed from the waist down and emaciated with pain, he became the father of a beautiful daughter. Everything was soon to be stripped away from him. Still he always smiled in joy, grateful for whatever came into his life. His words still ring in my heart:

Why should I be disturbed by dying at such a young age? I am grateful.

Why was I allowed to associate with devotees for even a moment? I did not deserve it.

Why was I ever blessed to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission for even a minute? I am totally unqualified.

Why was I allowed to chant Kṛṣṇa’s names even once? I am such an offender. Yet still in this lifetime I was given all these blessings.

Why was I given the supreme benediction of being the servant of the servant of the devotees? I am so fallen, I did not deserve such a blessing.

Then with sincere tears in his eyes he poured out his heart:

I feel nothing but gratitude and joy that in this short lifetime I received so much of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s causeless mercy.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your lifetime of compassion. Thank you for your books, lectures, and instructions, which are the basis of our lives. And thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for teaching us through the hearts of those who love you.

Your servant,  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“Don’t keep a guru as a fashion,”Śrīla Prabhupāda would often say. This is especially true in Western countries, where some people think it is trendy to show off their guru to others. Unfortunately, their personal lives don’t always reflect a serious attitude towards genuine spiritual advancement.

In India a lot of gurus present themselves through media such as television, newspapers, billboards, and posters. In this way they have gained widespread popularity. Many are advertised as the latest “television guru avatar.” But who is the true guru?

In the *Purāṇas* it is said:

*guravo bahavaḥ santi śiṣya-vittāpahārakāḥ*  
*durlabho sad-gurur devi śiṣya-santāpa-hārakaḥ*

**“O Devī, there are many gurus who are expert in plundering the wealth of their disciples, but a bona fide guru who can remove the miseries of his disciples is very difficult to find.”**

To actually remove material miseries is not so easy. Modern-day society is becoming more and more superficial and doesn’t think deeply about these matters. Of course, since time immemorial people have tried to alleviate material misery and distress, but who has actually achieved it? People may think, “Oh, I have done it,” but have they? The harsh reality is that they are in illusion, only thinking that they have. In this world, while misery may decrease in one area, it increases in another. No one can stand up and say, “I am happy all the time.”

We are so fortunate that Śrīla Prabhupāda forced us to think seriously about these things. “Kṛṣṇa consciousness is reality,” he said, “and this temporary material nature is phantasmagoria.”“You are all eternal spirit soul, part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa, and this material world is *māyā,* that which is not.” When Śrīla Prabhupāda spoke in this way he had a penetrating effect on the hearts of all those who listened. This was because of his immense spiritual potency. He was like a hot knife cutting through the rancid butter of illusion.

This world is definitely a much better place because of Śrīla Prabhupāda, especially for those who appreciate him. And for those who don’t, any contact with him — through his books, his devotees, or any other medium — gives them eternal benefit. We beg for his mercy so that he may bless us to go back home, back to Godhead. We are grateful that he kindly gave us the process of pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

He traveled through the Kali night,  
Giving mercy from above.  
Although he is now out of sight,  
We always feel his love.  
For who but him in this fleeting world  
Of squalor, disease, and death  
Could take us like a flag unfurled  
To cross this ocean’s breadth?  
“There is life beyond this suffering place,”  
He constantly would say.  
“Can’t you see the illusory face  
Of the world so dark and gray?  
“Come with me and see our Lord,  
Who’s waiting with a smile.  
He’ll welcome us with loving words,  
Even if we take awhile.”  
O Prabhupāda, take us with you,  
For you always light the way.  
Your form shines bright with golden hue,  
Turning Kali’s night to day.

Your insignificant servant,  
Ramāi Swami

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dearly beloved spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and prostrated obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories unto to Your Divine Grace!

On this anniversary of your appearance in this world, your loving disciples and hundreds of thousands of followers from all around the world are singularly focused upon the same object, and the same activity: glorification of Your Divine Grace! A crescendo of voices is raised, minds are acutely focused, hearts are melting in exaltation of your qualities and mercy — what a glorious symphony!

A most joyous expression of unity is demonstrated in this way, but our service to you throughout the rest of the year discloses unity among diverse personalities and divergent wills, and thus glorification of you often comes in other wonderful ways as well.

I would like to share with you a recent experience I had that made a very significant impact upon my sense of your imminent shelter, even in your physical absence.

While riding in a car to attend a Sunday program in a far-away place, we got caught in a huge traffic jam, with long strings of vehicles at a total standstill heading in both directions, for no apparent reason. Very puzzled, we patiently waited for the stalled traffic to move. Soon we discovered the cause. Eight or nine very tiny ducklings, with still-bristling newly-grown and immature off-color feathers, were madly running, directionless, in the middle of the road. In a group they scurried in one direction, then halted, then, after pausing for a poised moment, dashed in a frenzy in a different direction, then again halted, then scurried in a frenzy in another direction. Helter-skelter, this continued for some time. All the while the cars did not move, for fear of crushing their fragile little bodies.

Finally, drivers and passengers alike descended from their cars and began to carefully pick up the panic-stricken ducklings and gently place them into a cardboard box, until the last duckling was safely secured. Only then did the cars move.

Then we saw the cause: the mother duck was lying dead on the road, where she had been recently struck by a passing car.

Unprotected, the ducklings lacked direction and shelter. How could humans, however well intentioned they may be, become a surrogate mother for them?

Over the next several days, my thoughts went back to these unsheltered little entities. What would be their fate?

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.19, Prahlāda Mahārāja prays:

My Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva, O Supreme, because of a bodily conception of life, embodied souls neglected and not cared for by You cannot do anything for their betterment. Whatever remedies they accept, although perhaps temporarily beneficial, are certainly impermanent. For example, a father and mother cannot protect their child, a physician and medicine cannot relieve a suffering patient, and a boat on the ocean cannot protect a drowning man.

The lesson was 100% clear. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us devotional service, which is the means to gain the shelter of Kṛṣṇa. Without that shelter, any sense of protection or safety or well-being is but a terrible illusion.

Showing us the way of furthering his devotion, Prahlāda prays for the continued association of Nārada Muni, his spiritual master, so that he (Prahlāda) can be further trained in the ways of devotional service.

O great one, O Supreme Lord, because of combination with pleasing and displeasing circumstances and because of separation from them, one is placed in a most regrettable position, within heavenly or hellish planets, as if burning in a fire of lamentation. Although there are many remedies by which to get out of miserable life, any such remedies in the material world are more miserable than the miseries themselves. Therefore I think that the only remedy is to engage in Your service. Kindly instruct me in such service. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.17\]

We likewise pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to continue to guide us on the path of devotional service by your Bhaktivedanta purports and by the association of your dedicated, unalloyed servants.

In the days that followed the incident with the ducklings, my service to you and your wonderful Society of Vaiṣṇavas disclosed so many opportunities, by challenge or by circumstance, to connect with you and utilize the situation to create a unified *saṅga* of Vaiṣṇavas. Or it disclosed how failing to do so would result in the situation appearing to be a source of immense distress. Or it showed why when I encountered a wonderful preaching opportunity I should feel great gratitude to you for providing this vision of devotional service and should become enlivened and encouraged. Or it showed that seeing only the obstacles blocking the path of progress toward fulfilling that preaching opportunity results in becoming resigned to stagnancy or, worse, discouraged.

You have taught us to see both good fortune and apparent misfortune equally, to see how both circumstances somehow provide us with an opportunity for service and surrender. By taking shelter of this teaching by *applying* it in practical circumstances that arise in day-to-day life and service, we currently find a tangible and palpable shelter in you and your loving service. In that position we feel both absolutely safe and protected, and we also continuously feel ourselves being brought ever closer to your surrendered followers. How satisfying both of those outcomes of your loving service are!!

Unity is found in this way, through common service—including the “challenging” kind—and not only on Vyāsa-pūjā day!

I am very much valuing the expanding experience of unity among your sincere followers as I travel throughout North America, while still respecting the diversity that exists among us all. Such diversity! Unity is such a blessing, and such an important quality to strive for!

In a similar expression of gratitude and appreciation of your shelter and service, I am thrilled to report to you that, in my travels, I find ever-increasing examples of very creative ways devotees have developed to carefully study your books in a systematic way. I just met Bṛhad Mṛdaṅga Prabhu, for example, who is busy touring the West to systematically train congregation members in the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I am finding abundant and ever-proliferating study groups, reading groups, conference-call classes, distance learning, small and large Bhakti-śāstrī courses, and so forth — all focused upon absorption and assimilation of the divine teachings found in your books.

I know, beyond a shadow of a doubt, that this is most pleasing to you!

One other very encouraging sign I am happy to report is that in America many youths and older persons as well are taking very serious interest in Kṛṣṇa consciousness once again. I see this wherever I go. We join together in praying for your mercy, to somehow capture these sincere souls’ hearts with your teachings and our demonstration of the qualities that you wanted to nurture in us by your association. May these young Western persons join the ranks of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement!

Finally, a number of younger devotees are taking up leadership positions within your Society, and this is most encouraging! Thank you for inspiring them in this way!

Just last night I had a vivid dream. I saw the same young ducklings, moving about in unison just as before, but now with matured feathers and bigger bodies. They were not fully adult in stature yet, but clearly they were slightly matured. And clearly they were acting and moving about with unity.

When I awoke, I immediately thought of the sense of shelter and protection found in your service. We, too, can become mature if we stay safely in your shelter and service, living our lives by your divine instructions.

I pray to remain ever committed to you and your unalloyed service, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please keep me fixed like a tiny dust particle clinging to your lotus feet.

Whatever service you place before me, I am yours.

Your eternal servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

You deserve all praise as the spiritual master who was the first to bring pure Vedic knowledge, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, from India to the West, beginning in America. The gift you brought, under the order of your spiritual master, cannot be overestimated. Kṛṣṇa consciousness brings liberation from birth and death. It is the path to eternal love of God in its most pristine form, worship of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Your expertise was to bring the highest spiritual teaching in a simple form, which any sincere follower could take. You presented this in your lucid translations from original Sanskrit and Bengali texts, especially *Bhagavad-gītā*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* along with your Bhaktivedanta purports to the texts. Many millions of copies of these books are being distributed in dozens of the world’s languages.

By the proliferation of temples and book distribution, your ISKCON (International Society for Krishna Consciousness) has truly become a worldwide religious movement.

As one tiny follower of Your Divine Grace, I feel a very personal connection as son to father. You assured me of this in my early years of surrender to you. When I asked you whether you or my material father was my real father, you replied that my material father was only temporary or illusory, whereas you were my *real* and *eternal* father.

I feel very privileged today to praise you along with my godbrothers, godsisters, and other followers.

Now we must accompany our homages with the contribution of work and surrender to your mission. Then as we are proud of you, you will see us as worthy and be proud of us.

Your servant and son,  
Satsvarūpa Dāsa Goswami

## Śeṣa dasa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

A poem for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda . . .

**Śrī Guru**

Guru plants the seed . . . wait—  
Patience is required.  
Advancement you will make—  
Chant! Chant! Be inspired.  
Truth, Mercy, Celibate—  
Guru’s order does conspire.  
Hearts clean, then do wake  
Beings earthly mired.  
Guru’s mercy does create  
Vrajeśvarīs desire.

Your servant,  
Śeṣa Dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Sitting down to write your Vyāsa-pūjā offering means sitting down to think about you; thinking about you means understanding how you always thought about preaching, how you always preached, and how you wanted us to always think of preaching and to always preach.

Preaching, you said, is the best way to be recognized by Kṛṣṇa, the secret to getting His mercy. But preaching is also not without risk, for it means money, followers, and position—serious allurements for aspiring transcendentalists; it means making enemies of materialists, religionists, and governments—serious obstacles to a successful movement.

And if those were not hazards enough, preaching means a very personal kind of inconvenience, something beyond the risk of falldown or persecution, a kind of difficulty most beautifully spoken of in relation to Lord Śiva’s drinking the ocean of poison.

*tapyante loka-tāpena sādhavaḥ prāyaśo janāḥ*  
*paramārādhanaṁ tad dhi puruṣasyākhilātmanaḥ*

**“It is said that great personalities almost always accept voluntary suffering because of the suffering of people in general. This is considered the highest method of worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is present in everyone’s heart.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **8.7.44)**

When as a *brahmacārī* I first read this verse, I envisaged that for Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s cause I would be either crucified like Jesus Christ or tortured like Prahlāda Mahārāja. “OK,” I thought. “I’ll bear the cross.” However, as years of preaching revealed, the “voluntary suffering”Śukadeva Gosvāmī speaks of is more a crucifixion of the heart than of the body. This crucifixion of the heart—a suffering as painful as nails in the hand or a spear in the ribs—is caused by seeing devotees turn back from the doorstep of Vaikuṇṭha.

Preaching means not only pointing the way to Godhead but patiently guiding devotees along the path that leads there. In giving such guidance, I often find myself in the role of a spiritual father, a person closely connected with the evolving lives of his spiritual children. And that path is not without its share of dangers and obstacles. Sometimes devotees lose heart while waging war with Māyā-devī, an enemy who never tires. Sometimes they succumb to the urges of the senses and call a truce, only to perish. Sometimes they lose faith in the institution, in its leadership, in me, even in you. Sometimes they stray to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness their own way, only to deviate.

When these things happen, devotees often give up their vows, their services, their practices; they lose spiritual self-confidence, become critical, sometimes acrimonious, devoid of spiritual radiance. At these times I feel like a parent who laments the rebelliousness of his children. This was the ungratefulness my parents spoke of when I acted contrary to their wisdom. This was the sorrow they felt. The foolishness, stubbornness, breach of trust, and harsh words of devotees are heart-wrenching—in Jīva Gosvāmīs words, “as painful as a lance in the heart.” But a lance plunged into the heart causes pain only once, at the instant before death. But the sight and sound of dispirited Vaiṣṇavas cause pain repeatedly, for after witnessing them I continue to live. That pain brings forth warm tears, deep sighs, and the perennial question “How can I continue like this?”

And invariably I remember your order to preach till the last, your personal example in this regard, your reassurance that this voluntary suffering will please the Lord. Then I take heart, though my heart appears increasingly scarred.

I thus turn to you, who are always thinking of preaching. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please sometimes think of me, your insignificant servant, one who tries his best to preach. When you do, please shower some mercy my way, some special salve that quickly mends broken hearts, that gives determination to preachers, that soothes the pain of voluntary suffering.

Your servant  
Śivarāma Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

You saved me. You are always saving me. You are my Lord and master. But when will I truly and practically accept this? That is up to me. You have demonstrated your love and compassion for us all. Now it is up to me to reciprocate. I would like to try, or so I think. Thank you for all that you are doing for us. I will keep trying. Thank you for always being there as my guide and shelter.

Your insignificant servant, the fool,  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-'bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.**

**When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?**

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious 110thanniversary of your appearance, I wish to humbly rededicate myself to your preaching mission, which continues, like you, to be the most glorious.

In the pastime where Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu mildly slapped Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī out of love when he read His mind and wrote a verse in response to the Lord’s most inner feelings, you write in that purport:

Devotees acknowledge Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s special mercy upon Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī in the following words:

*śrī-caitanya-mano-‘bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

The special function of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī is to establish the feelings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. These feelings are His desires that His special mercy be spread throughout the world in this Kali-yuga.

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

The desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is that all over the world everyone, in every village and every town, know of Him and His *saṅkīrtana* movement. These are the inner feelings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī committed to writing all these feelings of the Lord. Now again, by the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the same feelings are being spread all over the world by the servants of the Gosvāmīs, and devotees who are pure and simple will appreciate this attempt. As concluded by Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, however, those who are on the level of hogs and dogs will never appreciate such a great attempt. Yet this does not matter to the preachers of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s cult, for all over the world they will continue to perform this responsible work, even though persons who are like cats and dogs do not appreciate them. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā,* 1.117\]

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have so wonderfully understood the inner feelings of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who in turn has fully imbibed the inner feelings of all the previous *ācāryas,* up to the Six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself. As in the example of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, who received the special mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, by which he could carry out the *saṅkīrtana* mission successfully, so without receiving your special mercy, it will remain impossible for me to understand your most inner feelings and desires.

You have given us all this most responsible work of helping you spread the glorious mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in every town and village. You remain our constant source of inspiration and our guiding force in pushing forward this *saṅkīrtana* mission. May I ever remain indebted to you for giving me such a wonderful opportunity to be part of this sacred mission.

This mission of Lord Caitanya remains ever fresh and pure, as shown when we see new souls becoming very inspired when coming in contact with the process of devotional service. You have taught us the unchanging and eternal process of *bhakti,* a process that does not require us to change it every couple of years, as mundane processes require change and re-adaptation. In one of your powerful lectures on the topic of “change,” you heavily criticized those who wanted to introduce change for the sake of change. Thus you protected us against the modern Western disease of always wanting to introduce something new. The process of devotional service is perfect and complete, just as the Vedic knowledge is perfect and complete, because it is given by the Lord Himself, who remains ever perfect and complete.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help me understand and articulate more clearly how the processes of *sanātana-dharma* and *varṇāśrama-dharma* are both standard processes given by Lord Kṛṣṇa, and how they are meant to be applied and followed by all people, at all times, and in all places. In the same way that your devotional service is the means of serving you in the spiritual world of Hari-dhāma, similarly, the social process of *varṇāśrama-dharma* and *varṇāśrama* education are the means for serving you while we are still here in this material world of Devī-dhāma. If we simply follow the process Lord Kṛṣṇa has given us to practice while in the material world, the system of *varṇa* and *āśrama,* then those very activities, being performed for His pleasure and satisfaction, will automatically become our devotional service in this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am simply amazed at how you so clearly and boldly presented this concept of “a classless society” while referring to the social system of *varṇāśrama*. Persons with a mundane vision regard the divisions of *varṇa* and *āśrama* as “class distinction” or “class division”; yet you, with your transcendental vision, refer to this same “class division” as “a classless society.” The reason is easily understood: the focus of *daivī-varṇāśrama* remains devotional service to Kṛṣṇa; hence we are all servants of the same master, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and class divisions become very secondary.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that I may develop a similar vision in the matter of understanding the concept of Gītā-Nāgarī. You wanted so much that we understand and put into practice the simple concepts based on simple living and high thinking, which you have so wonderfully expounded in your essay on Gītā-Nāgarī: “Make Vṛndāvana villages.” In the same way that your life was always centered on the service to Lord Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana, I request that you kindly bestow some mercy upon me so that I may develop a similar vision and understanding and thus always be nicely engaged in your devotional service.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I fall at your lotus feet and beg your kind mercy again and again so that I may never deviate from the association of your surrendered devotees and always somehow remain engaged in helping the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Please allow me to show you my appreciation and gratitude by always being engaged in your devotional service with all enthusiasm and with ever-increasing surrender.

Your aspiring servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are the empowered representative of Lord Caitanya and an emblem of pure devotion for the Divine Couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

On this auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this world, we have the opportunity to recount your unlimited glories and pastimes in this world. When we explain to guests how at the age of seventy you traveled to the Western world on a boat without friends or money, they are truly amazed. Seeing your fine temples with beautifully decorated Deities and elaborate worship, books full of timeless wisdom and philosophy, and followers from all parts of the world, they can at once appreciate that you were no ordinary person. As it is said,

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27)**

This verse also appears in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 1.46), where Your Divine Grace comments as follows:

This is a verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.17.27) spoken by Lord Kṛṣṇa when He was questioned by Uddhava regarding the four social and spiritual orders of society. The Lord was specifically instructing how a *brahmacārī* should behave under the care of a spiritual master. A spiritual master is not an enjoyer of facilities offered by his disciples. He is like a parent. Without the attentive service of his parents, a child cannot grow to manhood; similarly, without the care of the spiritual master one cannot rise to the plane of transcendental service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have been a child long enough. I should have risen to manhood years ago, but I thought only of my own pleasure and was content to take service from you. I was happy to live in your temples, enjoying the facilities. I was the center of my existence, when it should have been you. Now, in my old age, I can see my faults and the errors of my ways. I therefore fall at your feet and pray to you to please forgive this fallen soul and allow me to use this fag end of my life in service at your feet.

Your unworthy son eternally,  
Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am completely dependent on your mercy. “Completely” means *completely*. Not 90% or 99.9% or 99.999%. Completely: 100%. I have no ability or strength or intelligence of my own. Whatever I am in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is due only to your mercy. And my many failings are simply my own nonsense.

Due to my own offensiveness and unwillingness, I haven’t gone very far in devotional service, and that I am still on the path is due only to your mercifully not kicking me away, as I deserve, but keeping me engaged. All my attempts in *sādhana,* preaching, and other endeavors in Kṛṣṇa’s service depend entirely on your mercy, without which I am nothing. Only if imbued with your mercy can my activities be successful.

Realization of dependence on the guru’s mercy naturally arises early in the spiritual career of a disciple, so it might seem strange that I am expressing such a sentiment after a relatively long period in your service. These feelings are not new to me, but recently struck me afresh as I was considering how some of my disciples—flowers whom you have sent to be prepared for offering to Kṛṣṇa—apparently feel toward me. They might think I am great and wonderful, but I am not. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are great and wonderful.

Remembering your greatness and my smallness keeps me safe under the shelter of your lotus feet. For me to accept disciples is certainly risky, but if I just remember that your mercy is all that I am made of, and that my duty is to transmit that mercy to others, then I will remain safe. I am inadequate, but you carry what I lack and preserve it—by your mercy.

Happily bowing down at your lotus feet, I pray millions of times to be your truly humble servant,  
Bhakti Vikāsa Swami

## Candramauli Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each year as we undertake this service of writing our Vyāsa-pūjā offering to you, we can deeply reflect on the many spiritual treasures you have so mercifully bestowed upon us. This reflection increases our appreciation of you and our understanding of this rare treasure of pure devotional service.

Among these precious gifts is the gift of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya-līlā* 22.113) Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī mentions this verse from the *Padma Purāṇa:*

*smartavyaḥ satataṁ viṣṇur vismartavyo na jātucit*  
*sarve vidhi-niṣedhāḥ syur etayor eva kiṅkarāḥ*

**“Kṛṣṇa is the origin of Lord Viṣṇu. He should always be remembered and never forgotten at any time. All the rules and prohibitions mentioned in the śāstras should be the servants of these two principles.”**

This verse is a quotation from the *Padma Purāṇa*. There are many regulative principles in the *śāstras* and directions given by the spiritual master. These regulative principles should act as servants of the basic principle—that is, one should always remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him. This is possible when one chants the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*. Therefore one must strictly chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* twenty-four hours daily. One may have other duties to perform under the direction of the spiritual master, but he must first abide by the spiritual master’s order to chant a certain number of rounds. In our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, we have recommended that the neophyte chant at least sixteen rounds. This chanting of sixteen rounds is absolutely necessary if one wants to remember Kṛṣṇa and not forget Him. Of all the regulative principles, the spiritual master’s order to chant at least sixteen rounds is most essential.

One may sell books or enlist life members or render some other service, but these duties are not ordinary duties. These duties serve as an impetus for remembering Kṛṣṇa. When one goes with a *saṅkīrtana* party or sells books, he naturally remembers that he is going to sell Kṛṣṇa’s books. In this way, he is remembering Kṛṣṇa. When one goes to enlist a life member, he talks about Kṛṣṇa and thereby remembers Him. *Smartavyaḥ satataṁ viṣṇur vismartavyo na jātucit*. The conclusion is that one must act in such a way that he will always remember Kṛṣṇa, and one must refrain from doing things that make him forget Kṛṣṇa. These two principles form the basic background of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

It seems like if one very carefully tries to follow these instructions, there is no service that is too difficult.

Lord Caitanya stated that the duty of a devotee is twofold: to become Kṛṣṇa conscious and to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to other. Becoming your instrument to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others entails accepting any and every responsibility and instruction given by you.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya-līlā* 4.144, purport) you write:

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s devotional cult teaches one not to sit down in one place but to spread the devotional cult all over the world. The Lord dispatched Sanātana Gosvāmī and Rūpa Gosvāmī to Vṛndāvana to excavate and renovate the holy places and from there establish the cult of *bhakti*. Therefore Vṛndāvana was given to Sanātana Gosvāmī and Rūpa Gosvāmī as their place of residence. Similarly, everyone in the line of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s devotional cult should accept the words of the spiritual master and thus spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. They should go everywhere, to all parts of the world, accepting those places as their *prabhu-datta deśa*, the places of residence given by the spiritual master or Lord Kṛṣṇa. The spiritual master is the representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa; therefore one who has carried out the orders of the spiritual master is understood to have carried out the orders of Kṛṣṇa or Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu wanted to spread the *bhakti* cult all over the world (*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*). \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\] Therefore devotees in the line of Kṛṣṇa consciousness must go to different parts of the world and preach, as ordered by the spiritual master. That will satisfy Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states:

Whenever one offers a prayer to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one always requests some benediction from Him. Even pure (*niṣkāma*) devotees pray for some benediction, as instructed by Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His *Śikṣāṣṭaka: ayi nanda-tanuja kiṅkaraṁ patitaṁ māṁ viṣame bhavāmbudhau/ kṛpayā tava pāda-paṅkaja-sthita-dhūlī-sadṛśaṁ vicintaya*.

I am not a pure devotee, but I want to please you by assisting you to the best of my ability in spreading Lord Caitanya’s mission. Therefore my prayer is to become a more qualified instrument for giving the mercy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much.

A fallen but happy dog at your lotus feet,  
Candramauli Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Thank you for a philosophy that is actually internally consistent.

Thank you for taking the trouble to bring it from India—an amazing task.

Thank you for the movement intended to help us follow and understand.

Thank you for the regulative principles, without which I would have certainly floundered.

Thank you for insisting that we help others as we have been helped; it is Lord Caitanya’s demand.

Thank you for showing by your personal example what we should be.

Thank you for explaining the basics, explaining the basics, explaining the basics.

Thank you for inspiring the devotees who have reached out their hands to me.

Thank you for removing my darkest fears and making them small in the light of Truth.

Thank you for making Vedic knowledge abundantly available.

Thank you for making it self-confirming from many perspectives.

Thank you for making it clear.

Thank you for basic mass preaching strategies.

Thank you for the knowledge that we must also extend preaching by our own intelligence.

Thank you for bringing the Deity forms and helping me realize what They are.

Thank you for showing that I can have a simple and personal relationship with Them.

Thank you for Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa; Jagannātha-Subhadrā-Balarāma; Sītā-Rāma; Prahlāda-Nṛsiṁha, and more. How would I have ever guessed that God appears in such variety?

Thank you for a real explanation of the word *love*.

Thank you for a working definition of *truth*.

Thank you for practical *sādhana,* which is what is lacking in today’s religion.

Thank you for simple arguments against the skeptics, sour realists, dogmatic fanatics, determined hedonists, vacuous scientific atheists, too-down-to-earth fools, only-wrong-wayers, workaday-workaholic-world-without-end-blind-men.

Thank you for the holy name.

Thank you for the holy name.

Thank you for the holy name.

(I would have gone insane.)

Thank you for *maṅgala-ārati* and *Bhāgavatam* class.

Thank you for a music, an art, and a culture I never would have explored.

Thank you for a kitchen religion that brought me through the door.

Thank you for the festivals that refresh my life.

For all this and more . . .

Thank you.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Candramukha Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

Every year, on this sublime occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I take the opportunity to take special *darśana* from you. It is a special situation in which I can internally reveal my mind to you and receive direct instructions from you. I believe this relationship is possible because you are always there to hear our prayers and understand our difficulties, and therefore always available to instill the transcendental light of your teachings in our hearts.

However, there seems to be more than your internal presence on this special occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā. It is as though you are again physically present among us, staying in your sacred quarters, receiving each devotee personally, and offering your unique help and protection. As I contemplate all this in a heightened mood, with my heart beating hard, I enter your room and fall down at your feet, requesting to take *darśana*. Devoid of courage and the audacity to look directly at your brilliant face, shining like sunlight, I simply contemplate your feet with complete satisfaction. Your lotus feet are the true source of shelter for sincere souls.

I sincerely apologize for my faults. I am also aware of my weaknesses, of which I feel ashamed. There is no doubt that I have committed repeated mistakes, and I deeply regret my fallen condition. Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the only thing left to do is ask for your forgiveness and beg for your mercy so that I may have the strength to continue.

I daily chant your name and call upon you. But am I worthy of your attention? On your side is the omnipotent Lord, in the supreme golden abode by the imposing temple, surrounded by thousands of pure and qualified singers. What chance do I have with my timid song and whining pledge? But you are merciful, and I am so fortunate to have received the torch of knowledge form Your Divine Grace — a benediction I don’t deserve. I also received the privilege to speak on your behalf — another reward I ought not to have.

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please don’t give up on me. You are my only hope, for my disturbed mind is pacified only by your association. As I sit on the ground and gaze into the sky, I dream of your arrival, seeing a sudden brilliance as you approach and come to my side. The flags on your divine carriage flutter, and when we meet I look at your moonlike face in great astonishment. Then you raise me from the ground and place me by your side. Like a beggar who just received a fortune, I remain in bliss with my heart absorbed by your love.

Your eternal servant  
Chandramukha Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear beloved Guru Mahārāja, Your Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

In this darkest of ages, you provide light for the sincere souls. You translated the *Vedas* into English and other languages, and the Bhaktivedanta purports reveal Your Divine Grace to be a stylist:

The *Vedas* are made just to revive the forgotten consciousness of the conditioned souls, and any literature not meant for reviving God consciousness is rejected at once by the *nārāyaṇa-para* devotees. Such deluding books of knowledge, not having Nārāyaṇa as their aim, are not at all knowledge, but are the playgrounds for crows who are interested in the rejected refuse of the world. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.5.15 purport\]

Do we have time—a day, a year—to focus our attention on ordinary literature or mundane arts—even if they may produce a glowworm’s glimmer of illumination? Should we be content to read relative comics when exalted transcendental literature is available? That transcendental literature, however, needs to be heard from bona fide teachers and put into practice in the association of devotees.

Although earthlings’ hearts are desert dry, still some are ready to absorb the nectar oasis. Pursuing knowledge and peace outside of Vedic scriptures is like trying to chase a mirage. Only by the mercy of the bona fide spiritual master can one stay on the path to attain Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet with determination and intelligence. Māyā is so powerful that one cannot do it alone. By your causeless mercy, the great expansive material ocean reduces to a tiny drop of water.

The thing that offends Lord Kṛṣṇa the most is to offend His beloved servants. Therefore, we must regularly check to make sure we are not slipping into a grave emergency. We are prone to four flaws—error, illusion, cheating, and imperfect senses. By our own efforts we are allured by Māyā, then beaten and kicked in the teeth.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like the local Vaikuṇṭha planet — Dhruvaloka, the polestar — ever fixed in our midst. And as all other planets revolve around that extraordinary planet, all intelligent luminaries humbly honor you by revolving around your perfect instructions.

I spoke to a scientist yesterday who somberly admitted that modern science faces a tremendous crisis by attempting to explain how the universe should be filled with 95% dark matter. “There should be much more matter,” he said. He concluded that physics’ string theory has thus far failed to provide verifiable answers. You always taught us that speculation can never replace real knowledge.

Now we take complete shelter at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet, begging for revelation of the *śāstras* and the reality they describe. This is the method for understanding the *Vedas: yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau/ tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*.

Your insignificant beggar,  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

My life is yours, although I am still bound to such illusions as attachment to inert matter and the objects of the senses, diversions, mind fluctuations, enjoyment of philosophical inquires, and adoption of a false psycho-social identity. Hence, my soul is yet very conditioned. Fortunately, Your Divine Grace touches my innermost self and, as your servant, I completely accept your instructions. Please be patient and forgive my conditioned state.

You possess qualities, virtues, and values of both human and divine beings. Your dignity is unquestionable. Your obedience to the orders of your spiritual master is incomparable. Your vigor and strength are invincible. Your intelligence is insurmountable. Your devotion to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa is pragmatic, externally active, internally pure, and esoteric.

By your mercy and patience, I have come to firmly believe that one day I may prostrate myself unto your lotus feet with pure love.

Your eternal servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**The Empowered Preacher**

Sometimes He requests His devotee to preach His glories all over the world, although He alone is quite competent to perform this task. Even though the Supreme Personality of Godhead is endowed with all possessions and is self-sufficient, He depends on His devotees. Therefore the relationship of the Lord with His devotees is extremely confidential. Only the devotee can perceive how the Lord, although full in Himself, depends on His devotee for some particular work. This is explained in *Bhagavad-gītā* (11.33), where the Lord tells Arjuna, *nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savyasācin:* “O Arjuna, merely be an instrument in the fight.” Lord Kṛṣṇa had the competence to win the Battle of Kurukṣetra, but nonetheless He induced His devotee Arjuna to fight and become the cause of victory. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was quite competent enough to spread His name and mission all over the world, but still He depended upon His devotee to do this work. Considering all these points, the most important aspect of the Supreme Lord’s self-sufficiency is that He depends on His devotees. This is called His causeless mercy. The devotee who has perceived this causeless mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead by realization can understand the master and the servant. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.19.5, purport\]

Just as Śrī Kṛṣṇa annihilated the Kuru armies through the strong arms of Arjuna, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu delivered the esoteric truths of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *tattva* through the eloquent words of Rāmānanda Rāya. Śrī Rāmānanda admitted:

Do not consider me the speaker in these talks about Kṛṣṇa. Whatever I speak is personally spoken by Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Like a stringed instrument, I vibrate whatever He causes me to speak. In this way the Lord speaks through my mouth to preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā* 5.72–73\]

And just as Rāmānanda Rāya became the instrument for the preaching of Mahāprabhu, so did Śrīla Prabhupāda become the effective mouthpiece of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and our entire Gauḍīya lineage.

One may wonder why the Lord chooses to exhibit His glories more through one servant than another. Śrīla Prabhupāda has supplied a clue in the above-quoted purport: The devotee must be in an “extremely confidential” relationship with the Lord. Of course, confidentiality is necessarily preceded by a period of examination, and the fact that a devotee is able to endure such severe tests and austerities is added evidence of the mercy of the Lord. This was demonstrated especially in the early days of Prabhupāda’s mission, and it has fortunately been recorded for our instruction and his eternal glorification.

Furthermore, such a confidential devotee must intensely desire and fervently pray to be engaged as the Lord’s agent, and this was also displayed by Śrīla Prabhupāda:

I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own. . . . I can simply repeat Your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding. Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. . . . \[*Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma,* 10, 12–13\]

Finally, just as Rāmānanda Rāya certified his humility by attributing his own words to the intelligence of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, so Śrīla Prabhupāda humbly attributed all his success to the mercy of his spiritual master:

Actually I am not worthy of any one of the words spoken by you but all of them are due to my Spiritual Master Who was so kind to me. In fact I am a worthless person because my Spiritual Master ordered me to take up this work in 1922 but I did not carry his order until 1958, when I was obliged to carry out His order by His arrangement only. This means although I was not very enthusiastic to carry out His order He forced me circumstantially to accept it. So this is His special mercy upon me and I always think about this with gratitude to this exalted personality coming directly from Vaikuntha World and we had the great fortune to meet Him. I think that is the only credit on our part that we happened to meet Him by some “ajnata sukriti” or unknown auspicious activities. He is so kind upon me that when I came to your country, where I was completely unknown, He sent to me some good souls like you unsolicited. So I accept you all as assistants or representatives of my Guru Maharaja Who is still helping me because I am so feeble and unworthy. \[Letter to Hayagrīva Dāsa, 14 January 1970\]

Such is the exalted nature of the empowered preacher.His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda *kī jaya!*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

His prospective servant,  
Gaṇapati Dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the cooling shade of your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day, this struggling soul begs at your lotus feet for empowerment. It is certainly true that by your grace the preaching in our *yātrā* has been increasing. We have more buildings, more programs, more devotees, more public recognition, and more possibilities. That is all very enlivening to us. At the same time, we are seeing now how much we lack, how far we have to go to be truly effective in your service. You always made it look so easy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In spite of an increasing number of disciples and more and more temples, fame, money, and success in every sphere, you nevertheless remained humble and fixed in consciousness. You never became ensnared by the pitfalls of Māyā. Your only happiness and satisfaction came from chanting and preaching the glories of the Lord. You saw everything in a clear and simple way and never got entangled the complexities of conditioned life. You always knew exactly what to do and when to do it. You said the right things at the right time and in the right way. As I approach the age that you took *sannyāsa,* I can’t help but compare myself to you and realize how much more I need your help to accomplish during this lifetime even a tiny, infinitesimal fragment of what you did all over the world in just twelve short years. Thus, on this most auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance, I offer this prayer of supplication at your lotus feet:

I pray to you for the purity to lead with transparency.  
I pray for the power to affect others by your words.  
I pray for understanding to give proper guidance.  
I pray for continued strength to maintain my vows.  
I pray for enthusiasm to inspire.  
I pray to imbibe your preaching vision.  
I pray for the heart to love.  
I pray for the humility to see all my faults.  
I pray for the courage to give up my pride.  
I pray for the wisdom to make the right choices.  
I pray for the sensitivity to respond well to others’ needs.  
I pray for humor to lighten the day.  
I pray for equanimity to chastise with affection.  
I pray for soft-heartedness to forgive.  
I pray to be unaffected by worldly association.  
I pray for discipline to regulate my life.  
I pray for sweet words to communicate my feelings.  
I pray to appreciate the sincere service of others.  
I pray to become more absorbed in *śāstra*.  
I pray to love Kṛṣṇa more each day.  
I pray to receive your smile of approval.  
I pray to see only the good in everyone.  
I pray to be unimpeded by old age and disease.  
I pray for the courage to face impending death.  
And finally, I pray to always remember that I am absolutely nothing more than your humble servant.

My petition was quite long. But although I have asked many things for myself, my heartfelt desire is to use everything only for you. You are the repository and bestower of all that is wonderful, all those sublime qualities that have made you the greatest of personalities and that have enabled those empowered by you to be the torchbearers of your glorious legacy.

I am far from sincere, far from qualified to be a member of your upper ranks. I am an offender who has not been doing much at all to extricate himself from the mire of this age. But still you have kept me in your service, and for this I am eternally grateful. Please accept this meager offering as a plea to better myself in the only way possible — by taking refuge at your divine, cooling, and inconceivably potent lotus feet.

Praying that I will never stop crying for your mercy, I remain . . .

Your insignificant servant,  
Guṇagrāhi Dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

Let us offer our obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter of His lotus feet. We offer our obeisances to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are a servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī and are saving the Western countries from voidism and impersonalism by spreading the teachings of Lord Caitanya.

Dear Srila Prabhupada,

In this Vyāsa-pūjā book, from your disciples and admirers there will be so many attempts to glorify your qualities and contributions. That is very nice. It is like trying to glorify a very nice city with so many parks, beautiful buildings, citizens, and heroic events. We humbly submit that trying to glorify you is like that — an endeavor that requires the attentive effort of many enthusiastic people writing, painting, and talking for considerable time, and it seems to us that the effort would still be incomplete.

In this festival of your qualities and activities, what should we humbly try to illuminate? There are so many things. What finally came to our intelligence was that we really were standing in the darkness of ignorance. Even now we repeatedly fall into ignorance, as simply as just falling asleep each night and having to wake up and reconstruct our intelligence each morning.

Our first appreciation is that you have given us the *mahā-mantra*. It wakes us up, and the first word in the *mahā-mantra* is *hare*, “Oh, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī,”“Oh, Vṛṣabhānu-nandinī.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

How many people can every realize this? In this current world we are dying from a lack of mothers. The understanding of womanhood, motherhood, has sunk to something shallow, flashy, with bright colors, but no depth.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us a mother to whom we can cry like stupid babies. If we understand correctly, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī is the spiritual mother of countless numbers of universes, and in Her the concept of mother is most highly manifested. She is the mother of all the cowherd boys, and even one of the best mother of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself.

We hope to just chant these two syllables, *ha* and *re,* with the proper attitude, as you instruct in your *Kṛṣṇa* book, *Upadeśāmṛta,* volumes of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and so many other books. By your mercy, we hope to glorify Kṛṣṇa and Rāma as They appear in this *mahā-mantra,* for Her satisfaction.

I think that’s all I want to say. Now I pray to hear the words of other devotees on this wonderful day.

Thank you for this *mahā-mantra*. Thank you for giving us the hope that we can become eternally situated in your association, like singing bees in Vṛndāvana flying in the dust of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇīs and Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

Your humble servant,  
Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

This year I am writing my offering to you in Mexico. I have just returned from a *harināma* party in Cuernavaca, a town outside Mexico City. But it wasn’t just any *harināma*. While chanting in the local plaza, a devotee informed me that you yourself had chanted and given a public lecture there in 1972. Not only that, but you had autographed your books and sold them to interested people at that outdoor event. I was astounded! Suddenly the plaza took on a special charm. It became a *tīrtha* — a holy place where your lotus feet had walked and you had chanted the holy names for the masses.

There are many such places on the earth planet — and, I imagine, beyond as well. Your mission, based on deep compassion for the suffering conditioned souls in this world, is very broad and expansive. I feel fortunate to be able to assist you and play a small role in your efforts.

A disciple one asked you about your identity in the spiritual world. You replied, “If I told you, you would faint.” It is not for us to know that now, but one day, when we are pure, we will see you daily in your spiritual form as we assist you in your service in the spiritual sky. For now, we are duty-bound to serve your mission here. It is such service alone that will qualify us to join your pastimes in the spiritual sky.

*paraṁ gopyam api snigdhe śiṣye vācyam iti śrutiḥ*  
*tac chrūyatāṁ mahā-bhāga goloka-mahimādhunā*

**“The** ***Vedas*** **say that to a loyal disciple one may speak even the most confidential secret. Therefore, O most fortunate one, now please hear the glories of Goloka.” (*****Śrī Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta*** **2.1.6)**

Please bless me to help fulfill your desires here, so that one day I may serve you properly in the transcendental abode, where you sport eternally with your beloved Lord.

Your servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This Vyāsa-pūjā book started out as a small booklet in which the disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda offered their homage to their spiritual master. It has turned into something else: an official institutional book in which offerings from each temple and project appear, along with offerings from people belonging to certain privileged classes — *sannyāsīs* and members of the GBC.

I’m not sure what I think of this. Some people resent it and would like to see the book opened up, at least to all of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples. (I would like to see that too, though the editors of the book say it would bury them beneath an avalanche of logistical problems.)

For me, I suppose, the changes in the book reflect the changes inevitable from ISKCON’s growth. We have long ago stopped being a ragtag band of disciples, inspired by our personal touch with our guru. Śrīla Prabhupāda now “belongs to the world,” or at least to his generations of followers.

Tens of thousands of people now revere Śrīla Prabhupāda and dedicate themselves to his service. They connect with him through his books, his teachings, his followers, his movement. Some take seriously to the discipline he taught. Some strictly follow the process he recommended, the process taught by our *sampradāya-ācāryas* and the scriptures.

Other people, also claiming to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda, concoct their own methods, promote all sorts of rubbish, and create a disturbance in the community of devotees.

Do I want to talk about that? Probably not. It’s wearisome, and does nothing to add to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s glories.

But it’s part of a larger issue: How do we understand what Śrīla Prabhupāda taught?
