It’s easy: We read his books, hear his words, and follow his instructions. Yet it’s hard: “You read black where I read white.”

But let’s try to make a start.

First of all, we ought to follow the most basic of the orders he gave us: Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, sixteen rounds a day, and strictly follow the four regulative principles.

“Disciple,” he said, means “discipline.” We ought to follow that discipline. Otherwise, how will our minds be clear enough to get further understanding?

To conclude that much is easy. And onward from there?

Try to avoid the ten offenses.

This is a lifetime project. We can’t fake it. (At least we can’t fool Kṛṣṇa.) And we have our work cut out for us trying to avoid even the first of these offenses: “To blaspheme the devotees who have dedicated their lives for propagating the holy name of the Lord.” And that offense is only the first; there are nine others.

It is Śrīla Prabhupāda who has given us the great gift of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, and we can chant that name purely only by his mercy:

*ekakīāmāra, nāhi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kori’, śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho’ kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*

**“Alone, I do not find the strength to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Please be merciful by blessing me with a drop of faith and bestow on me the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda is “he at whose feet there are many *prabhus*.” It is only by his mercy that we can have the association of many *prabhus,* and by their mercy that we can have his.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Kadamba Kānana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

By your mercy I have become a caretaker of ISKCON. I am a most insignificant and incapable one, but I am still here as your servant. Forty-one years have passed since you first landed in America and began miraculously spreading this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. All of us have joined at some time or other within that period. I came when the missionary spirit was everything. Our leaders would quote you: “If only you were cent percent pure devotees, you could take over the world within eighteen days.” And we thought we were well on our way.

Now we realize that besides conquering the world, we must also overcome our own material desires. ISKCON looks different now than it did then. The material world is no longer just a place to preach; one can also try to live there in a Kṛṣṇa conscious way. Definitions have changed. What is preaching, actually? To be an exemplary *gṛhastha* is success, while for the renounced *āśramas* simple survival helps create faith. Now we are not only looking at growing numbers but also at individual development and spiritual quality.

As a *sannyāsī,* I still see the mission as my priority. Although I am not a kamikaze, I still want to change the world and do something for you in Africa, in Europe, in Vṛndāvana. I want to be genuine. I want to chant real *japa* and become absorbed in *śāstra,* to be close to Kṛṣṇa, to be a pure devotee, so that in my preaching I can deliver the goods. I’ll try to improve my endeavor, and maybe Kṛṣṇa will grant me some useful results that I can offer to you. You have achieved more than the impossible. You have totally excelled in all aspects of your own personal devotional service and your preaching. Your fame will remain unique throughout history. You changed the world and changed our lives for good.

Praying to remain your servant,  
Kadamba Kānana Swami

## Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

**Śrīla Prabhupāda: The Complete Devotee**

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, dust which to this day continues to purify the earth. How?

Recently a teenage boy in El Paso, Texas, purchased a *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* at a public library book sale. He had three friends who were also seekers of truth, and the four of them began to meet together regularly to read and study the book. A few months later they were all philosophically convinced and so spiritually enlivened that they decided to join the Dallas temple.

We were all amazed at how attached to your lotus feet these young people had become, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just by reading your book. Of course, we all know that you are present in your books, but all-powerful time tends to dull that remembrance. It was as if you had sent these young people as dramatic evidence to remind us that you are personally present in your ecstatic purports.

The next day, these new devotees appeared to be glowing with a happiness that can only come from pure faith. Again we were reminded that only pure faith—faith that if we become Kṛṣṇa conscious all our needs will be fulfilled—can qualify one to perform pure devotional service. The boys with freshly shaved heads and *dhotīs,* the girls dressed in saris, all with bright *tilaka,* looked as if they had descended from the spiritual world. They seemed to have been in the movement forever. And with that pure faith they at once became attached to the forms of the Lord on the altar.

Over the next few weeks we all watched as Māyā tested them. Suspicious parents and relatives had to be pacified; their own minds and senses needed answers to the usual doubts and questions that invariably come up in the hearts of new devotees; the sometimes contradictory advice given by a few well-meaning but sometimes immature older devotees had to be braved. But because these youths had surrendered with their intelligence, having being convinced by your strong arguments, all of them passed these initial tests with poise and maturity. And in the process some of their family members and relatives were being transformed by the company of these new Vaiṣṇavas.

Of course, they needed more than the books to pass these tests. They needed counseling, guidance, and support from your devotees. Eventually these new *bhaktas* will need personal spiritual masters, as confirmed by your teachings. We found in our talks with them that they instinctively understood that from what they had read. We instructed them to fix up their faith in you as their *śikṣā-guru* for at least a year before seeking the shelter of one of your direct disciples as *dīkṣā-guru,* and to know that you were there for them eternally.

The organic or natural spiritual growth of the devotional creepers of these youngsters testifies to the completeness of your personality, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and of the process you gave us for advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*āpane ācare keha, nā kare pracāra*  
*pracāra karena keha, nā karena ācāra*  
*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’,—nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi—sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

**“Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.” (*****Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā*** **4.102–103)**

In your purport to the second verse, you state:

The members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement chant a minimum of sixteen rounds a day, which can be done without difficulty, and at the same time they must preach the cult of Caitanya Mahāprabhu according to the gospel of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is.* One who does so is quite fit to become a spiritual master for the entire world.

As you did in your physical presence, so through your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* you act perfectly and preach perfectly. While your spiritual master’s institution was burning in the fire of factionalism, you carried on undeterred to fulfill the desire of your guru mahārāja, because you were fixed in your determination to fulfill his order to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the West and throughout the world. You were not interested in political considerations or in gathering followers and resources for name and fame, but you were interested only in changing the hearts of the conditioned souls and relieving them of their suffering for the pleasure of your spiritual master and the Supreme Lord.

Watching these youngsters, all of us were reminded of how we had met you in the pages of your books, how we had reacted in the same way, how our consciousness had been transformed, and how we had come to dedicate our youth to your mission. You are alive and well, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Times may have changed, but the truth of your words, your powerful logic and arguments, your revelation of the futility of material existence, your entire approach to preaching and practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness have stood the test of time. Time is powerful. It is Kṛṣṇa. It can cause us to forget the fresh enthusiasm, the pure faith that poured forth from our hearts when we first contacted your sublime teachings, your wonderful personality, your complete authority. Time has even caused some to overlook these things to search elsewhere for “higher teachings.” But your teachings, your approach, your compassion, your level of organization — all are more relevant today than ever before.

The spiritual growth you engender is organic. It naturally leads to the higher realms of devotional service. Why? You are a selfless servant. This is the essence of spontaneous love for Kṛṣṇa, *vraja-bhakti.* You weren’t preoccupied with your own advancement, and you didn’t teach us to be.

In fact, Śrīla Prabhupada, you sacrificed everything, even your own reputation as a *sannyāsi* in India, to give us Westerners a chance to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. No *sannyāsi* had ever participated directly in the marriages of his disciples, nor given brahminical and *sannyāsa* initiations to Westerners. And many so-called spiritual authorities in your homeland criticized you for doing so. But you took that risk for the sake of others, for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa, and you uncompromisingly defended us even when we couldn’t behave properly. This is the seed of organic spiritual growth, the selfless service attitude, not artificial attempts at *nirjana-bhajana.*

This is why you made so many devotees so quickly. And it is why you are still making them through your purports, which are pure, transparent, and powerful.

Be merciful to us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let our pure faith not be compromised by spurious arguments about how your books were edited, nor by dwelling on mistakes we made in our immaturity, nor by arguing why we can’t initiate disciples in the face of your numerous instructions to do just that. Let us not lose our energy by fighting with one another. Let us see the evidence before us. Let us see the clear light of your example and the tenor of your instructions. And let us follow them, not sit on the sidelines and find fault with those who sincerely try to follow them.

Bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with the intelligence to surrender our false egos, that we may do more than *try* to follow your example and teachings, that we may *actually* follow them without fear. And then please mold us into your instruments to increase the numbers of pure devotees, to light up the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You are the complete devotee because you have full faith in Kṛṣṇa and His holy names. You don’t demand things of your disciples that they can’t give or do. It is up to us to follow. No one can do it for us, and no one can stop us from doing it.

You taught us how we could engage in full devotional service from wherever we were. In that way you were fully transparent to our previous *ācāryas* and to *śāstra.* Your teachings and example were neither culturally constricting nor elitist, and therefore they had the potency to create a spiritual revolution in the hearts of deeply conditioned souls, individually and collectively, from all parts of the world.

In stressing the omnipotence of the holy names, you were the ultimate *rasika ācārya* because you could touch the heart in a way that only Lord Caitanya could. Strictly following Mahāprabhu and His empowered followers, you assured us that we could see Kṛṣṇa face-to-face by offenseless *harināma-sankirtana.*

*kṛṣṇa-mantra haite habe saṁsāra-mocana*  
*kṛṣṇa-nāma haite pābe kṛṣṇera caraṇa*

**“Simply by chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa one can obtain freedom from material existence. Indeed, simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra one will be able to see the lotus feet of the Lord.”**

In your purport to this verse, you quote your guru mahārāja:

However, when one chants the holy name of the Lord offenselessly, one can realize a transcendental position that is completely aloof from the material conception of life. Rendering service to the Lord, a devotee relates to the Supreme Personality of Godhead in one of five relationships—namely, *śānta, dāsya, sakhya, vātsalya,* or *mādhurya*—and thus he relishes transcendental bliss in that relationship. Such a relationship certainly transcends the body and mind. When one realizes that the holy name of the Lord is identical with the Supreme Person, he becomes completely eligible to chant the holy name of the Lord. Such an ecstatic chanter and dancer must be considered to have a direct relationship with the Lord. \[*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*7.73, purport\]

I beg for the empowerment to follow in your footsteps, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I beg for pure faith in you, the complete devotee.

Your eternal servant,  
Keśava Bhāratī Dāsa Goswami

## Krishna dasa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. We eternally remain indebted to you for giving the world the most precious jewel, the opportunity to develop transcendental love for the Divine Couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Forty years ago, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Your Divine Grace came to the Western shores to grace the land of America by launching Lord Caitanya’s movement. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu has said:

*bhārata-bhūmite haila manuṣya janma yāra*  
*janma sārthaka kari’ kara para-upakāra*

**“One who has taken his birth as a human being in the land of Bhārata-varṣa should make his life successful and work for the benefit of all other people.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*** **9.41)**

Forty years have passed, and Ratha-yātrās, unheard of outside Bhārata, are now a frequent sight all over the world. People from various creeds and castes in almost every town and village pull the chariots of Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā Mahārāṇī. On these occasions, Lord Mayors of every city become Lord Jagannātha’s *dāsa* when they sweep the road ahead of the *ratha* being pulled.

For over forty years now, the *mahā-mantra* had been sung in every corner of the world. To many tunes, in many concerts, by many musicians (like the late George Harrison) the *mahā-mantra* has been sung with devotion to bring about world peace. Initiated devotees and others have faithfully chanted the *mahā-mantra* at the time of death, hoping to achieve a place in Vaikuṇṭha, if not Goloka.

*harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā*

Amazingly, thanks to you, the last forty years have seen the practice of vegetarianism, mantra meditation, and yoga increase exponentially in the modern world. Your books, especially the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* have become the basis of our preaching to the educated young people and to the professional classes. The temples started by you and the standard of worship set by Your Divine Grace have attracted people from all walks of life. The practice of honoring *prasāda* has become a household practice all over the world. Your net of preaching, woven with many jewellike features, has been cast widely and is attracting generation upon generation of new devotees all over the world. In short, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are greatest savior the world has ever known. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are indeed the thirteenth *mahājana*.

Thank you for coming to the West forty years ago. Thank you for bringing with you Lord Caitanya’s movement forty years ago. Thank you for saving us and shaving us forty years ago. And thank you for giving the world Kṛṣṇa consciousness forty years ago. Those who are enjoying the remnants of your *prasāda* and leading a sincere Kṛṣṇa conscious life truly know the miracle you have given to humanity at large for all time. We are truly indebted to you, to your mercy, and to your benevolence. Simply saying thank you is not good enough, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so we hope you will be happy to hear us say that we are truly indebted to you eternally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and that we all love you in the same way that you eagerly loved us.

Your humble servant,  
Krishna Dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You strongly condemned the modern civilization, which is completely misdirected by scientific materialism, and you declared that there was an urgent need to introduce the spiritual knowledge and wisdom of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to the scientists, philosophers, and scholars, as well as the general masses of the world. The very last public lecture series you delivered, at Mumbai in 1977, was entitled “Modern Civilization Is A Total failure — The Only Alternative Is Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.” I was there among the thousands to witness you destroy all materialistic misconceptions with the sharp sword of knowledge.

In an exchange with your disciples in Fiji, you said:

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:**People are being misled into accepting ignorance as knowledge. Take, for example, Darwin’s theory: Somehow matter, which is dull and dead, has produced us, who are conscious and living. This is simply speculation based on ignorance. And yet people are accepting it as knowledge. . . . Is all this really science and philosophy? Find this verse in *Bhagavad-gītā,* thirteenth chapter; one of the phrases is *tattva-jñānārtha-darśanam*.

**Disciple:**Here it is, Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Adhyātma-jñāna-nityatvaṁ tattva-jñānārtha-darśanam/ etaj jñānam iti proktam ajñānaṁ yad ato ’nyathā*. The Lord says, “Accepting the importance of self-realization, and philosophical search for the Absolute Truth—this I declare to be knowledge. And besides this, whatever there may be is ignorance.” \[*Back to Godhead* magazine, Vol. 32, No. 6, Nov.–Dec. 1998, “Śrīla Prabhupāda Speaks Out”\]

An American scientist once commented, “Every now and again I guess somebody comes up with some little interesting idea that takes a little while for people to prove wrong. But before they do that, it’s fine and people talk about it a lot until somebody comes up with a different idea.”

In the above-cited conversation you gave your assessment of such cynical so-called scientists, Śrīla Prabhupāda: “These rascals are passing as big scientists and philosophers. This is the situation, the whole world over. So how important this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is. Just imagine.”

I was trying to further imagine the importance of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement during my recent visit to the holy forest of Naimiṣāraṇya with over three hundred ISKCON devotees. There we recited *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the very spot where Śrīla Sūta Gosvāmī had narrated it to 88,000 assembled sages. We felt that even to this day the atmosphere is surcharged with the sound of the *Bhāgavatam,* the literary incarnation of the Lord. I was contemplating the following statement a lot:

On the other hand, that literature which is full with descriptions of the transcendental glories of the name, fame, form and pastimes of the unlimited Supreme Lord is a transcendental creation meant to bring about a revolution in the impious life of a misdirected civilization. Such transcendental literatures, even though irregularly composed, are heard, sung, and accepted by purified men who are thoroughly honest. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.11\]

The sages had strategically selected Naimiṣāraṇya as a spot for performing sacrifices and reciting the *Bhāgavatam,* with the aim of minimizing the worldwide effects of Kali-yuga, the age of darkness. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, the foremost exponent of the philosophy of *bhāgavata-dharma* in his day, visited Naimiṣāraṇya in 1926 in connection with starting a new branch of the Gauḍīya Maṭha there. You have continued to work for the complete welfare of humanity at large on behalf of the sages and your guru mahārāja by translating the Vedic texts, especially the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* into English, and later by instructing your disciples to translate your books into as many languages as possible. So you have amplified the *śravaṇam-kīrtanam* of Sūta Gosvāmī and the sages of Naimiṣāraṇya by beating the *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga* of transcendental book distribution.

May persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the Age of Kali get light from *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and the *Bhāgavata Purāṇa*.

Your servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Because of your glorious, adventurous *sannyāsī* ambition to follow Lord Gaurāṅga’s order forty years ago, ISKCON was first registered in New York. In an ebullient mood you lectured in your storefront to the fortunate youth washed up on the doorstep by the waves of Kali-yuga, this age of quarrel and hypocrisy:

So *mat-karma-kṛt.* So one has to do what Kṛṣṇa desires. Just like Arjuna did. Kṛṣṇa desired that he should fight. Arjuna did not like to fight, but because Kṛṣṇa desired, he accepted to fight. This is Kṛṣṇa’s work. That we have to select.

Now, what is the work at the present moment for us, Kṛṣṇa’s work? Kṛṣṇa is not present now to dictate that “This is My work. ” Just like Arjuna was fortunate enough. He was personally present before Arjuna. Lord Kṛṣṇa was personally present, and He was directing. But that does not mean that we have no direction. We have direction. We have direction. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* you’ll find that anyone who preaches the gospel of *Bhagavad-gītā* to the people of the world, he is the most dear, the dearest person in the world to Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa accepts him, the dearest person. So therefore our duty is to preach the principles of this *Bhagavad-gītā,* to make people Kṛṣṇa conscious. People are suffering for want of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore each and every one of us should be engaged in the preaching work of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the benefit of the whole world.

Lord Caitanya, whose picture you see in the front of our this store, Lord Caitanya very nicely preached this philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness:

*yāre dekha tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

The Lord says, *āmāra ājñāya.* Because He came with this mission, to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, so He says, “Just take My order, and all of you, you become the spiritual master. ”*Guru hañā.* Anyone who teaches people Kṛṣṇa consciousness, never mind what, what he is and where he is, he should be understood the spiritual master. So Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya gives order to everyone that *āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa:* “Every country, every, I mean to say, province, you go everywhere and just preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

*yāre dekha tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

*Kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* means this *Bhagavad-gītā,* the instruction given by Kṛṣṇa. That is *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa.* And *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* is also *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam. Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is full of instructions by which one can become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Similarly, in the *Bhagavad-gītā* also, we receive the instruction how we can become Kṛṣṇa conscious. So Lord Caitanya selects these very two particular books, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* and He asks everyone, in any part of the world, to take up this matter very seriously and preach in the world. That is the direct order of Kṛṣṇa. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā* 4. 14–19, New York City, 3 August 1966\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, forty years ago I missed this lecture of yours, and I have no diary to exactly trace where I was, except that at that time I was a twenty-one-year-old art student in the UK. That year my well-meaning parents asked me what I wanted as a gift for my twenty-first birthday, which they considered an especially significant event in my life. I asked them for a rather bizarre gift — a real human skull. My parents were liberal-minded and thought I had asked for a human skull for my college drawing studies. But for me the gift was mostly symbolic. My parents, who had given me birth, would now give me a human skull as my twenty-first-birthday present. What a modern art “happening”! In other words, their gift of my birth was only a meaningless death. In this way, in my drug-filled darkness, I tried to somehow grope for some light. My well-meaning father kindly took the trouble of obtaining the skull through official medical channels and said it once belonged to an African woman. Maybe she was from Rwanda, a victim of the ongoing tribal tensions that exploded in genocide some twelve years ago.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you may be interested to know that now, by your mercy and order, I am functioning as a spiritual master an am involved in *mat-karma-kṛt,* trying to serve you by distributing your books in the French language in the central African countries of Rwanda, the Democratic Republic of Congo, and Burundi. So far, most of the almost one billion people in the fifty-four countries of “the dark continent” know virtually nothing about Lord Caitanya’s mercy. There is still so much service to be done and mercy to be won. The people there are partially Kṛṣṇa conscious through their traditional African religions, as well as through Islam and Christianity, which were delivered to them by the colonial Europeans with their Koran or Bible in one hand and their gun in the other. Our best customers are the young African students proudly boasting their mobile phones and striving to imitate the Western trends while scarcely able to eat one meal a day. Meanwhile, behind the central African political and military scenes, giant multinational corporations aided by the UN are the merciless puppeteers aiming at cornering Congo’s vast mineral wealth.

To distribute your nectarean books, ISKCON (L’Association International du Conscience du Kṛṣṇa) is now fully registered in the Congo and almost registered in Rwanda by the efforts of Bāl Nitāi Dāsa, an enthusiastic young educated preacher who read your books and met the devotees in Kampala, Uganda. This summer he has invited me to embark on an adventure for a few months, traveling by boat two thousand miles down the winding Congo River to the capital, Kinshasha, while doing Saṅkīrtana festivals in towns and villages on the way. He says there is even a tribe somewhere in the jungles that worships a Deity of Narasiṁhadeva!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I really need your special mercy this time! I am your struggling fool-*sannyāsī* trying to follow your order forty years after you registered ISKCON in New York. Only by your mercy can my mind and body stay out of mischief by performing *mat-karma-kṛt,* the essence of *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā*. Forty years ago you said in New York: “Every country, every, I mean to say, province, you go everywhere and just preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” But I can see that after forty years there are still many countries where your followers have never yet gone! So this opportunity for me to preach in central Africa must surely be your special mercy on me and devotees who want to help.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me this chance to stop this repetition of skull-like birth and death and soon go back to Godhead, to meet you again.

Your fallen servant,  
Maha-viṣṇu Swami

## Nava Yogendra Swami

My most dear Gurudeva Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my billions and billions of humble obeisances unto the dust of your lotuslike feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are very merciful unto the fallen souls, like myself!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your divine appearance day. It is such a divine arrangement of Śrī Kṛṣṇa that yesterday the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, appeared and today is the appearance day of your good self, who can tell us that Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the only God and everybody else is His servant. *Ekale īśvara kṛṣṇa, āra saba bhṛtya:* We are all servants of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, but because of our material contamination we are forgetful of this. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with reasoning and logic based on the revealed scripture, explain to us that Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the only God. And the fortunate souls around the world who accepted this became devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, without approaching the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we cannot get out of this *saṁsāra*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can I repay my debt to you? It is not possible to repay this debt. You utter this from your lotus mouth — that the disciple cannot repay his debt to the spiritual master.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the only prayer I humbly direct unto your lotus feet is that you, out of your causeless mercy, please purify this servant of yours.

*ohe!*  
*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura, doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse karuṇā kori’*  
*diyā pada-chāyā, śodho he āmāya,*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*

*chaya vega domi’, chaya doṣa śodhi’,*  
*chaya guṇa deho’ dāse*  
*chaya sat-saṅga, deho’ he āmāre*  
*bosechi saṅgera āśe*

*ekākīāmāra, nāhi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kori’, śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho’ kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*

*kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro*  
*tomāra śakati āche*  
*āmi to kāṅgāla, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli’,*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

O venerable Vaiṣṇava, O ocean of mercy, be merciful unto your servant. Give me the shade of your lotus feet and purify me. I hold on to your lotus feet.

Teach me to control my six passions, rectify my six faults, bestow upon me the six good qualities, and offer unto me the six kinds of holy association.

I do not find the strength to carry on alone the *saṅkīrtana* of the holy name of Hari. Please bless me by giving me just one drop of faith with which to obtain the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me. I am simply running behind you shouting, “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!”

I hope, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you very kindly and mercifully consider my prayers and purify me so that I can become a worthy servant of yours right now. I am hopeless because of so many defects and faults.

*āmāra jīvana, sadā pāpe rata,*  
*nāhiko puṇyera leṣa*  
*parere udvega, diyāchi ye koto,*  
*diyāchi jīvere kleśa*

*nija sukha lāgi’, pāpe nāhi ḍori,*  
*dayā-hīna svārtha-paro*  
*para-sukhe duḥkhī, sadā mithya-bhāṣī,*  
*para-duḥkha sukha-karo*

*āśeṣa kāmanā, hṛdi mājhe mora,*  
*krodhī, dambha-parāyaṇa*  
*mada-matta sadā, viṣaye mohita,*  
*hiṁsā-garva vibhūṣaṇa*

*nidrālasya hata, sukārye virata,*  
*akārye udyogīāmi*  
*pratiṣṭha lāgiyā, śāṭhya-ācaraṇa,*  
*lobha-hata sadā kāmī*

*e heno durjana, saj-jana-varjita,*  
*aparādhi nirantara*  
*śubha-kārya-śūnya, sadānartha-manāḥ,*  
*nānā duḥkhe jara jara*

*bārdhakye ekhona, upāya-vihīna,*  
*tāte dīna akiñcana*  
*bhakativinoda, prabhura caraṇe,*  
*kore duḥkha nivedana*

My life is ever given to sin; in it there is not a particle of good. I have caused others great anxiety, and have troubled all souls.

For the sake of my own enjoyment I have never hesitated to perform sinful acts. Devoid of all compassion, I am concerned only with my selfish interests. Perpetually speaking lies, I become dejected upon seeing others happy, whereas the misery of others is a source of great delight for me.

There are limitless material desires within the core of my heart. I am wrathful, fond of exhibiting arrogance, intoxicated by vanity, and bewildered by worldly affairs. I wear the cherished ornaments of envy and egotism.

Ruined by laziness and sleep, I resist all pious deeds, yet am very enthusiastic to perform wicked acts. For the sake of worldly fame and reputation I engage in the practice of deceitfulness. I am victimized by my own greed, being always lustful.

A vile, wicked man such as this, rejected by godly people, is a constant offender. Devoid of all good works, forever inclined toward evil, he is worn out and wasted by various miseries.

Now in old age, deprived of all means of relief, thus humbled and poor, Bhaktivinoda submits his tale of grief at the feet of the Supreme Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly purify me before it gets too late. Already so many of my godbrothers have left their bodies, and I am in the que. You are my only savior; kindly save me from this horrible world.

Thank you for considering my prayers.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are saving the whole world by spreading *sanātana-dharma* and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name all over the world!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Nava Yogendra Swami

## Pārtha Sārathi Dāsa Goswami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

This year I sign my name as “Goswami” and not just the usual “Dāsa.” Also, my offering is included in the official BBT Vyāsa-pūjā book. Some would say that I’m progressing — going forward. After thirty-three years as a struggling *brahmacārī*, now I am a struggling *sannyāsī*.

Real progress in a disciple’s spiritual life can be measured not outwardly but internally. Am I increasing my attachment to you, my beloved spiritual master? The more attached I become to you, the more advanced I become. First there is some *laukika śraddhā* or sentimental faith in you. Gradually it becomes more *niṣṭhā,* or firm faith in you. Then *āsakti,* or real attachment to you, and then genuine love for you.

In the hierarchy of love toward Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, it starts with you, then *paraṁ guru,* then the previous *ācāryas,* then Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Nityānanda, and finally Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

This attachment to you is not just to you personally but to your instructions, your mission (ISKCON), and your faithful followers.

According to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, the *svarūpa-lakṣaṇa,* or primary symptom of *rāga,* is *gāḍha-tṛṣṇā,* or deep attachment for the Lord. The *taṭastha-lakṣaṇa,* or secondary symptom of *rāga,* is *āviṣṭatā,* or deep absorption in the Lord. So first attachment, then absorption.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this progression can also be applied to you. First comes deep attachment to you and your instructions and mission (ISKCON). Then, from that attachment comes deep absorption in you, your instructions, and your mission (ISKCON).

Every year I look forward to reading your Vyāsa-pūjā book, especially the offerings by your GBC members and *sannyāsīs*. Your ISKCON is an amazing phenomenon. There are so many diverse and different personalities involved in it. Some are seemingly diametrically opposed to each other, some introverted, and others highly extroverted. Some are conservative and others liberal. Some are steeped deeply in the rules and regulations, and others tend to be more spontaneous.

Yet there is an undying common factor bonding all these great souls together — that they are all sold out to you. The theme that runs through their lives, or rather their very existence, is their unadulterated love for Your Divine Grace.

As expressed by Śrīla Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī in his prayers to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī:

He is my life and soul. Again and again he kindly splashed me with the fragrant waters of the shoreless ocean of limitless love. Now misfortune forces me to be swallowed up by the forest fire of material calamities. I have no shelter. Except for him, of whom can I take shelter. \[*Śrī Prārthanāśraya-caturdaśaka* 10\]

When I read the various offerings glorifying Your Divine Grace, I feel proud to have such Vaiṣṇavas as friends and superiors. Being a little reclusive by nature, many of them I have never met, but by reading their realizations of what you mean to them, I become emotional.

I pray with all the humility at my command that you continue to allow me to associate with your followers. In your physical absence I have no other shelter than your sincere disciples and granddisciples.

Begging for a single drop of mercy from your divine lotus feet, I remain,

Your servant,  
Pārtha Sārathi Dāsa Goswami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Śrīla Prabhupāda created many programs to engage devotees in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to spread the mission of Lord Caitanya. He stressed certain programs, such as distributing his transcendental literature, establishing *gurukulas,* and developing farm communities with cow protection — all meant to bring about a cultural revolution to convince people that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Śrīla Prabhupāda often mentioned that if we could not expand the programs he began, at least we should try to maintain them.

And Śrīla Prabhupāda had very good reasons to push on these programs. Developing self-sufficient farms would provide an economic basis by which householders could raise their families free from the degrading influence of modern cities. Establishing *gurukulas,* as Prahlāda Mahārāja has advised, would give children a chance to become educated in how to perform divine austerities from the beginning of their lives and attain Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And transcendental book distribution would keep the devotees in the cities on the transcendental platform, unaffected by material contamination. In this way, along with other spiritualized activities such as dance, art, music, and theater, a cultural revolution would take place to enable people’s dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness — the only real asset anyone has in this world — to fully awaken in their hearts. Such are the timeless words and instructions of *ācāryas* like Śrī Prahlāda Mahārāja and Śrīla Prabhupāda. Now it is our duty to think deeply how to satisfy them by carrying out these instructions.

There is an intimate connection between our service and our Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If we perform our service according to the orders of our previous *ācāryas* and take guidance from Śrīla Prabhupāda with the sole intention of pleasing Lord Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotees, then the result will be that we will naturally think of Lord Kṛṣṇa always. This, Śrīla Prabhupāda taught, is perfect Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Practical programs such as transcendental book distribution certainly help our patience and enthusiasm to flourish. Conversely, without the growth of practical service, the execution of our daily *sādhana —* of attending classes, reading books, chanting at least sixteen rounds of *japa* daily, and preaching — will become increasingly difficult to maintain because all these practical activities and services are food for our devotional creepers.

Surely, developing and implementing such programs as *gurukula* and even farm communities with cow protection is a challenge in present-day ISKCON. Still, if its leaders and devotees in general understand how essential these programs are for both our personal success and that of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness mission, then, with enthusiastic support and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s blessings, these programs will develop and, perhaps sooner than later, flourish in glorification of Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Puruṣatraya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Oh, Prabhupāda, my dear master, all glories to you!

This year we are celebrating the one hundred and tenth anniversary of your appearance in this world, and also the fortieth anniversary of the founding of your movement. It is amazing how your mission has spread and become established in so many places in the world! Even here, in this remote and immense country of Brazil, we see that your mission is expanding in a solid and mature way. Kṛṣṇa’s holy names are heard more and more, and an ever-increasing number of people are taking the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously. In my constant preaching trips, I always meet groups of serious and enthusiastic devotees fully dedicated to continuing and expanding your mission. This is proof that your mercy is present and always acting, since who would dedicate himself to a cause if he were not inspired? Oh, Prabhupāda, you are the inspiration of our lives.

Your mercy is being disseminated all over the world! Because it is a spiritual substance, the more it is distributed, the more the reservoir is filled, and it never becomes exhausted. In fact, it always increases its capacity and is always overflowing. This overflowing mercy of yours is the very vital energy of the devotees. Oh, Prabhupāda, allow me help disseminate Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, which flows through you.

In a purport in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.9.35), you write:

A pure devotee of the Lord always desires to expand the number of devotees of the Lord in various ways. Such expansions of transcendental service may appear to be material to the materialist, but factually they are expansions of the causeless mercy of the Lord towards the devotee. Plans for such activities may appear to be material activities, but they are different in potency, being engaged in the satisfaction of the transcendental senses of the Supreme.

In this quote you state clearly that your desire is to increase the number of devotees in your mission. It is your desire that the largest number of people recognize the presence of Kṛṣṇa within their hearts. This is, in truth, the most magnanimous and genuine desire. I humbly make your desire my very own. In fact, I pray to Kṛṣṇa that this desire become my permanent propriety.

Puruṣatraya Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Today, on your auspicious appearance day, I would like to glorify you in two ways: First as the representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and second as the great personality who stepped into my life with a multitude of lovable qualities.

**The Guru-Tattva**

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī describes the spiritual master as Kṛṣṇa Himself, coming to save the fallen souls:

*yadyapi āmāra guru—caitanyera dāsa*  
*tathāpi jāniye āmi tāṅhāra prakāśa*

**Although I know that my spiritual master is a servitor of Śrī Caitanya, I know Him also as a plenary manifestation of the Lord.**

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*****1.44–45\]**

You have explained these wonderful verses in your purports to the texts. The guru is one — Kṛṣṇa.

In the month of February, in the year 1936, you gave a very notable address during the Vyāsa-pūjā celebration of your own spiritual master, where you dealt with this point. I quote you:

Gentlemen, the offering of such an homage as has been arranged this evening to the *ācāryadeva* is not a sectarian concern, for when we speak of the fundamental principle of *gurudeva,* or *ācāryadeva,* we speak of something that is of universal application. There does not arise any question of discriminating my guru from yours or anyone else’s. There is only one guru, who appears in an infinity of forms to teach you, me, and all others.

Just as the Lord is one although He appears in so many manifestations, the guru is also one in *tattva* — the *guru-tattva,* which is Kṛṣṇa or Lord Nityānanda Rāma, the first manifestation or expansion of Kṛṣṇa, the original spiritual master.

We can see this inconceivable nature of the *guru-tattva* when the Supreme Lord receives Gopa-kumāra in the Vaikuṇṭha worlds. The Lord says:

For so long I have been eager to see you! My dear friend, you have passed many lifetimes without paying any attention to Me at all. For so long, hope had Me dancing like a fool, thinking, “Perhaps in this lifetime, or this, or this, or this, he will finally turn his face toward me.” But I could find no pretext on which to bring you to my abode, dear brother, and still follow the timeless laws that I Myself have created. You showed me no mercy. \[*Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* 2.4.81–85\]

Then the Lord tells Gopa-kumāra that He made an arrangement to bring him back home without transgressing His own law that He would not interfere in the living entity’s freedom: “Dear boy, in that divine district of Govardhana, My most beloved abode, I Myself became your guru, known by the name Jayanta.” (*Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* 2.4.86)

When Kṛṣṇa later receives another soul, Janaśarma, He paints almost the same picture: An anxious God who wants everyone to come back and who becomes active to achieve that end. (See *Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* 2.7.38–42)

Whenever I think about this Kṛṣṇa aspect of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I become mesmerized, like an ant looking at the Himalayan Mountains. By Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s inconceivable mercy you have come into my life and personally saved me. You have prayed for me, you have worried for me, and you are still present. Like a loving father who sits by His feverish child, comforting and also encouraging him to become healthy again, you stay by the side of this insignificant prisoner of this world.

And you are leading the way in my ongoing journey. It is your footsteps that guide me through the territory of *bhakti*. Your footsteps that guide me through the *dhāma*. Your footsteps that guide me through the vast territory of *sādhana*. And it will be your footsteps that will guide me through the final examination, my own physical death.

**Three Pictures — Three Million Words**

In part two of my homage to you today, I would like to glorify your very special personality by looking at three pictures of you. There are a thousand pictures in my heart – or more — but today I will look at three.

The picture on my personal altar shows you looking like a trusting, loving child — looking upward in the direction of your Lord. You seem to see Him — His light fills you. Your face looks like the happy face of a pilgrim who, after a strenuous journey, has reached his destination.

I love this picture. When it was lost, I searched for it desperately until I found it, surprisingly, in a dusty little village in Vṛndāvana.

Another picture: You have a pair of *karatālas* in your hands. Your eyes are closed, your demeanor is ecstatic. Your body swings from left to right. It is clear: you are tossed by waves of love.

After I looked at that picture exclusively for one month, I thought, “Oh, you are so far away, fully engaged in your own world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You seem to say: ‘If you wish to find me, look for me in the land of ecstasy.’ Unfortunately, I still don’t have a visa for this land. Looking at you so far away from my miserable situation, I asked myself, Will I ever meet you again? And, Do you still find time to look in the direction of this fallen soul of this world?

My third picture is one I posted in front of my office table twenty-five years ago. Now it is in my heart. On this picture you sit upright on the floor. You are in Dallas, in the *gurukula*. There is a stick in your hand, a stick that a teacher uses. You seem to be teaching discipline to the young, restless boys. Your eyes and face show determination and strength. That picture is also of great importance to me. For me it is of therapeutic necessity to look at it. It sobers me — drives all the nonsense out of the heart.

And surprisingly, the picture answered the question that had arisen from contemplating the second picture. Yes, you still look in my direction. And you talk. Your words cut the attachments of the material mind.

Lately I found this picture in a stack of old photographs of you. As I looked very closely, I saw something I had never seen before. Underneath your intense demeanor I could see a very merciful smile, which lit up the room and spread its influence like the rays of the rising sun.

On this auspicious day I beg you to kindly always shower your mercy on me. You are the visible sign of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy on me. Without you, I am nothing. It is your mercy that awakens this hopeless mercy-case of yours to live.

I wish that your glorious mission will become successful all over the universe, and I beg you to accept my tiny service in that mission eternally.

Your humble servant  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Smita Krishna Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As I write this I am at your farm in Sweden, Almviks Gard, New Śrī Chaitanya Chandradoya Mandir. It is cold here just now, but there is some spirit of devotion that keeps us warm. In our small gathering for *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class in the mornings, we are reading from the Fourth Canto about King Purañjana. One aspect that attracts me in that story is the mention in the beginning of Avijñāta, the unknown friend of the king. That friend will come back at the end of the story. There is a friend and he is unknown, and that is true for all of us. We all have an unknown friend.

We could say that we know that friend, but do we? Even Lord Brahmā said, “Others” may say that they know You, my dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, but as far as I am concerned I do not know you.” So for me it is a little like that in relation to you. It is certainly an honor for me to to try to write something in your honor for this sacred day of your apperance. But do I know you to honor you? As a person, I might not know you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Who were you and who are you on all levels? Who knows? So I start with not knowing you — that is my knowledge of you. You are great, but how great? What can my tiny intellect understand of your greatness?

So, then, what do I know? That it is by your mercy that I know anything at all worth knowing about Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa conscioussness. It is by your mercy that I can worship Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva here at Almviks Gard. It is by your mercy that I can give any lecture at all about Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You dared to do what others hesitated to do. You dared to cross the borders of India to preach in the *mleccha* countries. You dared to adjust things so that women could live in the temples in the West. Thus you spread the mission far and wide — that is your mercy.

Then, I have heard that you are still not far away, the unknown friend supervising your followers. You are my unknown friend who I have heard about but who I do not fully know or understand. That friend by whose mercy this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is still going on the world over. And by your mercy, I am here. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I try my best. Please have patience. It is not always easy, but the knowledge that there is an unknown friend makes it easier.

I beg to remain your attempting servant,  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am considering that every day that passes means one less day before I am again in your personal presence. Perhaps I am being arrogant in thinking thus, but I have this confidence. What concerns me is whether you will be pleased to welcome me into your association or not?

When we had your physical presence, you always managed to inspire us to give our very best in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. I can not honestly say that I have continued my service with the same level of intensity in your absence. Now that my time left here is growing short, I am hopeful I can regain that same sense of urgency your personal presence commanded. Please bless me that this is not just a “hope against hope.”

It is very inspiring to see how many of your disciples have been able to maintain there unwavering commitment to your mission. It is clear that you are pleased with them. Please bless me to follow in their footsteps so that I will not be an embarrassment to you, so that you will be truly happy to receive me into your circle of dedicated followers.

Your insignificant servant  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

**The Way Forward**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“It is easy to become Kṛṣṇa conscious,”Śrīla Prabhupāda said on a morning walk. “What is difficult is to become determined.”

I was on that morning walk, and these words keep coming back to me. As time goes by, it seems, the obstacles on the path of devotional service get bigger, and determination takes on added importance.

“How can one become determined?” someone asked.

“By austerity,” said Śrīla Prabhupāda. “That is why we have the regulative principles.”

Even before I came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I had come across the regulative principles in my readings, but it was only by hearing the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda that I was ever able to follow them and start living the life of a human being. Certainly, without the regulative principles, there can be no real determination to attain the perfection of human life.

Still, when the going gets tough, I sometimes need an extra boost in my determination. What do you do when the path forward is suddenly blocked by a brick wall? There is no turning back because there is nothing to go back to. There is no way to limp home like a wounded soldier because there is no home to limp back to. Home lies ahead, on the other side of the wall.

When I need this extra determination, I know where to go. I know the source of determination and of everything else that is good in my life: Śrīla Prabhupāda.

So I meditate on Śrīla Prabhupāda’s determination. I think about how nothing in the world could stop him, how nothing could make him retreat, not even a heart attack at sea.

I am now about the age Śrīla Prabhupāda was when he started his travels, but I travel on nice airplanes with my handy credit card. Śrīla Prabhupāda traveled on a slow ship with only a few rupees. I have devotees to greet me or travel with me wherever I go, but Śrīla Prabhupāda had no one.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s obstacles were far greater than mine, but he hardly seemed to notice them. What was his secret?

“Of course,” says Śrīla Prabhupāda, “when I came to your country without any friend, without any means . . . Practically, just like a vagabond I came. But I had full faith that ‘My guru mahārāja is with me.’ I never lost this faith, and that is fact.”

But I am a fallen soul. Where can someone like me find this kind of faith? Well, I go to the same source. I think about Śrīla Prabhupāda’s faith in his own spiritual master, and this gives me the strength and the determination needed to shatter the brick wall and go forward on the path of devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is whatever I have that is good and all I will ever need. He is my eyes and ears, my knowledge, my ability, my faith, and my determination to push homeward.

“I wish that all those calamities would happen again and again so that we could see You again and again, for seeing You means that we will no longer see repeated births and deaths.” (Queen Kuntī to Lord Kṛṣṇa, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.8.25)

I, of course, cannot directly see Lord Kṛṣṇa face to face, I cannot directly see the way forward, but my spiritual master can, and he is my eyes. Let me pray, then, that the obstacles of today and those to come may always turn my mind to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda. As long as I can remember Śrīla Prabhupāda, even death will not be an obstacle.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

As I contemplate the prophecy of Śrīla Vṛndāvana Dāsa Ṭhākura telling how “Those who are fortunate can see the pastimes of Lord Caitanya transpiring even today,” I can see how this verse is being fulfilled in and through you, now and forevermore. Ever since you appeared like the rising sun to dispel the illusion of a darkened world, the collection of memories and appreciations of you has continued to unfold like new chapters of Śrī Caitanya’s *līlā*. These ever-expanding appreciations are just like a multitude of streams and rivers that pour water into their common source and destination, the nectarean ocean of your remembrances. They testify to the countless manners in which you have touched peoples’ lives and transformed their hearts.

It is reminiscent of Lord Caitanya glorifying the unlimited compassion and sacrificing spirit of Śrīla Vāsudeva Datta, as if He possessed a thousand mouths. When the thousands of devotees sing your glories, it is Lord Caitanya’s way of praising you, through their voices. The analogy goes deeper. Lord Caitanya told Śrīla Vāsudeva Datta that he would not have to suffer endless hells to achieve his aspiration of the world becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious. Since it was his desire, Kṛṣṇa was sure to fulfill it. Similarly, so many times we have heard of a particular desire you expressed which later, often after your disappearance, was fulfilled by divine arrangement. Śrī Kṛṣṇa makes it unmistakably clear that your desires are so pure and compassionate that He takes it upon Himself to make the arrangements to fulfill them. As you, the pure devotee, have no desire other than to expand the glories of the Lord, He too has no greater desire than to glorify His beloved agent. And in so doing, His own glories are enhanced by the love He displays for His servitor.

Seeing the Lord’s pastimes even today implies realizing them, through faith, in hearts illumined by hearing from Śrī Guru. In contrast to the time factor, which diminishes the life of everything in this world, Śrī Kṛṣṇa lovingly echoes and amplifies your glories more as the years roll by.

As our time in this world approaches its close, your legacy sustains our spiritual lives, opening the doors to eternal life with you. Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s prediction, through Śrīla Vṛndāvana Dāsa Ṭhākura’s words, will ring even more true as time flows on. Those who are fortunate will be able to perceive this.

Your servant,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

On this auspicious day of your appearance, I offer my humble obeisances at your lotus feet because I am really trying to take shelter of them with proper understanding. I have been given the opportunity to serve you in the form of teaching chapters five through twelve of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* Third Canto in the MIHE Bhakti-vaibhava course. These chapters describe the Lord’s creative activities in the *mahat-tattva* for the purpose of differentiating the varieties of manifestations of His energies from subtle to gross. When the demigods took charge of their different departments of control, they could not progress in their service. Finally, they realized that they could not do their functions unless the Lord cast His merciful glance over the subtle manifestation until the gross manifestations were formed. Therefore the demigods took shelter of His two lotus feet and glorified them with a sequence of the choicest verses.

This passage really inspired me to understand that, just as the Lord, with His merciful glance, enables the demigods to function on His behalf, so Your Divine Grace, being the transparent medium to convey Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s same transcendental mercy-glance, have enabled me to perform all kinds of services in Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the gross manifestation of the ISKCON institution. Therefore, without taking shelter of your glorious lotus feet and remaining in the gross manifestation of ISKCON, how would it be possible to render any service that would allow me to transcend the material modes? Because your mercy is nondifferent from the Lord’s, I have every hope of overcoming any obstacles that may come from myself or outside. Therefore, I earnestly pray at your lotus feet, *nāṭheliha rāṅgā-pāya*. Please do not allow me to be kicked out.

Your servant,  
Vedavyāsa-priya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*Jaya oṁ viṣṇupāda paramahaṁsa parivrājakācārya 108 śrī-śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda kī jaya!*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. May we aspire for the dust of your lotus feet life after life, just as the *ācāryas* aspire for the dust of the lotus feet of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī.

*ādadānas tṛṇaṁ dantair idaṁ yāce punaḥ punaḥ*  
*śrīmad rūpa-padāmbhoja- dhūliḥ syāṁ janma-janmani*

**“Taking a blade of grass between my teeth I fall down and again and again pray that I may become the dust at the feet of Śrīmad Rūpa birth after birth.” (Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī in his** ***Muktā-carita*****)**

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥśrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master. ” (*****Śrī Gurv-aṣṭakam*** **8)**

Indeed, there is no real thought or meditation as auspicious as that of your lotus feet. Your lotus feet gave the most cooling shelter to our materially scorched hearts. Your presence gave us such a cooling shelter that in your physical absence, even twenty-eight years after your disappearance, we feel hopeless and morose. *Ye ānilo prema-dhana koruṇā pracur heno prabhu kothā gelā prabhupāda ṭhākura*. *Pāṣāṇe kuṭibo māthā anale paśibo gaurāṅga guṇera nidhi kothā gele pābo*. We feel like hitting our heads with stones or entering fire, since where can we find a person like you, who are the reservoir of all the qualities of Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu? There is no greater pain than that of your separation. Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 8.248):

*‘duḥkha-madhye kona duḥkha haya gurutara?’*  
*‘kṛṣṇa-bhakta-viraha vinā duḥkha nāhi dekhi para’*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked, “Of all kinds of distress, what is the most painful?”**

**Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “Apart from separation from the devotee of Kṛṣṇa, I know of no unbearable unhappiness. ”**

Rāmānanda Rāya also said that a pure devotee is the most famous person in the world. As the founder-*ācārya* of Lord Caitanya’s world *saṅkīrtana* movement, you are the most famous person and will remain so for at least ten thousand years.

*‘kīrti-gaṇa-madhye jīvera kon baḍa kīrti?’*  
*‘kṛṣṇa-bhakta baliyā yāṅhāra haya khyāti’*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu then asked Rāmānanda Rāya, “Out of all glorious activities, which is the most glorious?”**

**Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “That person who is reputed to be a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa enjoys the utmost fame and glory.” (Cc** ***Madhya*** **8.246)**

As we read the scriptures and hear about the qualities of the topmost saintly persons, we feel that they have been described keeping you in mind.

The Lord asked who the most educated person is, and Rāya Rāmānanda said that one who is most conversant in the science of Kṛṣṇa is the most learned. That is why you have the unique title Bhaktivedanta — ultimate in knowledge and devotion.

On one occasion the Lord inquired, “Of all types of education, which is the most important?” Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “No education is important other than the transcendental devotional service of Kṛṣṇa.” (Cc *Madhya* 8.245)

And you are the richest person. Therefore you told the Americans, “I have not come to beg anything from you. I have come to give you something.” You are the greatest capitalist, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*‘sampattira madhye jīvera kon sampatti gaṇi?’*  
*‘rādhā-kṛṣṇe prema yāṅra, sei baḍa dhanī’*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked, “Of the many capitalists who possess great riches, who is the topmost?”**

**Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “He who is richest in love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa is the greatest capitalist.” (Cc** ***Madhya*** **8.247)**

*Kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāra, tomāra śakati āche*. *Rādhā-kṛṣṇa prāṇa mora yugala-kiśora jīvane maraṇe gati āro nāhi mora*. Your life’s wealth was Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Yugala-kiśora.

You are the chief distributor of the fruits of love of Godhead. Lord Caitanya said each fruit of love of God is so precious that all the wealth in the world can’t buy it.

*tri-jagate yata āche dhana-ratnamaṇi*  
*eka-phalera mūlya kari’ tāhā nāhi gaṇi*

**“All the wealth in the three worlds cannot equal the value of one such nectarean fruit of devotional service.” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.28)**

This chapter of *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* — the ninth chapter of the *Ādi-līlā* — so nicely describes your mission.

*pākila ye prema-phala amṛta-madhura*  
*vilāya caitanya-mālī, nāhi laya mūla*

**“The fruits ripened and became sweet and nectarean. The gardener, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, distributed them without asking any price.” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.27)**

*māge vā nā māge keha, pātra vā apātra*  
*ihāra vicāra nāhi jāne, deya mātra*

**“Not considering who asked for it and who did not, nor who was fit and who unfit to receive it, Caitanya Mahāprabhu distributed the fruit of devotional service.” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.29)**

*añjali añjali bhari’ phele caturdiśe*  
*daridra kuḍāñā khāya, mālākāra hāse*

**“The transcendental gardener, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, distributed handful after handful of fruit in all directions, and when the poor, hungry people ate the fruit, the gardener smiled with great pleasure. (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.30)**

*mālākāra kahe,—śuna, vṛkṣa-parivāra*  
*mūlaśākhā-upaśākhā yateka prakāra*

**“Lord Caitanya thus addressed the multifarious varieties of branches and subbranches of the tree of devotional service:” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.31)**

*alaukika vṛkṣa kare sarvendriya-karma*  
*sthāvara ha-iyā dhare jaṅgamera dharma*

**“Since the tree of devotional service is transcendental, every one of its parts can perform the action of all the others. Although a tree is supposed to be immovable, this tree nevertheless moves.” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.32)**

*e vṛkṣera aṅga haya saba sa-cetana*  
*bāḍiyā vyāpila sabe sakala bhuvana*

**“All the parts of this tree are spiritually cognizant, and thus as they grow they spread all over the world.’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.33)**

*ekalā mālākāra āmi kāhāṅ kāhāṅ yāba*  
*ekalā vā kata phala pāḍiyā vilāba*

**“I am the only gardener. How many places can I go? How many fruits can I pick and distribute?’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.34)**

*ekalā uṭhāñā dite haya pariśrama*  
*keha pāya, keha nā pāya, rahe mane bhrama*

**“It would certainly be a very laborious task to pick the fruits and distribute them alone, and still I suspect that some would receive them and others would not.’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.35)**

*ataeva āmi ājñā diluṅ sabākāre*  
*yāhāṅ tāhāṅ prema-phala deha’ yāre tāre*

**“Therefore I order every man within this universe to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and distribute it everywhere.’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.36)**

*ekalā mālākāra āmi kata phala khāba*  
*nā diyā vā ei phala āra ki kariba*

**“I am the only gardener. If I do not distribute these fruits, what shall I do with them? How many fruits can I alone eat?’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.37)**

*ātma-icchāmṛte vṛkṣa siñci nirantara*  
*tāhāte asaṅkhya phala vṛkṣera upara*

**“By the transcendental desire of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, water has been sprinkled all over the tree, and thus there are innumerable fruits of love of Godhead.’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.38)**

*ataeva saba phala deha’ yāre tāre*  
*khāiyā ha-uk loka ajara amare*

**“Distribute this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Let people eat these fruits and ultimately become free from old age and death.’” (Cc** ***Ādi*** **9.39)**

So this is the order of the Supreme Lord: to distribute this fruits of love of God. And you so seriously took up this instruction. That is why you are the most sincere follower of the Lord and your spiritual master. And that is why you are empowered by the Lord to distribute this mercy. This power is a very confidential and special power, which is possessed only by the Lord Himself or His very dear servant.

*kali-kālera dharma — kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement.” (Cc** ***Antya*** **7.11)**

Simply by understanding your activities and then reading the scriptures, we can easily see that everything said in the scriptures has been brought to reality by your personality.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (12.13–14) Kṛṣṇa Himself describes the qualities of a pure devotee: “One who is not envious but is a kind friend to all living entities, who does not think himself a proprietor and is free from false ego, who is equal in both happiness and distress, who is tolerant, always satisfied, self-controlled, and engaged in devotional service with determination, his mind and intelligence fixed on Me—such a devotee of Mine is very dear to Me.”

When we remember your transcendental pastimes, we realize how much you embodied these qualities. In 1965, when you had to stay with Sally and Gopal Agarwal, who had no idea of how to be with a *paramahaṁsa sannyāsī,* you immediately won their heart by being a perfect guest. In the same way, even though you didn’t agree with Dr. Mishra’s impersonal philosophy, you cooked nice prasādam for him and won his heart. No one could understand the American youth of 1966. Neither their parents nor the educators nor the priests could impress these young American hippies. But you somehow charmed them, won their hearts and their confidence, and without their knowledge transformed their lives completely. You tolerated all their ignorance and bad habits, just like a loving mother tolerating her baby’s stool and urine. Because you had no personal likes or dislikes, you were always tolerant, satisfied, and self-controlled. Your only goal was to please Kṛṣṇa and benefit all the fallen living entities.

Krsna continues describing the qualities of the pure devotee (Bg 12.15–16): “He by whom no one is put into difficulty and who is not disturbed by anyone, who is equipoised in happiness and distress, fear and anxiety, is very dear to Me. My devotee who is not dependent on the ordinary course of activities, who is pure, expert, without cares, free from all pains, and not striving for some result, is very dear to Me.”

You never depended on the ordinary course of activities. You didn’t try to manipulate or materially manage anything. You just prayed to Kṛṣṇa and engaged in the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and all the results came. Because of your total surrender, the Lord arranged everything so nicely for you to spread this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.18.17) Lord Kṛṣṇa describes to Uddhava the qualities of a real *sannyāsī:*

*maunānīhānilāyāmā daṇḍā vāg-deha-cetasām*  
*na hy ete yasya santy aṅga veṇubhir na bhaved yatiḥ*

**“One who has not accepted the three internal disciplines of avoiding useless speech, avoiding useless activities and controlling the life air can never be considered a** ***sannyāsī*** **merely because of his carrying bamboo rods.”**

*Tridaṇḍi-sannyāsa* means *daṇḍā vāg-deha-cetasām,* controlling the body, mind, and speech. Indeed, there is no example in history of anyone who has done this as perfectly as you. That is why you have made disciples all over the world. As Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī says in his *Upadeśāmṛta,* only one who has controlled the six urges of the senses can make disciples throughout the whole world.

Kṛṣṇa continues describing the ideal *sannyāsī* in text 20:

*ekaś caren mahīm etāṁ niḥsaṅgaḥ saṁyatendriyaḥ*  
*ātma-krīḍa ātma-rata ātma-vān sama-darśanaḥ*

**“Without any material attachment, with senses fully controlled, remaining enthusiastic, and satisfied in realization of the Supreme Lord and his own self, the saintly person should travel about the earth alone. Having equal vision everywhere, he should be steady on the spiritual platform.”**

So you traveled the earth alone and exemplified this verse too. In Vālmīki’s *Rāmāyaṇa* we read that when Hanumānjī went to Laṅkā looking for mother Sītā, he didn’t know where she might be. He thought Rāvaṇa must have imprisoned her in his palace. So at night he inspected the inner chambers of Rāvaṇa’s palace and saw hundreds of beautiful women sleeping. He asked himself why his mind was not distracted by seeing women sleeping in an exposed condition. He said, “Because I am doing this for my service to ŚrīRāma, I am not agitated by lust.” In the same way, you were never, ever in duality. You are a *mahā-bhāgavata*. You simply saw Kṛṣṇa wherever you went.

*mahā-bhāgavata dekhe sthāvara-jaṅgama*  
*tāhāṅ tāhāṅ haya tāṅra śrī-kṛṣṇa-sphuraṇa*

**“A devotee advanced on the spiritual platform sees everything movable and inert as the Supreme Lord. For him, everything he sees here and there is but a manifestation of Lord Kṛṣṇa.” (Cc** ***Madhya*** **8.273)**

*sthāvara-jaṅgama dekhe, nā dekhe tāra mūrti*  
*sarvatra haya nija iṣṭa-deva-sphūrti*

**“The** ***mahā-bhāgavata,*** **the advanced devotee, certainly sees everything mobile and immobile, but he does not exactly see their forms. Rather, everywhere he immediately sees manifest the form of the Supreme Lord.”**

Simply remembering you drives away all dirty things from the heart, so we pray that we always remember you and in that way remain free from the terrible influence of *māyā*.

Thank you million times for saving us. Please always keep us in the shade of your lotus feet and protect us.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Ahmedabad, India.

## Aravade

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept millions of our obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are your granddisciples and so never saw you in your physical form, but we feel your presence in your disciples, in your ISKCON temples, in your books, etc., and your presence in these things helps us see you closely and associate with you in various forms.

Here in Aravade, fifteen devotees are doing service for your pleasure. We are doing preaching activities such as weekly programs in nearby places, youth preaching, *Bhagavad-gītā* courses, *padayātrās,* big annual festivals like Janmāṣṭamī, Rāmanavamī, Gaura-pūrṇimā, etc.

Only your blessings will give us the boost we need to build the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopāla temple in Aravade as soon as possible so that the glories of the holy name and the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu will spread all over the surrounding area. In this way all auspiciousness will become manifest in this age of quarrel and hypocrisy.

This year we increased the number of devotees distributing your books, and we will double the amount of books distributed in the upcoming book distribution marathon.

We had an opportunity to serve and welcome your dear disciple His Holiness Jayapatāka Swami Mahārāja with his Gaṅgā Safari tour. The tour include 130 devotees from more than thirty countries, and at that time several *sannyāsīs* and your senior disciples blessed our center.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us an opportunity to serve your mission.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your granddisciples at ISKCON Aravade, India.

## Austin

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**My respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Śrī Caitanya and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to you! All glories to your beloved disciples, who have sincerely shown us, your granddisciples, the path that you have so nicely chalked out for us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this humble offering on this most auspicious appearance day of yours.

One morning when I was on a *japa* walk with my son, he noticed a sick cat and asked why the cat is suffering. I mentioned that every living entity in this material world has to go through *janma, mṛtyu, jarā,* and *vyādhi* — birth, death, old age, and disease. “So what should we do?” he asked. I replied that we should chant Hare Kṛṣṇa for him so that he can take a higher birth, maybe a human birth, in his next life. At that moment I realized that though I had this human birth, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had it not been for your causeless mercy it would have been no different from the life of an animal. Had you not taken the trouble of preaching and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere, thousands of people like me would still be lying on the street of this material world like that helpless cat. Sometimes I shudder when I think, “What if you had not come to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness to us?!”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your mercy in the form of your books, your lectures, your pastimes, your temples, and most importantly your unalloyed disciples.

You have given so much in your books and your lectures; there is no question you have not answered.

When I hear or read your pastimes I am amazed to realize that every act and word of yours is a profound instruction for all future generations to come.

Every ISKCON temple in the world is a piece of Vaikuṇṭha on this planet. My *daṇḍavats* to all your sincere followers, who have completely dedicated their lives to your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world.

I cannot comprehend how much transcendental *śakti* you bestow upon anyone who sincerely follows your footsteps. This is so evident in your dear disciples.

Please bless me that I can also be the dog of my spiritual master and be his faithful servant through all eternity.

Your followers at ISKCON Austin, Texas, USA.  
(written by Abhay Caran Dāsa)

## Baha (Bali)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

All glories to Śrī-Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,
