Please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. Today the whole world should bow their heads down before your lotus feet, because today is always glorified as the anniversary of the day Kṛṣṇa sent His empowered and powerful devotee to this world so that fallen souls could get an opportunity to regain their true position.

I, who am a very fallen soul, feel very fortunate to have found your teachings. I had so many questions: “Who is God?”, “Who am I?”, and many others. But after I found your books and read them, all my questions were answered very, very clearly. Not only that, you also gave the right path to be followed so that everyone could see the true and real goal of life. What would I be if I hadn’t found your teachings? What state would this world be in if Kṛṣṇa hadn’t sent His empowered and powerful devotee? Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have built a house where the whole world can live.

Again and again I humbly thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and beg your mercy so that we can always remain Kṛṣṇa conscious and continue your mission of spreading devotional service to Kṛṣṇa.

*Jagat-guru* Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your servants in Bali, Indonesia.  
(written by Yaśomatī-suta Dāsa)

## Baroda

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

An offering in the mood of humility and submission on the occasion of the110th anniversary of the Appearance Day of Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Although we are unable to offer prayers to the Lord in an adequate fashion, our duty is to make the attempt in order to purify ourselves. It is not that we should stop our glorification because demigods like Lord Brahmā and Lord Śiva cannot adequately glorify the Lord. Rather, as stated by Prahlāda Mahārāja, everyone should glorify the Lord according to his own ability. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.16.2, purport\]

Considering the above instruction, we have attempted to glorify Your Divine Grace with the following composition, which we offer at your lotus feet with all the humility at our command.

**Lead Us Out**

We find ourselves in a state, forgetful  
Of our dear Lord pure and blissful.  
In our hearts do we always scream and shout,  
O jewel among leaders, lead us out!  
We’re captured by lust, slaves of anger,  
In vice and slander we see no danger,  
A state immersed in illusion and doubt —  
O reservoir of mercy, lead us out!  
Caught in a whirlpool of desire that never ends,  
Forever dissatisfied with what providence sends,  
We’re drowning unaware throughout —  
O savior of the fallen, lead us out!  
Our Lord we know not, ourselves we know not,  
Our goal we know not, our very cause we know not,  
Yet great wisdom do we verily flout —  
O deliverer among saints, lead us out!  
Your mercy knows no limit.  
Of sinful life we’re the summit.  
Our consciousness we can’t speak about —  
O purest one, lead us out!  
The brilliant light of the *Bhāgavat*  
Illuminates one and all, but  
We remain suffering Māyās bout —  
O seer of the Absolute, lead us out!  
From watering the seed of devotion,  
You take it to its culmination.  
Still my worldly desires are strong and stout —  
O giver of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* lead us out!

Your humble servants at ISKCON Baroda, India.  
(Written by Bhakta Sumeet Shetty)

## Beed

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! This year we observe the 40th anniversary of your founding ISKCON, and here is a report about ISKCON Beed.

ISKCON Beed is in a remote area of Maharashtra, about twelve hours’ drive from Amaravati. The presiding Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, attract the mind of everyone here; They look very young and fresh. It is a small, newly constructed temple that has a charming aspect. Around the temple is a garden. It contains several different kinds of trees and is always kept clean and neat. This year some young congregational members constructed a small replica of Vṛndāvana, with Giri-Govardhana, Rādhā-kuṇḍa, and Śyāma-kuṇḍa.

Recently we celebrated the third anniversary of the installation of the Deities, and for the first time a small ISKCON Ratha-yātrā festival was held, which was attended by thousands of people.

Most devotees taking care of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda are very young and are very attached to the Deities. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless these youths that they can have lots of devotion for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda and a taste for chanting the holy name, so that they can become fit to serve better the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Once a week we have a *harināma.* We would like to distribute your books, have a Food for Life program at least once a week, increase our preaching to the youth and congregation, have a Ratha-yātrā every year, train devotees and have more facilities for them. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us that we can spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Beed to help others.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very thankful to you, for even the small ISKCON center in the remote area of Beed is benefiting many people.

Your servants at ISKCON Beed, Maharashtra, India.  
(written by Rasamaṇḍala Dāsa)

## Belo Horizonte

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

No matter how much we try, we will never be able to appropriately praise Your Divine Grace. Nevertheless, we can remain very close to you by sincerely distributing your books and in this way give more and more people the same opportunity we have had. Thus, we pray to Kṛṣṇa for His mercy so that we may constantly have the chance to preach the glories of this *saṅkīrtana* movement, just as you have taught us. In doing so, we may eventually perceive how magnanimous was your feat. There is no doubt that only a completely liberated soul like you could have accomplished so much in so little time.

The world today is different from what it was forty years ago, during the primordial age of ISKCON, and in some ways it is worse. But the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement still remains the spark of hope for millions of people. This movement has the potential to decisively change the future of our society.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg your blessings so that we can obtain even a tiny fraction of the success you had during your physical presence with us. Please help us awaken the conditioned souls in this material existence, and grant them hope through your expression of love for Kṛṣṇa.

Your Divine Grace gave us the path, and now we should not let even a split second go by without thanking you and serving you. Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your humble servants in the Belo Horizonte Mandir, Brazil.

## New Jagannatha Puri (Berkeley)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your beautiful, transcendental lotus feet.

You are our beloved spiritual master and ever well-wisher. We pray that our attachment to you will continue to grow and eventually blossom into pure love for you.

Although we are very insignificant, simply trying to be devotees advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we want to remain always attached to your lotus feet. Because of your unconditional love for us, and because you are the reservoir of myriad beautiful transcendental qualities, we cannot forget you — even almost thirty years after your physical departure from this world.

You are so filled with compassion that, to give us the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, at the advanced age of sixty-nine you left India alone, without any visible means of support, and tolerated severe inconveniences on your journey to America and afterwards.

Because you are an eternally liberated soul, forever free from the influence of material time and space, your mercy is still available to all intelligent souls who are eager to receive it.

You are so magnanimous, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you gave us a wonderful philosophy to live by, knowledge of our true spiritual identities, beautiful Deities of Kṛṣṇa to worship, the all-auspicious holy name of Kṛṣṇa, and delicious *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. You also gave us the spiritually enlivening association of devotees and the chance to attain love of Kṛṣṇa and return to a life of eternity, bliss, and knowledge in the spiritual world.

In return for all these blessings, you asked nothing for yourself. You simply asked that we refrain from sinful activities, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. In this way, selflessly giving of yourself at every step, you perfectly exhibited the magnanimous nature of a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Despite your spiritual greatness, your personal humility is so profound and heartfelt that it inspired a generation of young men and women who had based their lives on the rejection of authority to willingly accept your authority. During one of your lectures, you touched the hearts of your disciples when you stated that you saw them as representatives of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who had sent them to assist you in your preaching mission. By this expression of sincere gratitude, you displayed your devout humility. We desire to follow in your footsteps in this regard and, in doing so, please you to some small degree.

Your transcendental quality of respect for all living beings was beautifully demonstrated during your visit to the New York city temple in 1976. As you strode gracefully down the center aisle amid a multitude of beaming devotees paying obeisances to you, as is the custom when receiving a worshipable guest, a young *gurukulī* boy, who had innocently bowed down too far into the aisle, was eased backwards by the devotee next to him so the boy wouldn’t obstruct your path. When you reached the boy, you stopped, placed your right hand gently on his head, and looked at him with affection, as if to make sure that his feelings had not been hurt. In this instance, and in all of your personal dealings, you treated everyone you met with the utmost respect, thus softening their hearts and rekindling their spiritual sentiments.

Your transcendental mind, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is always very peaceful — like a country pond filled with crystal-clear water, beautiful lotus flowers, and swans gliding happily across it. Your sublime peacefulness is a natural result of your being eternally situated in transcendence, at the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Shouldering the immense pressure and responsibility of a world-wide spiritual family, you gracefully meted out love, guidance, and practical instructions with full composure, never once losing touch with your peaceful Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your mild demeanor is another very attractive feature of your divine personality. In the material world, when a person attains a position of authority, he often becomes proud, abandons the quality of gentleness, and becomes a source of anxiety for his subordinates. Although you are the world’s greatest authority, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you never displayed even a drop of false pride and always maintained a mildness around which everyone felt at ease.

You are also transcendentally poetic, Śrīla Prabhupāda, not only in the literary sense, as evidenced by your beautiful poems glorifying your spiritual master and Lord Kṛṣṇa, but in every aspect of your personality as well. Your gesturing, walking, talking, singing, smiling, dancing, and worship were all done with a natural poetic grace befitting a swanlike pure devotee of the Lord. For those sensitive souls who had embarked on a pilgrim’s progress, in search of ultimate truth and beauty, attaining your divine association marked their journey’s end, and they happily gave up all mundane pursuits to engage in your loving service. Such was the attractive power of your poetic nature, which manifested wonderfully because of your superexcellent level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your full surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa was perfectly exemplified by your constant engagement in devotional service with body, mind, and words, and by your complete detachment from the bodily concept of life. At the advanced age of sixty-nine, despite several heart attacks and many other difficulties, you single-handedly penetrated Māyās formidable stronghold and planted the seed of devotional service in the hearts of thousands of fallen souls. You were able to do this because of your complete freedom from material attachment and full surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

It is stated in the *Kṛṣṇa* book story of Sudāmā Brāhmaṇa that when loving service is rendered to Lord Kṛṣṇa the entire creation benefits. Thus a pure devotee of the Lord, who always engages in devotional service, performs welfare work for everyone. This welfare work is nothing like mundane philanthropy, which provides unfortunate persons with material facilities to temporarily offset their suffering condition; rather, it is meant to completely extricate them from material existence and award them an eternal, blissful life in the spiritual realm. During your pastimes among us, you served Lord Kṛṣṇa wholeheartedly, without deviation. In this way, you benefited the entire world indirectly by your pure devotional service, and directly by offering a potent spiritual program capable of eradicating the material existence of anyone who takes to it seriously. Your welfare work did not stop with your physical disappearance, as the preaching force you set in motion will continue to spiritually uplift the inhabitants of this planet for the next ten thousand years, far exceeding the meager philanthropy performed by those absorbed in the bodily concept of life.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa states that He is the friend of all living beings. As the Lord’s intimate associate, you also displayed friendship to all by dint of your constant engagement in spiritual welfare activities. In the material world, friendships are formed on the basis of mutual sense gratification, and when sense gratification is disturbed, the relationship is either damaged or discontinued. You were a true friend to all, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you formed relationships with others motivated by your unalloyed desire to assist everyone in achieving the highest goal of life. You never desired to exploit anyone, and if there was ever an offense committed against you or some difficulty caused by others, you never took it seriously. Because you accepted the karma of five thousand disciples, you had to undergo what appeared to be suffering. In spite of this, you never withdrew your mercy and friendship. Significantly, you always closed your letters to your disciples with the words “Your ever well-wisher.” The spiritual friendship of a pure devotee like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the rarest of gems, and intelligent persons will certainly appreciate, revere, and guard it as life’s dearest treasure.

These are just a few of the reasons why we desire to keep you always situated in our hearts, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In spite of our human frailties, we beg you to also keep us situated in a tiny corner of your boundless loving heart. Please do not forget us — your dependent spiritual children — and please always prevent us from straying from your divine lotus feet by forever engaging us in your loving devotional service.

Your servants at ISKCON Berkeley, California, USA.

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please, accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine appearance!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

As the scriptures explain in detail, although a devotee seemingly does the same activity as an ordinary man, it is totally different because the goal of the devotee is Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure. The goal of an ordinary man is the gratification of his own senses, but a devotee wants to make Kṛṣṇa satisfied. So their activities have different results. Last year was about building the new temple in Budapest, and during this period we experienced the truth of this statement in many ways. Please let me glorify you by recounting some details of this effort.

You mention building temples several times in your books, but to understand the process in practice is a very special experience. Thank you very much for letting the new temple be built so that we can offer a worthy place for Śrī Śrī Dayāl-Nitāi Vijaya-Gaurāṅga, and so that we can have some realizations in connection with it. According to the scriptures, building this temple was possible only by your mercy.

As you mention several times, building a temple is not a very important thing. It is useful if it is possible, but preaching and distributing books are much more important. Building a temple is the kind of activity that the Vedic scriptures call *pūrta,* pious activities to help simple people come into contact with religion. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.8.21, purport) The most important thing is direct preaching. These pious activities are secondary, and they are useless without direct preaching. Without preaching, there is no point in building big temples; they will be empty, and the Lord will not be worshiped there. As Śivarāma Swami explained in a lecture, Śrī Śrī Dayāl-Nitāi Vijaya-Gaurāṅga will be pleased and accept our offering only if there will be appropriate preaching. Śrī Nitāi and Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu personally did that. They went door to door, village to village, spreading love of God through the chanting of the holy name. They did not need a big temple, jewelry, plenty of flowers — They needed only preaching. So whatever happens, They are still here. They can build the whole organization again.

When Rūpa Gosvāmī and Sanātana Gosvāmī went to Vṛndāvana, there was not a single temple, but by their preaching they were gradually able to construct various temples. Sanātana Gosvāmī constructed the Madana-mohana temple, and Rūpa Gosvāmī constructed the Govindajī temple. Similarly, their nephew Jīva Gosvāmī constructed the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, Śrī Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī constructed the Rādhā-ramaṇa temple, Śrī Lokanātha Gosvāmī constructed the Gokulānanda temple, and Śyāmānanda Gosvāmī constructed the Śyāmasundara temple. In this way, many temples were gradually constructed. For preaching, construction of temples is also necessary. The Gosvāmīs not only engaged in writing books but also constructed temples because both are needed for preaching work. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu wanted the cult of His *saṅkīrtana* movement to spread all over the world. Now that the International Society for Krishna Consciousness has taken up this task of preaching the cult of Lord Caitanya, its members should not only construct temples in every town and village of the globe but also distribute the books that have already been written and further increase the number of books. Both distribution of books and construction of temples must continue side by side in parallel lines. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 7.164\]

We tried this way, as you said, and it is working. We started a book distribution marathon when we found the appropriate place, and continued it till the opening ceremony. The success of the project was possible only by your mercy. Constructing buildings is not always successful nowadays. Sometimes the buildings are not finished — the work stops because of debts, or the client goes bankrupt, etc. When it is successful, that is because of the careful planning and a serious financial background. But we had neither careful planning nor a serious financial background! We had only the desire of you and Śivarāma Mahārāja to construct a temple.

The land was a big bargain. We made special arrangements with the owners through third parties, and at the end the land was much cheaper then the price originally requested. And we could pay in installments! They even let us start construction before paying fully for the land. We found a devotee who is a construction contractor, and he started the construction before the plans were ready, and our conception kept changing during construction as well. The designer was a devotee as well, who also accepted our ever-changing desires.

After paying each of the installments, we could hardly imagine how to continue the project, how to find the money for it. And the plans were getting bigger and bigger, more colorful and grandiose. At the last moment, when it was time to pay, the problem was solved again and again, and at the end the construction has finished. You write in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 12.16:

Constructing a big building requires great energy, and a devotee does not take to such business if it does not benefit him by advancing his devotional service. He may construct a temple for the Lord, and for that he may take all kinds of anxiety, but he does not construct a big house for his personal relations.

By such instructions as these, and by the arrangements described above, you always gave us hope even in the most difficult situations, and you maintained our enthusiasm to go on with the construction.

Such wonderful arrangements are possible only by your mercy. As you always gave the right solution to your disciples and showed the right way, you made the necessary arrangements for us, too. So in a wonderful way you made our faith deeper in you, Kṛṣṇa, and in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You were the master who accomplished this project; we were only unworthy and useless tools in your work. When people come and see this beautiful building, gaze at the Deities standing on Their wonderful altar, and enjoy the nice atmosphere, it is all your glory.

Only a really expert master can make a great work with bad tools. And only the supermost expert master can make good tools from bad ones. We are useless, bad tools, and as we look at this building, we know that we could not construct it. But you are the greatest master, and you are able to make an artwork like this using us despite our imperfections. Whatever worthy things we do, they are possible only because we have gotten the necessary instructions and abilities to act properly from our spiritual masters, your disciples, and your books. As one cuts and polishes a gem to make it worthy, so you make us worthy in the same way. All the other things we do are just a waste of time.

You emphasized how important the cooperation between devotees is. This is the real power of ISKCON. We experienced this fact during the time of construction, because we needed the effort of every devotee in Budapest. It was far beyond anything we had ever done before. What’s more, to be successful we needed the cooperation of devotees not just in Budapest but throughout Hungary. So the big challenges, instead of creating quarrels between devotees, created a closer relationship between the members of our community. Everyone could see what efforts the other devotees were making for the same goal, and it was very inspiring. So we could go together on the way of realization. And we understood what the word *ātmārāma* means, as you explain it in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.27.26:

*Ātmārāma* means “one who rejoices in the self,” or “one who enjoys in the spiritual atmosphere.” . . . An example may be cited in this connection. Just as a materialist engages in constructing a big skyscraper, a devotee engages in constructing a big temple for Viṣṇu. Superficially, the skyscraper constructor and temple constructor are on the same level, for both are collecting wood, stone, iron, and other building materials. But the person who constructs a skyscraper is a materialist, and the person who constructs a temple of Viṣṇu is *ātmārāma*. The materialist tries to satisfy himself in relation to his body by constructing a skyscraper, but the devotee tries to satisfy the Superself, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, by constructing the temple. Although both are engaged in the association of material activities, the devotee is liberated, and the materialist is conditioned. This is because the devotee, who is constructing the temple, has fixed his mind upon the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but the nondevotee, who is constructing the skyscraper, has his mind fixed in sense gratification. If, while performing any activity, even in material existence, one’s mind is fixed upon the lotus feet of the Personality of Godhead, one will not be entangled or conditioned. The worker in devotional service, in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness, is always independent of the influence of material nature.

Thank you very much for giving us these experiences, and many others, and thank you for keeping us here in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, although we do not deserve your mercy.

You say that the function of a temple is to be a place where people can come into contact with Kṛṣṇa and start to practice devotional service. We have to make the temple very attractive and very different from what people ordinarily see. The temple has to be a spiritual shelter. So it is quite obvious that our next and most important step is preaching, and we have to emphasize it more and more. We have to invite people into the temple and engage them in Kṛṣṇa’s service. It is also obvious that we can do this only if you give us the ability. It is unseemly to ask for something on this day, but now we ask for something. Please give us the intelligence and determination to fulfill your desire. What we can offer is not much: we would like to execute your instruction. This is very little, but by your endless mercy, and only by your endless mercy, we can be successful. Please keep us in your service.

Your servants at the ISKCON temple in Budapest, Hungary.

## Buenos Aires

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

To the lotus feet of you, my spiritual master, I offer my most sincere and respectful obeisances.

Thank you for allowing me to continue trying to serve you and to write something to glorify you on this wonderful anniversary of your appearance in this world. Thank you for keeping me in the association of your faithful disciples and followers, who are increasing in number day after day. You have given us so much, Śrīla Gurudeva, and I want to thank you. You have given yourself. You lived among us, tolerating unlimited burdens and thus showing us in practice how to execute the process of *bhakti-yoga*.

Thank you for giving us such a great mission, in which I never feel I’ve done enough. Your vision is so great and transcendental. I hope I can follow the basics you have established and please you in your mission.

You have given us the challenge of being Kṛṣṇa conscious and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thank you. Thank you especially for your Bhaktivedanta purports, which give us the essence of the knowledge of all the Vedic literature. Your purports are so brilliant and poetic, and they reveal the essential truth about Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you for teaching us so much. Thank you for fighting to save us from this world and for giving us shelter in the enlightened world of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

I just pray to remain under your shelter, to never abandon you, and to always follow your example.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Buenos Aires, Argentina.  
(written by Mūrti Mūrti Devī Dāsī)

## Chandigarh

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We repeatedly offer our prayers unto your divine lotus feet. You are an expert navigator who can ferry us across the dreadful ocean of material existence. Only by your divine grace can we successfully go back home, back to Godhead. All Glories unto you, the carrier of Śrī Caitanya’s mission.

One more year has passed in separation from you, but still you are with us in your divine instructions. Your instructions are like beacons in the midst of a vast ocean, providing direction for so many misdirected living entities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you successfully distributed the timeless knowledge of the *Bhagavad-gītā* throughout the world to deliver fallen souls like us. You are certainly not an ordinary conditioned soul governed by the material nature. You are the ambassador of the spiritual world. Your temples are like the spiritual embassies in the material world that issue visas to go back home, to back to Godhead.

You are an ocean of mercy. You opened 108 temples all over the world, printed millions of transcendental books, and engaged your dear disciples in preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the misguided people of the modern world. Your only aim is to connect the suffering souls to their real father, Kṛṣṇa. You don’t have any desire other than to please Kṛṣṇa by distributing the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Once, in a radio interview in the U.S.A., you observed that “I have not come to earn money from your country, but I have come to your country to give you something sublime.” Your mercy is still available, though you are gone from our material vision. A liberated soul is beyond time and space, as is his mercy. You assured us that you are always with those sincere souls who strictly follow your instructions.

Here in your temple at Chandigarh, we are trying hard to follow your instructions. Due to your causeless mercy, last year the Chandigarh temple celebrated with great fervor and charm Janmāṣṭamī, four Ratha-yātrās in different towns, Candana-yātrā, a two-week-long Salil Vihar festival, Gaura Pūrṇimā, and many other Vaiṣṇava festivals. Thousands of people in this area are benefiting spiritually from these festivals and your books.

You had a great vision that the educated youth could be the best recipients of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your vision has started to unfold in Chandigarh, which is a big center of learning, with a university, engineering colleges, medical institutes, architecture colleges, and many science and arts colleges. Our ISKCON Youth Forum (IYF) is conducting regular preaching programs at Punjab University and Punjab Engineering College, and many professional students are showing keen interest in taking up Kṛṣṇa consciousness whole-heartedly. At our IYF programs, held every Sunday in the temple, about a hundred young men come from various educational disciplines, and many have started chanting regularly. Last year Chandigarh IYF conducted many structured Kṛṣṇa conscious training programs for the serious upcoming young devotees. We have plans to expand IYF preaching bases outside the temple, and we seek your mercy and blessings for the same.

We beg for your grace. In materialistic education, grace marks in exams sometimes help one pass. But in spiritual life we cannot move an inch without the grace of Vaiṣṇavas. You are the topmost Vaiṣṇava, a *mahā*- *bhāgavata,* and if you shower your grace on us we can surely get the treasure of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. Without your grace, it is impossible.

Please always be merciful to us and help us surrender more and more unto your lotus feet, so that we may continue to remain a part — a very insignificant part — of your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Chandigarh, India.

## Chicago

**In Like a Needle and Out Like a Plough**

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Here in Chicago we see that the stage is set for a mighty revival of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Some are saying that ISKCON is in a perilous condition. Especially in North America there has been a serious test of faith. There have been legal battles, and some devotees are complaining that books are not going out as rapidly as they did many years ago and that the making of new devotees has been in steady decline. In last year’s Vyāsa-pūjā book, one of your disciples even suggested that ISKCON is not well or may even possibly be ill. If that is the case, then it may be worth remembering that you, at one point, said that ISKCON is your body. Taking that metaphor to heart, we might consider that if our body is sick, we can’t cast if off or leave it. Our only option is to cure it. So if we see some problems in ISKCON, our duty is to be the ISKCON we want to see, not to stand back and complain and find fault. We can be part of the problem or part of the solution. Here in Chicago we have decided to be healthy and happy or die trying. The *mahā-mantra* is the needle, and ISKCON is the plough. You built this movement on *harināma*.

The famous San Francisco Avalon/Fillmore Big Brother and the Holding Company rock concert poster, with a beautiful picture of you smiling on it, says “Save earth now.” And that’s exactly what you did by bringing the *mahā-mantra* across the ocean and half way round the world. By the early seventies you had a couple of thousand of us chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and you said, “Except for this handful of devotees chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*, the world would have been reduced to a charred cinder some time ago.” Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said we would go through three phases. Initially, you said, people would be amused by us. They’d see our shaved heads, ponytails, and robes and think we were a goofy-looking bunch of clowns, and they’d laugh. Then, you said, when they understood we were challenging the principal activities they take pleasure in, when they came to know we were threatening their meat-eating, illicit sex, intoxication, and gambling, they would hate us and even want to kill us. But we’re in the third stage now: Now I’m hearing that not only here in Chicago but anywhere we go, when devotees appear in public, almost without exception someone comes running up and says, “Where have you guys been, anyway?” Here in Chicago we are directly experiencing that. Whenever we go on *harināma,* the positive reinforcement we regularly receive from members of the public is clearly proving your prediction true. At first they laughed, then they despised us, and now they love us. The famous author Victor Hugo said, “All the forces in the world are not so powerful as an idea whose time has come.”

In 1977, when you left the planet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we became bewildered. Though many times we had heard the verse *harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam,* which says that there is no other way than the chanting of the holy name, without you physically present to literally remind us of the urgency of this message, we stuck our nose up and looked around and thought, “There must be some other way, there must be some other way, there must be some other way.” It’s been a whole generation, and now that thirty years have gone by it’s time for us to once again take your instructions to heart.

They say an army marches on its stomach, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Well, if that’s the case, we have a real head start on everyone else because with Viśvakarmā Dāsa, Śobhāmayī Devī Dāsī, and Vinodinī Devī Dāsī in our kitchen, we can’t lose. Anyone who has the great good fortune to peer into the kitchen in Chicago won’t see people cooking. They will see cooks worshiping. Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī are not only the most beautiful Deities in the movement, They are also the most well fed and happy.

The *saṅkīrtana* army in Chicago includes jolly young *bhaktas* who love nothing better than going out and distributing books and *prasādam* and chanting and preaching all day, every day. Our Bhakta Yeshua is recognized throughout ISKCON as a truly great dancer and *kīrtana* leader, and many college students throughout the Chicago area know that they can rely on him for good, reliable information about Kṛṣṇa consciousness and transcendental knowledge. Bhakta Jerry not only heads up the Chicago branch of the ISKCON Prison Ministry but is also organizing many *prasādam* distribution programs through other faith-based organizations, such as the Salvation Army. Heading up our preaching effort for the Hispanic community is a very remarkable young *bhakta* from Argentina named Bhakta Gonzalo.

Making all this possible, and nicely exemplifying mantra eleven in the *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*, is our temple president, Sunil Mādhava Dāsa, who shows how one can understand the process of nescience and that of transcendental knowledge side by side. He is a walking, living, breathing example of this verse. In this way Sunil Mādhava Dāsa is showing us all how we “can transcend the influence of repeated birth and death and enjoy the full blessings of immortality.” (*Śrī Īśopaniṣad* 11) He inspires all the devotees to be the best they can be. Because he is so energetic and active and has such a demanding schedule, no one could imagine doing anything less than their best in their efforts to serve Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī.

Sunil Mādhava Dāsa’s assistant is an exceptional granddisciple of Your Divine Grace’s named Murāri Caitanya Dāsa. In all his actions he is the very embodiment of both a Bengali grandmother and a British officer. Murāri Caitanya Dāsa is soft, sweet, and gentle in his genuine care and concern for all the devotees, but at the same time he is as powerful and definite as a thunderbolt when there is a need for adherence to strict and necessary standards of proper Vaiṣṇava behavior.

The *pūjārīs* in Chicago are also *pākā* and exemplary. Our head *pūjārī,* Gokula Dāsa, is a sword-wielding martial arts man from Manipur, and his dedication to Their Lordships is beyond compare. We also have a bright and powerful contingent of devotees from Orissa, led by Mahotsava Dāsa and Āpta Kāma Dāsa. The Jagannātha deities are extremely satisfied and happy. No one can dress the deities more spectacularly than Īśāna Dāsa, who also makes first-class ghee wicks.

Manning Govinda’s gift shop, heading up the life member programs, and regularly seeing to the needs of our congregational members are the energetic and personable Prema Bhakti Dāsa and the profound and pleasant Gaurakiśora Dāsa, who is always amazing everyone with his quiet but regular display of remarkable competence on many levels.

The punctilious and perspicacious Śubha-kṛt Dāsa and the suave and debonair Śaṅkara Paṇḍita Dāsa keep up the very wonderful and highly pleasing service of book distribution out at O’Hare Kṛṣṇa airport. They keep all the devotees enlivened with their regular descriptions of their amazing pastimes and adventures out there as they bless the fallen souls with your incomparably valuable transcendental literature.

Last but not least, we have that staunch factotum of ISKCON Chicago, Jitendriya Dāsa, without whose regular and constant attention to maintenance the whole temple would have crumbled to dust decades ago.

Altogether, Chicago has the necessary ingredients to charge full speed ahead and establish a fitting basis for a resurgence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout all of North America. All it really depends on is our willingness to do what you want us to do, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we are becoming more and more convinced that if we want to be happy by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious, then that’s the key. That’s how we can best satisfy you and make you happy.

Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī-*kī jaya*! All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your followers at ISKCON Chicago, Illinois, USA.

## Christchurch

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You came to give the highest level of spiritual knowledge to the lowest, most unqualified.  
Before knowing you we were helpless in this crazy, mixed-up world.  
You have given life to the lifeless!  
With the application of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, everything has a purpose, from being born to old age and death.  
You linked us with the *paramparā,* those shining jewels on the path back to Godhead.  
You shed light on the meaning of life!  
You have placed Vṛndāvana, which is very far away both in distance and culture, in our hearts and in our homes.  
You gave us the true definition of love and directed us onto the path of loving Kṛṣṇa.  
All this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was a task of boundless proportions. We understand what you have done for us.

Your servants at ISKCON Christchurch, New Zealand.

## Coventry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Thank Kṛṣṇa He Sent You  
You gave up everything,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda; we’re indebted to you.  
You saw us suffering  
And gave us the highest truth,  
We were all in *māyā* for the longest time,  
So cold inside.  
Confusion in our hearts would not subside,  
What is this life?  
And then we saw the light—

(Chorus)

Thank Kṛṣṇa He sent you.  
We’d be lost without you.  
Our every wish and every dream  
Can now become reality.  
You were the sunlight,  
Completed our whole lives.  
We’re overwhelmed with gratitude.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can never repay you.  
A devotee who gave everything—  
There’s nothing in this world you wouldn’t do  
To ensure Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
Was spread all over the world,  
Because without you we’d be on the birth and death ride,  
Full of ignorance and pride,  
But you gave Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa to have in our lives,  
So we’ll be all right.  
You’re a diamond in our lives.

(*Chorus*)

See, we are so blessed,  
And then it came together for me.  
Looking back, I guess it shows that we’re destined to be  
Part of your spiritual family.  
Can’t believe what we have,  
Don’t want to go through it all over again,  
Just want Kṛṣṇa in our hearts to stay.

Your servants in the Coventry Yātrā, UK.  
(written by Cintāmaṇi Devī Dāsī)

## Dallas

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

**The Value of a Devotee**

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept the most prostrated *sarvāṅga-daṇḍavat praṇāms* that our hearts can command at the dust of Your Divine Grace’s most magnanimous and divine lotus feet on this the most auspicious and holy occasion of your 110th Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

All glories to Your Divine Grace, and all glories to your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, and all glories to your ISKCON, all nondifferent yet full of variegatedness.

As our most loving father, grandfather, and great-grandfather, please accept the following sincere, albeit puny and simple, offering of services as an expression of our heartfelt love and appreciation for you.

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**“The value of a moment’s association with a devotee of the Lord cannot be compared even to the attainment of the heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which is for those who are meant for death.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.18.13)**

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* which you most mercifully and magnanimously translated for posterity, the value of devotees and their inseparable devotional service is clearly elucidated. One great reference appears at *Ādi-līlā* 9.28–53:

All the wealth in the three worlds cannot equal the value of one such nectarean fruit of devotional service. Not considering who asked for it and who did not, nor who was fit and who unfit to receive it, Caitanya Mahāprabhu distributed the fruit of devotional service. The transcendental gardener, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, distributed handful after handful of fruit in all directions, and when the poor, hungry people ate the fruit, the gardener smiled with great pleasure. Lord Caitanya thus addressed the multifarious varieties of branches and subbranches of the tree of devotional service:

“Since the tree of devotional service is transcendental, every one of its parts can perform the action of all the others. Although a tree is supposed to be immovable, this tree nevertheless moves. All the parts of this tree are spiritually cognizant, and thus as they grow they spread all over the world. I am the only gardener. How many places can I go? How many fruits can I pick and distribute? It would certainly be a very laborious task to pick the fruits and distribute them alone, and still I suspect that some would receive them and others would not. Therefore I order every man within this universe to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and distribute it everywhere. I am the only gardener. If I do not distribute these fruits, what shall I do with them? How many fruits can I alone eat? By the transcendental desire of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, water has been sprinkled all over the tree, and thus there are innumerable fruits of love of Godhead. Distribute this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Let people eat these fruits and ultimately become free from old age and death. If the fruits are distributed all over the world, My reputation as a pious man will be known everywhere, and thus all people will glorify My name with great pleasure. One who has taken his birth as a human being in the land of India \[Bhārata-varṣa\] should make his life successful and work for the benefit of all other people.”

\[You comment:\] This is not only the duty of Indians but the duty of everyone, and we are very happy that American and European boys and girls are seriously cooperating with this movement. One should know definitely that the best welfare activity for all of human society is to awaken man’s God consciousness, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore everyone should help this great movement. This is confirmed in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Tenth Canto, Twenty-second Chapter, verse 35, which is next quoted in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

\[Lord Caitanya continues:\] “It is the duty of every living being to perform welfare activities for the benefit of others with his life, wealth, intelligence and words.’ By his work, thoughts and words, an intelligent man must perform actions which will be beneficial for all living entities in this life and the next. I am merely a gardener. I have neither a kingdom nor very great riches. I simply have some fruits and flowers that I wish to utilize to achieve piety in My life. Although I am acting as a gardener, I also want to be the tree, for thus I can bestow benefit upon all. Just see how these trees are maintaining every living entity! Their birth is successful. Their behavior is just like that of great personalities, for anyone who asks anything from a tree never goes away disappointed.”

The descendants of the tree \[the devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu\] were very glad to receive this order directly from the Lord. The fruit of love of God is so delicious that wherever a devotee distributes it, those who relish the fruit, anywhere in the world, immediately become intoxicated. The fruit of love of Godhead distributed by Caitanya Mahāprabhu is such a great intoxicant that anyone who eats it, filling his belly, immediately becomes maddened by it, and automatically he chants, dances, laughs and enjoys. When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the great gardener, sees that people are chanting, dancing, and laughing and that some of them are rolling on the floor and some are making loud humming sounds, He smiles with great pleasure. The great gardener, Lord Caitanya, personally eats this fruit, and as a result He constantly remains mad, as if helpless and bewildered. With His *saṅkīrtana* movement the Lord made everyone mad like Himself. We do not find anyone who was not intoxicated by His *saṅkīrtana* movement. Persons who had formerly criticized Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, calling Him a drunkard, also ate the fruit and began to dance, saying, “Very good! Very good!”

The following is recounted as a very clear example of these teachings. Radio Salaam Namaste is Dallas Fort Worth’s premier Asian radio station. Two weeks ago, a Muslim priest defended his faith against a very irate Hindu audience on that radio station. One of the key radio talk-back show hosts, Mr. Mohammed, is a devout practitioner of the Muslim faith, and he hosted that program. As a result of that program, Mr. Mohammed asked if a Hindu preacher would defend his faith to a confrontational Muslim audience. One of our devotees was asked to represent the Hindu faith. At the end of the program, which was on the Sunday we celebrated the beginning of Candana-yātrā, Mr. Mohammed became so interested in Kṛṣṇa consciousness that he decided to come to the Sunday Feast that very day. He was so impressed upon his arrival that he asked if he could put on Vaiṣṇava *tilaka*. He attended the chanting, feasting, and dancing and became ecstatic. He proclaimed that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness was “Very, very good!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the teachings and example referred to above are clear proof of how great a devotee you are. All the wealth in the three worlds cannot equal any one of your disciples, granddisciples and great-granddisciples, what to speak of even a small fraction of your person.

This year, the Dallas community has decided to focus on our culture (chanting, feasting, and dancing, including *bhajan* and *kīrtana* programs), spiritual care, book distribution, and reading programs. We have also revived our *brahmacārī* and *brahmacārinī āśramas,* and are happy to report that in the last three months six new devotees have joined the program. Over the last few years, more than ten new *gṛhastha* families have joined our inner community. Thus far, our book distribution has quadrupled. We are extremely grateful to you for your blessings. We palpably feel your presence and shelter. Please continue to shower your mercy upon us all. We aspire to push forth your vital mission with more and more determination and enthusiasm.

We thank you *ananta-koṭī* times, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are more dear than our own lives. Please bless us that we may always please you and never displease you.

One hundred eight Śrī Śrīmad Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*, eternally!

Your servants, and the servants of your servants, and their servants, at Śrī Śrī Rādhā Kālachāndjī Dhāma, Dallas, USA.

## Denver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your most merciful lotus feet.

Please excuse any ineptness or offenses we may commit in our feeble attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your appearance. Due to our self-absorption, lack of humility, and uncontrolled mind, we are unable to find the proper words to convey our gratitude for having come into contact with your lotus feet and the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* 5.12.12 states:

*rahūgaṇaitat tapasā na yāti*  
*na cejyayā nirvapaṇād gṛhād vā*  
*na cchandasā naiva jalāgni-sūryair*  
*vinā mahat-pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekam*

**“My dear King Rahūgaṇa, unless one has the opportunity to smear his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees, one cannot realize the Absolute Truth. One cannot realize the Absolute Truth simply by observing celibacy \[*****brahmacarya*****\], strictly following the rules and regulations of householder life, leaving home as a** ***vānaprastha,*** **accepting** ***sannyāsa,*** **or undergoing severe penances in winter by keeping oneself submerged in water or surrounding oneself in summer by fire and the scorching heat of the sun. There are many other processes to understand the Absolute Truth, but the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the mercy of a great devotee.“**

Out of your great compassion and on the order of your spiritual master, you came to this godforsaken place to help the rotting souls of this age. In spite of our fallen condition, you have given us the greatest opportunity of service to your lotus feet, but we are so fallen that we have no attraction to it. Instead of applying ourselves to this sublime process, we’ve taken shelter of our senses and mind, which is full of concocted ideas that wealth, mundane relationships, social position, and modifying what you have given us will reveal the Absolute Truth and satisfy us. The residents of Jambūdvīpa prayed, “Who is the sane man who will not associate with devotees to quickly perfect his life?” (*Bhāgavatam* 5.18.11) Maddened by the intoxication of youth and sense enjoyment, we’ve chosen to associate with a world that is tottering like a drop of water on a lily leaf. At any moment it may come crashing down and we will have to again accept a material body. But the dust of Your lotus feet can very quickly purify our most fallen and addicted condition.

The *Bhāgavatam* (2.4.18) states, *ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥśudhyanti*: “Even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power.”

Because the pure devotee has the Supreme Personality of Godhead in his heart, all those who come in contact with him become enlivened in devotional service, as evidenced by the thousands who have met or heard from you or who are now reading your books. With no qualifications, coming from the lowest classes, we have received from you the greatest gift, the opportunity to achieve the perfection of life — to join the  *saṅkīrtana* movement — but we are so miserly that we can think only of our own comfort.

The *Bhāgavatam* (1.1.22) states:

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.“**

What hope would there have been for us had you not come into our lives? Surely we would have been condemned for countless lifetimes. Just as the sun dissipates the darkness of night, your appearance has removed the dark cloud hanging over our heads. All too often we became absorbed in the doom and gloom of everyday life, forgetting that our miserable condition was due to our having forgotten to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, being solely engaged in the devotional service of the Lord, never feared any condition of life, and thus you boldly preached the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu with such compassion that even the most wretched have become attracted.

One might question, “How can one not be attracted?” You have given everything so perfectly. Because of your humility and purity of intent, the Lord has empowered you to spread this supreme knowledge throughout the whole world. You predicted that the day would come when the people would note that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is saving the world. We are witnessing it daily. So many people are taking your books, getting *prasādam*, seeing the Deity, and hearing the holy names. Not only are they appreciating, but they are also lamenting their lives before meeting the devotees. I recently talked to one elderly gentleman who had just received one of your books, and although he spoke little English, as we were talking he became teary-eyed, appreciating the precious gift you have given us. It dawned upon me that this simple-hearted man is what I wanted to be, not someone complicated by so many plans.

Recently we were trying to put your *Bhagavad-gītā* in some hotels, and some proprietors were objecting, “What will our customers say?!” or, “We get other literature free; give yours free.” As if books grow free on trees! Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us your mercy so that we may become expert and sincere preachers, because, as you’ve stated, “No one wants to serve God.” Due to the bodily concept of life, we are making so many excuses, but the service of the Lord is our real success. The spiritual master is compared to a rain cloud, which indiscriminately distributes its mercy. We may be hardhearted, but even a stone is worn away by constant drops. We don’t expect or deserve anything, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we pray that you will consider that we are aspiring to be your servants.

Your servants at ISKCON Denver, Colorado, USA.  
(written by Naikātmā Dāsa)

## Detroit

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Diving Grace!

Thank you very much on behalf of all the devotees at Devasadan Mandir for giving us most merciful Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārījī and the most beautiful Devasadan Mandir. This year, all of your beloved followers are celebrating the 30thanniversary of Devasadan Mandir. There is a new spark in the atmosphere due to the desire to increase Kṛṣṇa consciousness in everyone’s heart. This started with our most respectable GBCs, His Grace Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu, and Her Grace Mālatī Prabhu, conducting a strategic planning session with the support and guidance of Śrīmān Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Prabhu and Śrīman Ambarīṣa Prabhu. A team of energetic young devotees has been formed to take up different tasks in the temple. They are all fired up to make a difference by participating wholeheartedly in performing these tasks and encouraging others to participate.

Mercifully, you have provided us with so many areas in the loving service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī that now we are desiring to know how many more souls can take shelter of the system established by you, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

The first step is to attract more people to take shelter of the holy name. A program of chanting *japa* with Śrīla Prabhupāda has been established every Sunday before the Sunday Feast program. With your mercy, the Sunday Feast program is steadily growing, from the 4:15 pm *ārati* to the 7 pm *ārati* to the 9 pm *ārati,* and further. Detroit ISKCON youth members are enjoying continuously singing the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra for the pleasure of the Deities between the *āratis*.

The second step is to try to strengthen the book distribution program. Many members are signing up to distribute a certain number of books and encourage some friends to distribute books to *their* friends as a service to you in honor of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

The third step is to maintain what you have given us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you are an ocean of mercy, only with your help and guidance are we able to continue from one day to the next on this path of devotional service. Lord Kṛṣṇa gave you this gift, the opulent Fisher Mansion. Now please give us the intelligence to guide the new souls being attracted by your philosophy and this beautiful home of Kṛṣṇa.

We are continuing renovation projects of the temple roof, interior, exterior, and grounds. When construction workers get bewildered by looking at the amount of constant work required to maintain the building, your beloved disciple Śrīmān Surabhi Prabhu, our temple coordinator, answers them, “Every day Lord Kṛṣṇa gives us more and more opportunity to serve Him continuously.”

O savior of the whole world, please hold our hands tight because we have a tendency to be lost in this material world.

Your humble servants at the Devasadan Mandir, Detroit, Michigan, USA.  
(written by Jagad-Guru dasa)

## East Hartford

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your disciples, granddisciples, and aspiring granddisciples at your East Hartford, Connecticut, Hare Kṛṣṇa temple wish to offer their humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

By your mercy we have been very fortunate to get the association of many of your senior disciples, such as Nirañjana Swami, Jayādvaita Swami, Candramauli Swami, Indradyumna Swami, Prahlādānanda Swami, Bhakti Vidyā Pūrṇa Swami, Romapāda Swami, Bhakti Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami, Rādhānātha Swami, and others. Also, at our temple we were privileged to have had the association of Śrīdhara Swami, Gaura-Govinda Mahārāja, and Bhakti Tīrtha Swami, who are now personally serving you and Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world.

Sometimes we think of the Pāṇḍavas and how they served their elder brother Yudhiṣṭhira, or how Lakṣmaṇa, Bharata, and Śatrughna served their elder brother Rāma. Similarly, we are confident that we can please you, by serving our elder godbrothers, those who are pleasing you by their dedicated service.

As you wrote to Śivānanda Prabhu on January 23, 1969:

Regarding serving your godbrothers, this is a very good practice. The Spiritual Master is never without his followers, so to serve the Spiritual Master also means to be the servant of his disciples. When you want to serve the king, you must also serve his minister, secretary, and everyone who serves him. And to serve his servants may please him more than to serve the king personally.

We pray that you continue sending your elder sons to our humble temple to give us their association and an opportunity to serve them, and in that way please you.

Endeavoring to please you,  
The servants of your servants in East Hartford, Connecticut, USA.  
(written by Pyāri Mohan Dāsa)

## Eger

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day!

On your Vyāsa-pūjā day it is my great honor to glorify you on behalf of the devotees of Eger.

In the spiritual world there is no birth, disease, old age, death, or grief. There are no such miseries as are found here in the material world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the resident of that world, free of all anxiety. The reason you came to this material world is completely different from the reason ordinary people take birth here. You left the spiritual world, took on the hardships of this material world and the material body, only because you wanted to teach devotional service.

Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura prays to his spiritual masters as follows: “All glories to you, ŚrīRūpa and Śrī Sanātana, for you are reservoirs of *prema-bhakti-rasa*. By your mercy, a desire tree has been brought to this world which can relieve the distress of all people.”

You are tha great soul who fulfilled the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and presented the works of ŚrīRūpa, Sanātana, and other *ācāryas* to the people of the world so that you can relieve them from their distress.

Kṛṣṇa’s greatest mercy upon us is that He sent you here to save us. After leaving the spiritual world, you appeared in the holy land of India, a birth very favorable for spiritual life. But you left that auspicious land and came to the big cities of the West, where people were not at all interested in spiritual life.

You have taught us everything we need to reestablish our relationship with God, although we have no qualifications. You have given as many gifts: the scriptures, the mystery of India, your personal association — which your disciples describe as unforgettable — the holy name, and so on. The list could go on and on and on. However, there is one gift I would like to emphasize: it is ISKCON, without which we would have no chance of making advancement in spiritual life. ISKCON is giving us the opportunity to associate with devotees (*sādhu-saṅga*), to follow the spiritual standards, to worship the Deities. Anyone who is interested in Kṛṣṇa’s teaching and wants to become a devotee gets the opportunity here.

You said when you came to the West you had nothing but forty rupees, an umbrella, and a box full of books. But actually the gift you brought with you was bigger than a steamer can accommodate. During the final twelve years of your stay on earth, you distributed all those gifts. Because of accepting those gifts from you, we are eternally indebted to you. This year the devotees of Eger will try to repay their debts to you by organizing many preaching programs and a wonderful *padayātrā*.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at the ISKCON temple in Eger, Hungary.  
(written by Tīrthapāda Dāsa)

## Ekaterinburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā the devotees of the Ekaterinburg *yātrā* want to express deep gratitude to you for everything you are doing for us.

Thank you for your wonderful society of the devotees.

Thank you for your instructions. Following them, we see that our lives are changing. The Lord is gradually freeing us from *anarthas,* and the relations between the devotees are becoming more personal and nectarean. Our community is becoming more perfect because you are teaching us how to remember Lord Kṛṣṇa always. The devotees feel responsibility for your mission and are learning to overcome difficulties. The devotees are becoming more qualified in different services. All this is only by your mercy. Kṛṣṇa is sending wonderful persons with wonderful abilities to make your movement successful. We feel deep gratitude to you and firm faith in your instructions. We are praying to you to engage us in your mission of spreading Vedic knowledge, so that many people may be free from the clutches of illusion. May all the people become free form material sufferings and come to the service of the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!

The servants of your servants in Ekaterinburg, Russia.

## Fortaleza

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Most of us members of your marvelous ISKCON didn’t have the opportunity to meet you personally, which on one hand is most unfortunate, since our flickering faith could have solidified by personal association. But on the other hand, the distance nurtures the feeling of separation that somehow intensifies our devotion.

Nevertheless, by your endless mercy your sincere disciples, whom you requested your guru mahārāja to protect, provide us with their saintly association. Such protection supplies nourishment for the devotional creepers of your sons and daughters, who earnestly try to concentrate their energies on serving you.

All glories to you, whose example we all admire. You preached without seeking many cheap followers, for your only desire was to satisfy Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura by arduously making the Divine Couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, known to all.

We long for the day that we meet again. Meanwhile, please help us understand that Govinda is the supreme provider of ultimate pleasure, and involve us constantly in your service by extinguishing our false ego, the misidentification that entangles living entities in this fruitless material existence.

Eternally seeking protection under the shade of your feet, we remain  
Your servants at ISKCON Fortaleza, Brazil.

## Gainesville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Your picture calms fear and inspires hope.  
Your words clear doubts and fascinate the mind.  
Your instructions stir action and protect our tradition.  
Your *mūrti* emanates focus and love.  
Your life’s story mocks imitation yet compels us to try.  
Your ISKCON movement rolls over those who would stop it.  
Your active disciples teach others.  
Your disciples on sabbatical cannot forget you.  
Your granddisciples find strength in you.  
Your gift of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* transforms empty lives like nothing else can.  
When did you ever leave us?

Your servants at ISKCON Gainesville, Florida, USA.

## Gaṅgā Sāgara Baru Village

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please accept our prayers at your lotus feel, O Śrīla Prabhupāda.

On this auspicious day, kindly permit us to offer you some words of glorification, even though we are incapable of doing so properly.

By your mercy, now we can perform devotional service to you and Kṛṣṇa, not only in the big city but also in this small, remote village called Gaṅgā Sāgara Baru. This shows that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is developing rapidly. All of this would not be possible without your causeless mercy. Alone you struggled to spread the *mahā-mantra* in the Western world, always trying to save ignored souls like us. Obviously, your efforts were successful. By your transcendental mercy, devotees in Central Borneo now have a large piece of land for farming and are establishing a Vaiṣṇava village. where many devotees will come to live and learn to cultivate their land as in the Vedic traditional culture.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are full of unlimited mercy and blessings. We cannot properly glorify your transcendental qualities. May your name, fame, and glories be spread more and more all over the universe. Śrīla Gurudeva-*kī jaya*!

Your fallen servants in Gaṅgā Sāgara Baru, Kalimantan, Central Borneo, Indonesia.  
(written by Brajarāja Dāsa)

## Gītā-nāgarī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust at your divine lotus feet. All glories to your divine grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we feel very grateful and eternally indebted to you. It is because of your intense love and determination to fulfill the order of your guru mahārāja and Lord Kṛṣṇa that we have the great fortune of experiencing Kṛṣṇa consciousness today. You built the house in which the whole world can live. You brought Kṛṣṇa’s holy names to the land of the *mlecchas* and freely distributed them without considering who was fit or unfit to receive them. You revealed your heart, and the secret of your success, in a poem upon your arrival in America:

My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do not know why You have brought me here. Now You can do whatever You like with me.

But I guess You have some business here; otherwise why would You bring me to this terrible place?

Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it.

But I know Your causeless mercy can make everything possible because You are the most expert mystic.

How will they understand the mellows of devotional service? O Lord, I am simply praying for Your mercy so that I will be able to convince them about Your message. . . .

How will I make them understand this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own.

Somehow or other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like.

O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding.

Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts, they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.

O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.

I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now, if You like, You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.

Signed — the most unfortunate, insignificant beggar  
A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami,  
on board the ship *Jaladuta,* Commonwealth Pier,  
Boston, Massachusetts, U.S.A.  
dated 18th of September, 1965

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the deliverer of the holy names: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. We are simply running after you, begging for a drop of this nectar. You write in *Teachings of Queen Kuntī,*

Kṛṣṇa is so magnanimous that He is present before us by the transcendental vibration of His name, which has all the potencies of Kṛṣṇa Himself, and if we remain in contact with that name we shall get all the benefits of Kṛṣṇa's benedictions. But still we are not inclined to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra.

You are the revealer of the holy *dhāmas* Śrī Vṛndāvana and Śrī Māyāpur, which you encouraged us to visit regularly for spiritual rejuvenation. And yes, you created hundreds of *dhāmas* all around the world just by touching many places with your lotus feet. Gītā-Nāgarī Village is one such place of great fortune. This September marks the thirtieth anniversary of your coming to Gītā-Nāgarī. Yes, Gītā-Nāgarī is the holiest of *dhāmas* because Your Divine Grace walked down the lanes and roads and bestowed your glances of benediction upon its fields and woods and buildings and countryside. Your beloved son and spiritual warrior Bhakti Tīrtha Swami certified the sacredness of this place by choosing to leave his body here at Gītā-Nāgarī*dhāma*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, here are some service accomplishments from Gītā-Nāgarī for the year 2005. Please bless us with renewed determination and empowerment to serve better in 2006.

Along with the usual but very special services of daily worship of the Deities and maintenance of the devotees, cows, and land, the Gītā-Nāgarī farm road was redone and finished to its full length. A new woodshed was built and completed with enough room inside to store a three-year supply of firewood. The *āśrama*/guesthouse back stairway was renovated. There has been further beautification of the Deities’ altar and temple-room/*kīrtana*-hall, along with the immediate temple surroundings, where there is a plan for a beautiful meditation park. In the midst of nicely maintained fields of hay, the devotees planted and grew a gorgeous garden with many vegetables, including a large variety of squash and pumpkin, greens, lettuce, beans, cucumbers, and corn. Your children here are becoming more and more of a youth force, exhibiting significant talents in music and drama, representing you without timidity in the local area with the support of their parents, who are valued members of our devotional community and so dear to your heart.

We are hoping against hope that you will continue to cast your merciful glance and smile of approval upon your Gītā-Nāgarī project. Please bless us with a drop of your faith in the holy names of Kṛṣṇa as we strive to fulfill the prophecy of Gītā-Nāgarī*dhāma* by serving all the residents of Gītā-Nāgarī, the land, the cows, and Their most beautiful Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandjī, Śrī Śrī Lalitā Devī and Viśākhā Devī, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Devī, and Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara.

The eternal servants of your servants at Gītā-Nāgarī Village in Pennsylvania, USA.  
(written by Lalitāmṛta Devī Dāsī)

## Gītā-nāgarī Baru

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-'bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept our humble obeisances on the occasion of this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā of Your Divine Grace.

Please allow us to continue engaging in your powerful *saṅkīrtana-yajña*. Our efforts are a very small part of your great mission. We are like the spider that assisted Lord Rāmacandra in building His bridge to Śrī Laṅkā; please always give us your mercy so that we can nicely execute your desire to spread the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of your mercy and the special mercy from my spiritual master, Bhakti Rāghava Swami Mahārāja, and our guide, Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi Prabhu, the devotees staying in Gītā Nāgarī Baru are able to practice devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana Gopāla. At present we have 25 families (87 devotees) staying in our village. Due to the efforts of Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi Prabhu and Bhakti Rāghava Swami, we now have the following facilities at Gītā Nāgarī Baru: a temple, the Śrī Viṣṇu Sahasranāma Project, the Bhaktivedanta Library, a community building, a *yajña-śālā,* a guesthouse, the Gopāl Kindergarten, etc.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, please help us develop some of the wonderful devotional qualities that you possess. Please help us advance in devotional service, and please help us become attracted to the process of pure devotion.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our village of Gītā Nāgarī Baru was established in June 1999. Although some of the devotees work outside, most of them are busy cultivating their land and learning how to become farmers. We try to plant things we can offer to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Self-sufficiency is our goal. In everything that we do, the center is actually Kṛṣṇa. We request you to please help us establish the concept of *varṇāśrama-dharma*. Please help us live a life based on the principles of self-sufficiency, and also kindly help us become detached from sense gratification.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are very kind. Your books are very powerful, and you have shown by example how to engage in devotional service. You are exemplary in all respects. As Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura says:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥśrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”**
