(written by Kṛṣṇa Prema Dāsa)

## Pretoria

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa! Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious and transcendental day of your divine appearance.

As time marches on, we are seeing the rapid advancement of this Age of Kali. We are supremely fortunate to be on the ship of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With you as our captain and your sincere servants as the crew, it is certain that our destination is glorious. The storm of Kali is getting rougher by the year, and we often feel the waves cascading over our heads and shaking our boat. Sometimes we wonder how we will survive, but just as the storm is at its peak we take shelter of you. We take shelter of your example. You went through thick and thin, depending on Kṛṣṇa. The results are all in Kṛṣṇa’s hands. We should simply take inspiration and instructions from you and try our best to sail this ship through the ocean of nescience.

This ship has definitely multiplied: now we have a fleet. The storm is sometimes blinding, so we pray to you to guide us in times when we cannot see where we are going. Our humble attempt to please you is by cooperating to steer this fleet of transcendental ships back to the spiritual world.

At ISKCON Pretoria we have a stable base of congregational devotees and a core group of temple devotees. We find that living together is blissful when we take shelter of the holy name and introduce new-comers to the ecstatic realm of devotional service. Please give us the intelligence and strength to maintain and expand on the base that has been created here by your sincere followers. Pretoria is a place where so many good effects can be created for the entire society of South Africa. It is the presidential capital and a place full with vibrant and progressive youth. We want to make “Hare Kṛṣṇa” a household name here. So much needs to be done, but without your mercy nothing will be possible. Therefore, we beg you to become more and more present in our lives. Thank you for tolerating us, and we apologize for our many shortcomings.

Your struggling servants at ISKCON Pretoria, South Africa.

## Puncak

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories, all glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

You are so kind and compassionate to the suffering conditioned souls. With your causeless mercy you have saved the world. Your only concern is to wake us up, to spread the glories of Kṛṣṇa, to inspire us to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and to engage us in devotional service. In this holy mission you are uplifting us and saving all the unfortunate human beings.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have blessed us with the most precious eternal gift of being united in one divine shelter under the umbrella of ISKCON, serving Kṛṣṇa in the spirit of unity regardless of nation, caste, color, etc. The Hare Kṛṣṇa movement has saved us fallen souls and helped us reestablish our divine relationship with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Just as the supremely merciful Lord Nityānanda and Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu saved Jagāi and Mādhāi five hundred years ago, you have appeared in order to dispel the misfortune of millions of fallen souls lost due to ignorance.

We fortunate souls at your Rādhā-Govinda Mandira in Citeko-Bogor, Indonesia, feel most grateful to you, O Śrīla Prabhupāda.

By your causeless mercy, for the last fifteen years we have been engaged in devotional service, guided by Your Divine Grace as we perform *sādhana* for our spiritual progress.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please purify our minds. We have not advanced very much in devotional service, but we sincerely pray to Your Divine Grace to bless us so we can to put into practice what you have taught us.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we abjectly beg for your mercy. Please be compassionate to us, your children and grandchildren. Please bestow upon us all the courage, strength, and talent needed for performing *kṛṣṇa-sevā*. Whatever problems, temptations, and challenges may come, please protect us and help us face them so we can continue practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please make us qualified instruments to serve your lotus feet in this *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement. May all of us, your children, be worthy servants of yours.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to us and never neglect us. Please help us remember you eternally, in the spirit of devotional service.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir, Puncak, Citeko-Bogor, Indonesia.  
(written by Sarvabhāvana Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta College (Radhadesh)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

ISKCON Belgium is bringing together Hindu organizations with the goal of gaining government recognition. Recognition will mean, among other things, that children enrolled in Belgian schools will be provided religious education in Hindu religion if they choose. Issues like this, of cultural integration, should increasingly concern our movement’s leaders in many countries. We must think about how everythi ng will hold together culturally, politically, and philosophically as we follow in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footsteps through this temporary world of miseries.

For now, devotees are aloof from many worldly troubles. At Radhadesh, devotees rarely discuss the war in Iraq, now in its fourth year, or the twenty-five million Muslims — immigrant workers, their wives, children, and grandchildren — in Western Europe, but we see Muslims in Brussels when we go there to preach. The females’ headscarves (outlawed in French schools) are highly visible. So is our devotional clothing. Both types of dress indicate strong commitments to religious identity. In multicultural Europe, “Democracies recognize religious faith, and sometimes practice it,” wrote the journalist Jane Mayer, “but they survive only by their faith in themselves. Democracies preclude contending absolutisms and the dicta of fixed identities. They have to do with identity in flux, with culture, and cultures constantly transforming, molding into something new — something surprising and different and open-ended and free.” Still, twenty-five thousand Europeans annually visit Radhadesh and open up to something spiritually new — unknowingly pleasing Śrīla Prabhupāda, performing *ajñāta-sukṛti*.

And the Muslims? If the Islamic diaspora in Europe produces “a modern, critical, democratic Islam,” Mayer continued, “the Islamist regimes of the Middle East would begin to fall.” So Muslims in the Gulf have used the Internet revolution to tell the new generation of European Muslims that the only true assimilation is to radical or militant Islam. Virtually every version of radical Islam demands “the subordination of women, the stunting of education, and the curbing of the freedom of speech, of the press, and of religion,” said one editorial, and democracies oppose such violations of human rights, but usually do not see them, in and of themselves, as a cause for war. Yet America is fighting a war, supposedly for democratic reform, across the broader Middle East. What would Śrīla Prabhupāda think of all this?

According to the Vedic view that he taught, transcendence, not politics or economics, is the foundation of reality and the human condition. Śrīla Prabhupāda said:

So generally, people are suffering on account of association with *tamo-guṇa* and *rajo-guṇa*, whole material world, mostly *tamo-guṇa* and few of them in *rajo-guṇa*. The symptoms of *rajo-guṇa* and *tamo-guṇa* are lust and greediness. Just like yesterday you told me the students are talking about homosex. That means *tamo-guṇa*, that the education—students, they are discussing about homosex. That means *tamo-guṇa*, lusty desires, very prominent, and how to fulfill, by homosex or sex with woman. This is their subject matter, *kāma*. So everyone in this material world infected with this *tamo-guṇa*, all lusty desires, in various ways, varieties. And some of them in *rajo-guṇa* — politics and improvement of material condition. So we have to cut down this *tamo-guṇa* and *rajo-guṇa*, come to the *sattva-guṇa*. Then he’ll not be disturbed by these lusty desires and greediness. Then he’ll be happy. *Sthita-sattve prasīdati*. When he comes to the *sattva-guṇa*, now he has to make further progress, *sattva-guṇa*. And the progress means, being situated in *sattva-guṇa,* if he advances in devotional service, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then he surpasses all the material qualities. That is perfection of life. \[Morning Walk Conversation, 11 May 1975, Perth\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda transcendentally comprehended the foundation of reality and human life. How glorious a spiritual master! How worthy a representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva!

Very few contemporary political philosophers think about transcendence in personal terms, as Śrīla Prabhupāda did. Eric Voegelin thought of God and demigods as symbols used by human beings to articulate an impersonal transcendental reality, but he expressed ideas applicable to both personal and impersonal experiences of transcendence:

The divine is recognized as the moving factor in human consciousness. The strength of the divine pull and the human response to it may vary in particular cases, but in each instance individuals participate in the same process of discovering reality. Consequently, experiences with transcendental reality are not unconnected. They are connected by the common factor of the divine presence.

The experiences with transcendental reality that people have in common are never perfectly reflected in politics and economics, unfortunately, and we have to live with that. Voegelin said that cultures tend to guard and defend their conventional representations of truth. Their philosophical insights become culturally preserved in some form of civil theology or civic religion akin to nationalism. For most individuals the content of the civil theology, though it is only a derivative of philosophical truth, is taken as the truth itself. Because the social and political order of a community, including its institutions, are a reflection of its understanding of the larger reality, any encounters with new philosophical truths are bound to disrupt the existing order. Thus, when communities new to Kṛṣṇa consciousness encounter it, people see it as a new truth or another conventional representation of truth, and feel challenged in the ways that Mayer and Voegelin describe. ISKCON’s preachers and intellectuals have to help them and us reach the highest cultural and philosophical understandings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda himself always attempted this, by preaching and writing, while trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village. For example, on the two-hundredth anniversary of America’s Declaration of Independence, in 1976, he was interviewed for *Back to Godhead* magazine, and he declared our dependence on God, the real proprietor of the land. He said:

The government should be very expert to know the aim of life — the common cause — and they should train the people to work for the common cause. . . . \[The actual duty of the government is\] to understand what God wants, and to see that society works toward that aim. Then people will be happy. But if the people work in the wrong direction, how can they be happy? The government’s duty is to see that they are working in the right direction. The right direction is to know God and to act according to His instructions. But if the leaders themselves do not believe in the supremacy of God, and if they do not know what God wants to do, or what He wants us to do, then how can there be good government?

Śrīla Prabhupāda had great hope that Kṛṣṇa consciousness would have an important place in countries throughout the world. He set the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement going with an extraordinary and sometimes aggressive preaching spirit. We feel very grateful that he lives on through his teachings. For his words can inspire humanity to lead pure and good lives and return to the eternal, imperishable abode of Godhead.

The Radhadesh community, having observed its twenty-fifth anniversary in 2005, continues its ongoing efforts to fulfill Śrīla Prabhupāda’s vision on many planes: a huge visitors program and guesthouse, a boutique and a restaurant, a bakery and a bookstore, book and *prasādam* distribution, first-class Deity worship, ground-breaking educational programs, communications, Dutch BBT translation, membership, local publications, prison preaching, family life, festivals, conventions, and more. Kṛṣṇa consciousness can offer more than any other culture, even in the matter of being “constantly new — surprising and different and open-ended and free,” to the degree that it purely responds to the fully personal “strength of the divine pull, the moving factor in human consciousness.”Śrīla Prabhupāda generously gave us the greatest means for this spiritual elevation and the duty to influence the members of society to sacrifice for spiritual culture.

Your servants at the Radhadesh community, Belgium.  
(written by Tattvavit Dāsa)

## Richmond Hill/Markham

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you proved that as guru you are bona fide.  
You would be a great exponent of religion, the astrologers did confide.  
You came and saved us from *māyās* stormy tide.  
No matter one’s race, gender, or caste, your teachings would not be denied;  
You effectively smashed our false ego and pride.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the exceptional pure guru.  
Your mission was confirmed when you met your spiritual master in 1922.  
You converted the atheist, the Christian, and even the Jew.  
You spread Lord Caitanya’s message from Africa to Peru.  
Holding ISKCON together, you are the glue.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is you we will always revere.  
Even through the *Jaladuta’s* rough ride you did persevere.  
The hippies you were able to effectively steer.  
In fulfilling Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatīs instructions you were sincere.  
The meaning of our existence is no longer unclear.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is so much we owe you:  
You took the daunting trip to America not knowing what you were getting into,  
You planted the *bhakti-latā* seed and it grew,  
To spread Lord Caitanya’s mission around the world many times you flew,  
And you gave Ratha-yātrā its North American debut.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, on your guru’s order you selflessly became our spiritual guide  
And showed us we could not be happy without Kṛṣṇa no matter how hard we tried.  
Your desire was that our lives would not be misapplied.  
You showed what is right and what is wrong so we could properly decide  
Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the solution to Kali-yuga’s conflict and divide.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, before you enlightened us we had no clue.  
You led by example to show us what was proper to do.  
You taught us how to counter people’s erroneous points of view.  
You wrote so many books to ensure we would not misconstrue.  
You would boldly preach to anyone and everyone to get the message through.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, for us you could make things clear.  
You taught us to take care of our soul and not to hold our body dear.  
You built a spiritual house for all of us among this world so drear.  
You showed us that to Kṛṣṇa’s laws we must adhere.  
The Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra is now known far and near.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, without you our lives would be askew.  
You effectively answered where, when, why, and who.  
Your preaching would always stand up under scrutiny and review.  
From neophytes you made artists for Kṛṣṇa that so beautifully drew.  
Our rejection of materialism was long overdue.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your grace we belong to the lucky few.  
You built your own Kṛṣṇa-consciousness-spreading work crew.  
You created the perfect *prasādam* and nectar brew.  
You would diligently give class in the early morning dew.  
You insisted that preaching and book distribution are what we should pursue.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so grateful that you came here.  
Around the world Kṛṣṇa devotees you have made appear.  
Like a father, you would correct our mistakes and not let us veer.  
You showed the world that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to be followed, not feared.  
We pray that we will remain at your lotus feet year after year.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, we wish to forever thank you.  
You imparted in your lectures, books, and instructions everything you knew.  
You brought us *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* original and true.  
You gave us the opportunity to begin our lives anew.  
You introduced us to Gopīnātha black and Śyāmasundara blue.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are truly qualified.  
Never again will we come across someone as purified.  
Books, deities, festivals, and temples you did provide.  
We hope that our efforts will with your wishes always coincide.  
With your mercy, our love for Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa will never subside.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya gurudeva!

Your humble faithful servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in Richmond Hill/Markham, Canada.  
(written by Mīnākṣī-devī dāsī)

## Rio de Janeiro

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Since time immemorial, human society has had the propensity to worship God through cult and idolatry. But you, empowered by Śrī Kṛṣṇa and guided by your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta SarasvatīGosvāmī Mahārāja, clearly showed us the gap between fallen souls and eternal bliss. The various parts of a tree can survive only if its roots are watered. Similarly, one can achieve true happiness only by worshiping the personal aspect of the Absolute Truth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your humility and behavior exemplified transcendence, and your posture was always that of a menial servant of God. In such a state of humility, you said that if any reward were to be given to you, it would be to serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa eternally and never act like God.

As a pure devotee of the Lord, you possess unlimited good qualities. Your qualities and example enable us to understand our true position. Therefore, we kindly request you to let us remain small parts and parcels of your ISKCON. Please grant us your blessings so that we can carry on with your mission, the very mission initially established by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Your servants in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil.

## St. Augustine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

In November 1967 you wrote a letter containing the following words:

In my opinion, a single sincere soul can maintain a center. You know I started the center at 26 2nd Ave. alone. I took the risk of 200.00 dollars per month for the rent. . . . Still I was maintaining the establishment simply depending on Krishna and then Krishna sent me everything — men and money.

So this is the way you started the whole movement, and from this small spark the whole fire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission fanned out, thus giving the last hope for all humanity.

Here in this small temple we’re trying to preach the immortal message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and the only hope for us is your words, your voice, your books, and your love.

Although it is very difficult to overcome the three *guṇas* of material nature, it is possible by your mercy — the mercy of a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. So please help us disseminate Kṛṣṇa’s message in the way you did, and thus we will really be your sons and grandsons.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for empowering your especially dear sons to be our gurus and in this way giving us the ultimate goal of life — *kṛṣṇa-prema*. We can learn from them how to become good Vaiṣṇavas and your strict followers as we traverse the path back to Godhead.

Your humble servants at Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Mandir in St. Augustine, Florida, USA.

## Santiago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It is now forty years since you began this movement in New York under very amazing circumstances. The prediction at your birth was that you would open 108 temples all over the world during your lifetime. So, here in South America we can say that we were most unfortunate not to have had your personal association. You visited the South American continent only once, in 1975, when you went to Caracas, Venezuela.

Of course, we all know that the real association with the spiritual master is by his *vāṇī* (words) and not his *vapu* (form). Since we, your granddisciples, have your books, we should be content. But the truth is that we still do not have your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, or the Tenth and Eleventh Cantos of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* or your transcendental biography, *Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta,* by your very dear disciple Satsvarūpa Dāsa Goswami. We still don’t have these books, although the Spanish-speaking population is the second or third biggest in the world!

By your mercy, the translation of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is finished, but we have been waiting year after year to get that transcendental nectar published. Also, one of your granddisciples is translating your biography here in Santiago.

But please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the transcendental strength to finish this most important work as soon as possible! Hearing how you pushed your disciples to engage in a two-month marathon in Los Angeles in 1975 to publish the English version of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* makes us understand your transcendental mood, and we wish something similar would happen in our case also.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, inspire us, your disciples and granddisciples, to take up this most important transcendental work and sacrifice our lives for it. Otherwise we will lose faith that it is really going to happen. We all hope that this transcendental anxiety we are all feeling will become part of Kṛṣṇa’s plan for producing these transcendental books, and that our hopes will not stay unfulfilled for all time.

We hope to become your faithful servants in this regard.  
Your servants in Santiago, Chile.

## Seattle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you, our dearmost spiritual master and guide.

Today is your *āvirbhāva-mahotsava*. Worldwide, your disciples, disciples of disciples, friends, and well-wishers are celebrating the 110th anniversary of you appearance day. Yesterday, the eighth day of the waning moon, we celebrated the birth of Lord Kṛṣṇa; today, the ninth day of the waning moon, we celebrate your day of birth. One cannot but marvel at such an auspicious conjunction! First comes the Lord, then comes His pure devotee. First the master comes, followed by the servant. The master clears the way (*dharma-saṁsthāpanārthāya*) and the servant, on behalf of the master, maintains the path (*evaṁ paramparā-prāptam*).

But can’t we also say that the servant appears before the master? Saint John the Baptist appeared before Christ and cleared the way for the Messiah; King Pratāparudra swept the road with a golden broom in front of Śrī Jagannāthajī, and Advaita Ācārya came before Gaura-Hari.

Today is your Vyāsa-pūjā, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You appear a year less a day before the birth of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You prepare the way for Kṛṣṇa consciousness according to the time and place designated by the Lord Himself. History seems to favor this fact. You, our Prabhupāda, appeared on the horizon of the Eastern seaboard in New York in 1965. After spending a lifetime in preparation in India, you, as *bāṅgāla candra*, lit up our America and replaced the generic God with your master’s true epithet: *īśvaraḥ paramaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ*. Via the *Bhagavad-gītā* you taught the meaning of God, the controller. You taught about nature, you taught about time. You taught about the living entities as part and parcel of the divine. Particularly, you revealed your master in the role of the friend of the poor and Lord and master of the *bhaktas*. You preached love of Kṛṣṇa, where there is light, in the heart of darkness called the Lower East Side. You clapped your hands in the park, you clapped your *karatālas*, and you tapped your feet to the beating of the cosmic *mṛdaṅga*. Ringing out the *mahā-mantra*, you shone like the wild-flower garland of Gaurāṅga. You shone like the shark-shaped earrings adorning ŚrīŚyāma, and you shone like the pearl-white, pale jasmine buds known as the teeth of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. A beam in the night, a light at the end of the Kali-yuga corridor, illuminating the heart, you tripped the name fantastic. Thus clearing a path through the forested haunts of *māyā*, you heralded the auspicious and imminent arrival of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Now we are well into the twenty-first century, and the sun of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has lit up the entire world, from Sidney to Seattle (where we are), from New York to New Delhi, and from Surinam to Suva. Kṛṣṇa consciousness has become a household word, Hare Kṛṣṇa is a frequent epithet, talk of karma is everywhere, even our Buddhist brethren spread dharma, and yoga is the common parlance of, indeed, the masses. But Kṛṣṇa Himself remains exotic! Is He the speaker of the *Bhagavad-gītā*? Is He the God of love? Is He the charming cowherd by the side of a beautiful girl? Is He a God for the *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas*, or just an emblem of Hindu culture worldwide? Considered a Cupid among Cupids, He is sometimes reviled and at other times glorified.

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the forerunner of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the age of quarrel. Running ahead on the dark path of the *yuga,* you cry *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam, kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*! And with that divine war cry you establish the shield of truth in a world torn by the fire of lies. What is that equation of ultimate reality that you proposed in your war against *māyā*? God as Kuñjabihārī, God as Gopījana-vallabha, God as Girivara-dhārī. The reservoir of all pleasure, the source of all relationships, the cynosure of the eyes of the *bhaktas* — that is Śrī Kṛṣṇa Bhagavān!

And who is Bhagavān? He who possesses all opulence. He to whom all the demigods, including Śivajī, offer their obeisances. All *īśas* and *gaṇeśas* fall at His feet and merrily sing His praises. All creation proclaims His glory. All are in Him, but He is in no one. He is the energetic, and all others are the energy. Yes, Einstein gave the equation for matter and energy: energy equals matter multiplied by the square of the speed of light. But you give the equation for reality: *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam,* that cowherd boy of Vraja, Kṛṣṇa, is God Himself! The Lord is the creator, maintainer, destroyer, controller, friend of all. Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Puruṣottama, is Hari-vilāsa, king of all pastimes, indeed Kuñjabihārī, etc.!

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by the moon of your heart you gave God in His highest modality: Gopījana-vallabha, lover of the *gopīs* of Vraja. This highest truth you gave to one and all. Yet just as the moon appears in daily-dosed phases, sometimes exhibiting full-faced opulence and sometimes becoming dark in the middle of the cycle, so knowledge of the Absolute is accepted by the living entities according to their particular qualifications. But in truth the moon is always full and the Godhead always fully replete with all opulences, particularly within the deceptively simple sixteen-name mantra beginning with Hare Kṛṣṇa. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, gave the equation, you gave the name, you gave the tune, you gave the rhythm, and you gave the tongue a taste to vibrate *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. You are so great, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Oh, merciful Gurudeva, may we accept the moon of your teachings in our lightless hearts and run along with you, crying in chorus that ancient *śloka*: *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*!

As I write, our Seattle *yātrā* is preparing to break ground for a new temple for your Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Nīlamādhava. It will be a double-storied elegant Vedic structure standing on a busy road in the new suburb of Sammamish, in northwestern Washington state. We beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we may build a house resounding with praise for you, who came to announce the arrival of the Ṭhākuras of our heart. Only by the mercy of guru can we obtain the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. First you sit on our *vyāsāsana*. First you sit on the lotus of our grateful hearts. First we establish our understanding of *guru-tattva.* First we cry “Prabhupāda!” Then we pray, by the grace of our dearmost Gurudeva, may we proceed to the bowers of Vraja and dare to understand, to serve, to adore, making our life’s breath Rādhā-Nīlamādhava!

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble and aspiring servants at New Kurukṣetra Dhāma, Seattle, Washington, USA.  
(written by Rādhikā Devī Dāsī)

## Pune

Respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you and your divine mission!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, with every passing day as Kali increases his ravages, our appreciation of the value of your gifts cannot but increase.

• When natural disasters shake and shatter the faith of the religious worldwide in a benevolent God, your clear and convincing words — “The miseries of material existence indirectly serve to remind us of our incompatibility with matter”— increase our faith and desire to return to Kṛṣṇa.

• When materialistic media goad people to slave worse than donkeys for paltry monetary gains and inane material pleasures, your example and teachings of seeking satisfaction in simple living and highest thinking — Kṛṣṇa consciousness — resonate in our hearts.

• When AIDS and other lust-centered malaises wreak havoc in the lives of millions, they reveal the profound truth of your words: “There may be some happiness in sense gratification, but that so-called happiness is the ultimate enemy of the sense enjoyer.”

• When pseudo-spiritualists attract and mislead thousands into so-called sattvic forms of sense gratification, we realize how you have saved us from a world of cheaters and cheated.

• Whereas theistic scientists struggle to barely establish the existence of God, you freely distribute the highest form of love for the most complete understanding of God — the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

• When the mind — the modern menace — torments and ruins people all over the globe, you provide us shelter in the supreme sound of the holy name and your most well-known instruction: “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy.”

• When excruciatingly painful diseases like cancer torture and terminate millions, you open the doors to eternal bliss for your followers by blessing them with the strength to remember Kṛṣṇa and thus tolerate and transcend all suffering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you taught us how to live happily and leave happily. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your innumerable and invaluable gifts. Please protect our intelligence from the deceptive propaganda of Kali so that we may never forget or minimize your intelligence. Please bless us with the vigor to share your gifts with as many (un)fortunate souls as we can in our brief lifetimes. Please use us, your servants in Pune, to offer a new temple to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī so that we can better serve the thousands of young educated people whom you are attracting to the shelter of your lotus feet here.

Your aspiring servants at ISKCON’s Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī Temple in Pune.

## Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As the most expert servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you sowed the seeds of His devotional tree farther and wider than anyone else before you.

And because of your efforts, even years after your departure, from a small twig on the ISKCON branch of Lord Caitanya’s tree, an enthusiastic community of devotees has miraculously blossomed here in Silicon Valley.

As we daily collect and enjoy the many fruits from this magnanimous tree of devotional service, we pray to remain aware how meticulously you sowed, watered, and nourished it so that it would flourish for our benefit. With extreme care, you made certain that we received every possible advantage to help us advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

For example, before completing your translations and purports to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* you produced *Kṛṣṇa* book, assuring that we would have the all-important Tenth Canto, even if you could not complete the entire *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* before departing from this world. For a similar reason you summarized the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* in *Teachings of Lord Caitanya*.

You taught us how the Lord’s names are spiritually powerful and how chanting them would allow us to surpass all material barriers. To demonstrate how we were to rely on *śāstra,* you sometimes asked your disciples to look up and recite verses as you preached. “Find out that verse,” you’d say.

You gave us faith in *saṅkīrtana,* proving by your example how it is the source of all good fortune, wisdom, and purification. You taught us how cleanliness, punctuality, and an abundance of fresh flowers were the solid pillars for excellent Deity worship. You brought us to Śrīdhāma Māyāpur and Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and invited us to live there with you forever. And you left us all of your instructions and realizations in your transcendental books, lectures, and letters, which now keep us constantly in contact with you through spiritual sound.

Appreciating the profound value of your many gifts, we naturally ask how we may try to repay you. (Fortunately, you’ve clearly answered this question for us.)

You once said:

If you become Kṛṣṇa conscious, naturally you will try to propagate the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. That means you are paying. You have learned something from them, and you are distributing the knowledge. That was their \[Vyāsadeva’s and Nārada’s\] mission. If you keep it for yourself, then you are simply debtor. You are not paying. So there are so many complications. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, 16 June 1969\]

We can see from your example how one should never stop trying to repay one’s spiritual master. In the service of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you wrote, published, and distributed millions of Kṛṣṇa conscious books and magazines. You organized men and women, bought real estate, erected temples and schools, met with dignitaries, traveled in ships, jets, buses, cars, trains, and on foot, logging many thousands of miles — all to spread the message of Lord Caitanya to every part of the world. Virtually until your last breath, you revealed *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* whispering your commentaries into a dictaphone that your disciples held close to your lips.

Encouraging your disciples to follow your example, you sent them all over the world to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, stressing that this is the best kind of service. In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 9.29) you wrote, “The only purpose of the preachers of the *saṅkīrtana* movement must be to go on preaching without restriction.”

You impressed upon us that the lowest class of devotee (*kaniṣṭha-adhikārī*) is content to eat and sleep nicely, living in luxury and remaining free from personal inconvenience. The more advanced, middle-class devotee (*madhyama-adhikārī*), you taught us, extends his mercy toward others; and being compassionate to the innocent, he endeavors to awaken their dormant *bhakti*. You therefore urged us to sacrifice our comforts and to work hard to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Even years before founding ISKCON, sitting in your room at the Rādhā-Dāmodara Mandir in Vṛndāvana, you wrote the following stanzas revealing your bias for active preaching and heralding your coming mission:

Just preach the Lord’s message to the fallen souls,  
Going continuously from door to door.  
By the grace of that preaching,  
Your life will become truly successful.  
\[*Vṛndāvana Bhajana*\]

Just as the Lord delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi  
Out of His own causeless mercy,  
You \[Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta\] explained to everyone that this same method  
Of preaching work has to go on.  
The world has now filled up  
With many Jagāis and Mādhāis to deliver.  
Everyone is anxiously looking down the road  
Waiting for Caitanya-Nitāi to come to their rescue.  
\[*Viraha-Aṣṭaka*\]

Now, years after you wrote those stanzas, we find that we have become recipients of your merciful campaign, and we are determined to fully appreciate and remember the value of what you have given us.

Sometimes, after receiving a gift a person takes it for granted and, forgetting its value, leaves it aside for something insignificant. Or, sometimes the recipient becomes puffed up after receiving wealth and, neglecting his benefactor, foolishly thinks himself independent of his mercy.

The *Mahābhārata* tells the story of such a man, who, after becoming lost in the forest, was rescued from starvation by a crane. After nursing him back to health, the crane kindly told the man about a nearby king who would give him wealth. The man followed the crane’s advice, met the king, and received charity from him. But while passing back through the same forest where he had been saved by the crane, this ungrateful man killed the crane and ate him — simply to satisfy his tongue.

The king, hearing about this atrocity, apprehended the man and punished him by setting him loose among the Rākaṣas in the forest, who were always eager to kill human beings to eat their flesh. But even the Rākṣasas, after hearing how the man had killed and eaten the crane, refused to touch him. Apparently, ingratitude is repugnant even to the Rākṣasas.

Therefore, taking this opportunity to remember your truly unlimited mercy toward us, and with our hearts filled with gratitude, we have come together to worship you on your divine appearance day, praying to always remain in the shade of your lotus feet.

You are our true benefactor and master, and we beg only to continue working together in your service, birth after birth.

Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika Dāsa)

## Solapur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*namo mahā-vadānyāya kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*  
*a.c. bhaktivedanta-nāmne gaura-pārṣade namaḥ*

**“Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most munificent intimate associate of Lord Caitanya, for you are freely distributing the fruit of** ***kṛṣṇa-prema.*** **I offer my obeisances unto you again and again.”**

We are your unworthy grandchildren, Śrīla Prabhupāda, yet we are so fortunate that we have been connected to the Supreme Personality of Godhead through your causeless mercy. We have no qualification at all to present our offerings to you, yet by your blessings and mercy we have gained the strength to try to glorify you, the foremost pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Though we are not sincere, determined, or intelligent in our efforts to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa as guided by you, out of your compassion we are already saved from hellish life because of your divine intervention in our impious lives.

Only because of you, O spiritual grandfather, are we able to move our hands and legs a little bit in Kṛṣṇa’s service for His pleasure.  
Only because of you, O great spiritual master, are we able to take *darśana* of ŚrīGaurāṅga and Nityānanda Prabhu and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.  
Only because of you, O most compassionate one, are we able to appreciate the real *sādhus* and devotees even a little bit.  
Only because of you, O most merciful one, are we able to live like human beings, leaving behind the life of animals.  
Only because of you, O foremost Vaiṣṇava, are we able to elevate ourselves from the platform of *śūdras* and understand the qualities of *brāhmaṇas* and Vaiṣṇavas.  
Only because of you, O most compassionate one, are we experiencing real friendship and brotherhood with fellow human beings and other living entities.  
Only because of you, O leading modern proponent of Vedic culture, are we able to live life without the sinful activities of meat-eating, intoxication, gambling, and illicit sex.  
Only because of you, O intimate devotee of Govinda, are we able to appreciate the divine qualities of our mother, the cow, and make good use of our senses.  
Only because of you, O favorite servant of ŚrīmatīRādhārāṇī, are we able to understand the divine meaning of service to Lord Kṛṣṇa and His associates.  
Only because of you, ! most loving disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, are we trying to become good disciples of your loving disciples.  
Only because of you, O most intelligent preacher, are we able to speak a little bit convincingly about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, spreading the message of “back to Godhead.”  
Only because of you, O most scholarly of modern Vaiṣṇava authors, are we able to write a few tiny words for your offering. Please accept them.  
Only because of you, O founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, are we able to chant the holy names of the Lord, dance like children, and take divine *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*.  
Only because of you, O loving devotee of Lord Jagannātha, are we able to pull the chariots of Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā Devī in our town.  
Only because of you, O gem among all speakers, are we able to convince anyone about the truths of *bhakti-yoga*.  
Only because of you, O well-wisher of all humanity, are we able to understand that love for Lord Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate benefit for all humankind.

O Grandfather, despite all these advantages, we devotees in Solapur are so unfortunate that we have not been able to satisfy either you or our spiritual master by building our family. We are most fallen and less intelligent, lazy, influenced by *māyā,* and bewildered due to our thoughts of our own sense gratification. Thus we are not grateful to our Kṛṣṇa consciousness society for the transcendental benefits we received from it.

Therefore, O most compassionate one, please give us more intelligence, strength, and enthusiasm for giving the people your message of love for Lord Kṛṣṇa.

You said that the grandfather loves his grandchildren more than their father. So please give us the vision by which we can serve you and Lord Kṛṣṇa through serving our spiritual master.

Lastly, please forward our prayers to the Lord and His associates — Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, ŚrīŚyāmasundara, Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, Śrīmatī Subhadrā Devī, Śrī Narasiṁha Bhagavān, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Śacīnandana, Śrī Pañca-tattva, Śrīla Ṣaḍ Gosvāmī Vṛnda, Śrīla Guru-paramparā, and all Vaiṣṇavas.

O Gurudeva, O Prabhupāda, O ṢaḍGosvāmīs, O Mahāprabhu, please accept us, please accept us, please accept us, please accept us!  
O Gurudeva, O Prabhupāda, O ṢaḍGosvāmīs, please place us on path of *bhakti* and remove all *anarthas* from our hearts!  
O Gurudeva, O Prabhupāda, please give us health, intelligence, and strength for your mission and protect us from all Vaiṣṇava *aparādhas*.  
O Gurudeva, please give us the desire and strength to serve all Vaiṣṇavas, and make us humble and tolerant and toward our fellows beings, especially all the nice devotees.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda! Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!  
Your servants in Solapur, India.

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your exalted lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Veni, vidi, vici.* In this world of speculators there have been many conquerors who used their intelligence like the hogs, dogs, camels, and asses — never caring for an ant. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your *vyavasāyātmikā* intelligence, are the great conqueror of the *māyā* in our hearts. We are children of Kali-yuga, lost in illusion and full of lust, greed, pride, anger, and madness. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our dear all-compassionate founder-*ācārya,* are merciful even toward the little ant!

You came by Kṛṣṇa’s request, you saw our pitiful condition, and you conquered our illusion by feeding us — and you’re still feeding us — the milk of the *Bhagavad-gītā*, the nectar of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, delicious *bhāgavata-prasādam,* and your extraordinary example, association, and teachings. You gave your life to give us a society dedicated to nourishing transcendental loving exchanges in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and you are victorious.

Now we pray in the dust of your most exalted lotus feet to please bless us to always be able to keep you with us in the transcendental relationship of our loving service to you, as we remain fully aware that Kṛṣṇa consciousness has come to us by your unlimited kindness. Please bless us with the desire for achieving the qualities and etiquette of Vaiṣṇavas. Please bless us with your mercy so that we can develop our temple for your satisfaction. You are able to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone — even to the little ant!

Please accept our deep gratitude. All glories to you!

From all of us at Hare Kṛṣṇa Center, Stockholm, Sweden.  
(by Udārāṅga Devī Dāsī)

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

One day, as a young boy, I remember walking to school, and as I walked along I was trying to give some thought to the purpose of my life. I logically deduced, on the assumption that — as we were taught at our Catholic primary school — going to heaven was the ultimate goal, one would at least have to become a priest or a nun to have any realistic chance of success. If this were not so, then why would anyone bother to choose such a life? I therefore concluded almost as a matter of inevitability that I would also need to make that choice. This idea was not very attractive in itself, suggesting as it did a certain bleakness of the future, but I did feel inspired by the innate sense of “rightness” involved in making such a commitment.

Many years later I found myself again grappling with the issue of commitment. How or where exactly should one apply one’s energy, assuming that the ultimate purpose of life — as we now understood — was to return home, back to Godhead? Apart from the indications of *śāstra,* logic itself seemed to dictate that achieving the status of a “pure devotee” had to be the minimal requirement. Again, seeing that the general idea involved forgoing all prospects of sense enjoyment, this idea was not in itself very attractive. Still, as before, the innate correctness and nobility of the concept did inspire a certain idealism. However, some questions naturally followed: What was a “pure devotee?” How exactly did one go about becoming one? To what activity in pursuit of this ideal, specifically, should one become committed?

We find that within the vast realm of *bhakti* numerous options assail and beckon the devotee. Through what I believe to be the greatest undeserved good fortune, however, despite the innumerable lures, “trips,” and pure-devotional red herrings, I quickly arrived at a certain unequivocal conviction: namely, that short of somehow or other satisfying the desire of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the whole concept of “pure devotion” simply has no real meaning. That in my life, at least, pure devotion separate from the specific intent of seriously pleasing Śrīla Prabhupāda reduces to a hypothetical only — which in itself, furthermore, is devoid of even the slightest appeal. Whatever the possibilities and prospects of my existence, they simply do not, and cannot, extend beyond a mere contingency of this essential obligation of serving and somehow satisfying Śrīla Prabhupāda. This conviction, and its consequent commitment, is the only real treasure of my life, and I thank God continually for somehow having acquired it.

Within this simple profundity, the impulses first felt in childhood have found ultimate repose. Under its rule life is sane, viable, practical, and, to a small degree at least, meaningful. Śrīla Prabhupāda and his service constitute my essential security, my confidence, and my one good hope. Moreover, though deductively such a conviction may indeed make the highest sense, I am beyond caring anymore. For better or worse, I must, and can only try to, go to Prabhupāda. I simply cannot understand or aspire to more than that. Thank God.

Your servants at ISKCON Sydney, Australia.  
(written by Ātmārāma Dāsa)

## Tallahassee

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you personally took up Lord Caitanya’s mission to spread the holy name to every town and village in the world, and you have asked us to assist you as well.

In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya-līlā* 4.102–103), Sanātana Gosvāmī glorifies the transcendental character of Haridāsa Ṭhākura:

Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.

These statements also precisely describes you, who are also the spiritual master of the entire world. The two essential qualifications mentioned are *ācāra*, proper behavior, and *pracāra*, the ability to most effectively preach the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You are ornamented by the highest transcendental qualities, and you preached with great determination out of compassion for the suffering humanity and out of your dedication to your spiritual master’s order. You mercifully ask us to assist you in preaching, which will help us develop the same mood of compassion and dedication to your order, as well as proper Vaiṣṇava behavior.

We feel so unqualified to take up this order. Yet we gain real hope from Lord Caitanya’s teachings to Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya-līlā* 22.51):

*mahat-kṛpā vinā kona karme ‘bhakti’ naya*  
*kṛṣṇa-bhakti dūre rahu, saṁsāra nahe kṣaya*

**“Unless one is favored by a pure devotee, one cannot attain the platform of devotional service. To say nothing of** ***kṛṣṇa-bhakti*****, one cannot even be relieved from the bondage of material existence.”**

So it is by your favor that we can gain that qualification to take up your order.

On this most auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this material world, I fall at your lotus feet and beg for your favor, your shelter, and your mercy. Please help me realize that I have no qualification for pure, loving service other than your mercy, and make this servant one who is made of nothing other than this mercy alone.

Begging to remain your eternal servant in the dust of your lotus feet,

Your servants at ISKCON Tallahassee, Florida, USA.  
(written by Rāmīya Dāsa)

## Togo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

My prostrated obeisance at your divine lotus feet. All glories, all glories to your manifest pastimes on earth, which keep expanding through your empowered disciples!

For your granddisciples in West Africa and other parts of the world where your illustrious son His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami cultivated the seeds of *bhakti,* this is a year that our souls cry out for your mercy as never before.

Devotees from all different part of the globe assembled in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur this year for the installation of Bhakti-tīrtha Swami’s *samādhi*. So many of our leaders who were there at the ceremony invoked the memory of His Holiness's dedication, austerities, humility, dynamism, and enthusiasm in your service. So great was the downpour of praise of his wonderful qualities that stonelike hearts were melting away in tears. This is proof of your power, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Because you are a *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee of the Lord, you have created other great devotees. It is true that in this Age of Kali no one is actually qualified to receive Lord Caitanya’s mercy, but you have actually created the needed qualifications for those who are assisting you in the *saṅkīrtana* movement. His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, following your footsteps, also created the needed qualifications for the race of people whom many still believe are lower than *mlecchas*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the same way that you went to the West and succeeded in planting the seed of *bhakti* in a place others considered barren, Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja planted the seed of *bhakti* in the regions where till today some still think Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the birthright of only those who were born Hindus. Without him, African Vaiṣṇavas would have been a rarity. Today, ISKCON can boast not only of temples in West Africa but also of many sincere followers of the Vedic culture, a *sannyāsī,* a representative of ISKCON preaching to academicians in American universities, dozens of devotees trained in their own base, managing temples and expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness to other frontiers. This is not an ordinary feat. We can understand that this is possible only when one is empowered by his guru and the *paramparā*.

Just as some detractors criticized your innovative ways of preaching, some have criticized Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja’s preaching techniques as well. But whoever will stand up to criticize a garden of roses flourishing on cemented ground will himself become an object of mockery. Everywhere he went, Bhakti-tīrtha Swami attracted media attention by his presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in style and grandeur. The result is that eminent leaders and millions of commoners in many countries all bear witness to his convincing messages of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through radio interviews, television shows, and his numerous books. In every way he was an embodiment of your mercy.

I heard that Your Divine Grace once declared you would take the dust of that person on your head who preached in Africa and in the Muslim countries (the Middle East). This is another area where His Holiness fulfilled your expectations. By his exceptional power he made many Africans into Vaiṣṇava *brāhmaṇas* and opened over twenty temples across seven countries in West Africa alone. You had indeed blessed Bhakti-tīrtha Swami by embracing him and declaring that his life was already successful. Your blessing was greatly manifest in him, and he gave testimony of this in his final meditations, saying, “I have been blessed by the best. I will settle for nothing less.” May we also realize how blessed we are by coming in contact with the movement you created, and by coming in contact with your empowered representatives like Bhakti-tīrtha Swami and a host of others who are strongly pushing on the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya.

For those of us who did not have the fortune of meeting Your Divine Grace in person, we became fortunate by meeting those who are carrying on your mission in the same spirit that you executed your guru’s mission.

On this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that we may continue to remain enthused and guided in the same spirit of those exceptional disciples of yours, the *mahātmās,* who are not bewildered and are busy creating waves of intense *bhakti* and *śaraṇāgati* to the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa.

The loss of our spiritual master means that it is time for us to grow up, to reach a new level of understanding of our own purpose, our own potential for taking on responsibility and even leadership positions. It is time for us to move forward to help fill the gap that has been left by the physical absence of the spiritual master. Our guru has been called to serve somewhere else by the Lord’s desire, which means we as his disciples are here to carry on with the mission of our guru, and some of us will need to step forward to take up more of that responsibility.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us that we may be able to grow up from this important branch of yours, His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja, on the great tree of which Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu is the root, and that we will be able to also draw on your blessings to make our lives successful in devotion.

Your granddisciples in the Togo yātrā, West Africa.

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our humble obeisances.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for crossing the Atlantic Ocean in 1965 despite so many difficulties. And thank you for creating your ISKCON — a place where we can find shelter from the sinful Age of Kali. Yes, Kali-yuga is progressing rapidly, and the situation in the world is very tense. Sense gratification is the meditation of most of the population. But because you have given us the most sublime process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we feel some protection. Chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa is so powerful. You started by just sitting down in the park and chanting. And the pure sound vibration is still being felt now, all over this planet. And sincere souls are feeling purification. We are still trying to appreciate and understand some of your glories. We simply pray that even with all our disqualifications you won’t forget us and that you will keep bestowing your mercy.

Your servants in Tokyo, Japan.

## Toronto

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories unto your lotus feet! All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how is it possible to glorify you — the person who saved the entire world from the pangs of material existence, the person who showed us how to achieve true, eternal happiness, the person whose actions shape and define our lives even now, so many years later? Through your transcending the need for sleep, your twelve circumnavigations of the world, your countless hours of writing, reading, preaching, chanting, cooking, loving, accepting, tolerating, and understanding, you have created a worldwide sanctuary for those of us you left behind. In particular, we’d like to tell you about the Toronto temple.

Toronto is a metropolitan city blanketed with all the aspects of *māyā* you warned us about. Just down the street lies a swanky, posh portion of the city called Yorkville, where people engage in all sorts of illicit activities. Devotees have often said that it is difficult to cultivate a healthy spiritual life in the midst of such darkness. But thanks to you and your disciples, we are slowly trying to “kīrtan-ize” the whole of Toronto. Yorkville now not only houses expensive boutiques and restaurants, but is now also home to *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. In fact, in the last few years the management and congregation have made a concentrated effort to serve your mission more than ever before. Just this past February, the Congregational Development Ministry (headed by your dear disciple Jayapatāka Swami) awarded our temple second place honors in North America, recognizing us for the number of *bhakti-vṛkṣa* programs and home programs we have, as well as other factors such as a large regular congregation and the biggest Ratha-yātrā in North America. Our book distribution efforts and results have also increased significantly. We were awarded third place in North America for small temples. We realize now why your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, suggested having temples in the busiest portions of cities; it truly has created a wonderful preaching opportunity for us.

Some devotees have lovingly dedicated themselves fully to a large altar-renovation project. Out of love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṣīracorā-Gopīnātha, they are creating a more comfortable and opulent living condition for Them. Others have begun an initiative to connect with all the newcomers to the temple, in an effort to save them from the wrath of *māyā* by maintaining contact with them. Additionally, we have very active young congregational members and a committed management team.

While we are all engaged in these activities, we are still determined to engage in more preaching with more fervor. The opportunities in this city are endless. Please bless us so that we can successfully spread your message, even though we are small and insignificant.

We also wanted to thank you for the everyday things of your society. Thank you for providing us with a home. Thank you for providing us with a family. Thank you for introducing us to our eternal friend and father.

Of course, you are always glorified and remembered for the rays of bright light you shed on this dark civilization through your miraculous activities, but we also haven’t forgotten the little things. Like your walking in a *dhotī* on a cold Manhattan day, the loving exchanges you intimately shared with your disciples, your sleeping on a hard floor, and your not sleeping at all. Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are too many to list. The love, the understanding, and the compassion you showered us with are still very alive and prominent around the world, including in the Toronto temple.

Your potency is revealed whenever a wonderful drama is performed or a sumptuous Sunday Love Feast is served. It’s revealed when almost every Sunday hands rise when we ask who is at our temple for the first time. It’s there when your disciples and granddisciples deliver lectures perfectly in line with your teachings. It’s there when we mount a cooperative effort to sell your books, renovate the altar, plan a Ratha-yātrā, or have a *bhakti-vṛkṣa* or *saṅga* program. It’s there in our daily lives when we struggle with the challenges that *māyā* throws at us. But thanks to you, we have a true, eternal home and family where we can always find perspective, support, and genuine love. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving us, and please bless us so that we can help save the rest of our city. Please also bless us so that we will serve you in a spirit of cooperation, always keeping your mission in mind.

Once again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how is it possible to glorify you? This feeble attempt certainly does not suffice.

Your most loving, indebted, and grateful aspiring servants at ISKCON Toronto, Canada.  
(written by Rūpānuga Dāsa and Bhaktin Rashi Singh)

## Tucson

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it has been such a long time since you were here with us in your beautiful form that we so loved. It has been a hard time, almost thirty years, and without your words and counsel we would long ago have returned to our lost places in Māyās world.

But before you passed on to Kṛṣṇa’s loving arms, you passed on to us a gift so great that even a tiny drop of its nectar has allowed us to continue to attempt to fulfill your instructions. You gave us a gift that Kṛṣṇa himself bestowed on you in full. This gift keeps us in Kṛṣṇa’s service and protects us in these long days without you.

You have given us “the strength of the strong”— that quality of Kṛṣṇa’s that he demonstrated by lifting Govardhana Hill, that quality you exhibited by coming to us in the West when most pious men would have been content to live out their days in reading and prayer.

At times our strength has wavered. At times it has been used recklessly or for the wrong purpose. But because it is Kṛṣṇa’s strength you gave us, whenever we think of you, our dear *ācārya*, and Kṛṣṇa, our most merciful Lord, we have been able to rally to your cause and, in doing so, keep ourselves from falling totally into the teeth of hell.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we will hold tightly to this gift you so lovingly bestowed. There will be no task that we will not endeavor in your name. There will be no adversary we will shrink from, even if it be within ourselves. There will be no time that we will cease to endure hardship if it will take us closer to meeting your goals.

Dear Prabhupāda, thank you. From the depths of our hearts, thank you.  
Your eternal servants at the Caitanya Cultural Center of Tucson, Arizona.

## Tyumen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please accept our respectful obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet.

The devotees of the Tyumen *yātrā,* Prabhupāda Desh, are realizing more and more how great your love for Kṛṣṇa is. Your compassion toward the fallen souls is so great. You tolerantly trained uncivilized people of the West and gave them Lord Caitanya’s mercy in the form of the chanting of the holy names of the Lord. In this way they could be purified and become pure devotees of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Every day we glorify your appearance in this world, your mercy, and your instructions. We worship your *mūrti,* offer *pūjā,* and sing *Guru-vandana*. We try to maintain your spirit in our *yātrā* by following your instructions in spite of any difficulties and obstacles.

The devotees of the Tyumen *yātrā* feel that everything that happens with us is completely your mercy. Every new devotee who joins your movement is your posterity. We try to be always with you and feel your presence in our life. We have firm faith in your instructions. The devotees are becoming more serious and perfect in devotional service. At the beginning of this year we organized the first Vaiṣṇava festival in Tyumen. The devotees and guests are very happy to remember those days spent together in *kṛṣṇa-kathā, kīrtanas,* and different services. The holy *dhāma* revealed itself in Tyumen. Please bless us to make such festivals every year. The December marathon was also very successful for the Tyumen devotees. We took first place in book distribution in the Ural region and took second place in book distribution for all of 2005. Thank you for engaging us in devotional service to push on your mission.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! We beg that you mercifully continue to give us opportunities to help you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have given us the essence of life, and your divine mercy nourishes our souls. Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

The devotees of Prabhupāda Desh, Tyumen, Russia.

## Udaipur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the sacred dust of your lotus feet.

How can I serve you? More and more people are coming to know about you every day. Starting in 1965, you blessed millions of souls by inspiring them to accept the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You started alone, with great faith in the holy name, feeling helpless but very confident in the instructions of your divine guru mahārāja. You came to fulfill what Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu had foretold five hundred years ago — that His name would be glorified in every town and village in the world. In a very practical way, by your pure devotion you revealed to the world the meaning of the *mahā-mantra*. In so many ways you preached to all classes of people, destroying with the shining lamp of knowledge their illusory bodily conception of life.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* we learn how King Pṛthu desired to have millions of ears to listen to the message spoken by pure devotees. You delivered that same message freely to millions with full enthusiasm. Because of your efforts, many, many generations will learn the wonderful art of developing Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the solution to all problems. You taught the simple method of devotional service by practicing it yourself, teaching by your perfect example. We know that if we please you, Kṛṣṇa will be satisfied (*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*), and you will be pleased when we become 100% Kṛṣṇa conscious.

All glories again and again to your divine appearance, to your instructions, and to the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the distribution of your transcendental books in the *saṅkīrtana-yajña*.

Thank you for coming and showing us how to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava.

Your eternal servants in Udaipur, Tripura, India.  
(written by Durgama Dāsa)

## Ukraine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurāṅga!

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (3.21), Lord Kṛṣṇa states, “Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues.” In the purport you write, “The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* also affirms that one should follow in the footsteps of great devotees, and that is the way of progress on the path of spiritual realization.” By what inconceivable fortune has this world attained the rare gift of your instructions? It is hard to understand the depth your mercy.

In this dark Age of Kali, when most common people do not follow any rules or authorities, you gave us ISKCON. When the TV, the press, and all literature are saturated with sex, violence, and time-wasting nonsense, you gave us your books, filled with the nectarean descriptions of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His devotees. At a time when most people find depression and frustration at the end of their lives, you left us the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which can deliver all of us from any calamities and troubles and give us the endless joy and happiness we are so eager to find.

You gave us a real society, a society of Vaiṣṇavas, where devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa is the very aim of life. Out of your unparalleled compassion, you left the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple to give all conditioned souls transcendental knowledge.

Although you often said that the material world is not a place fit for a gentleman, you gave us so many standards for everyday life so that we could make the best use of this bad bargain: the basic principles of cleanliness, health care, and Vedic morality; transcendental literature, music, and the art of cooking; and spiritual principles of finance, architecture, diplomacy, marketing, publishing, childcare, temple management, etc. Your Divine Grace taught us that everyone who meets the devotees should feel that they are ladies and gentlemen. This is a very important feature of your teachings, for you always wanted your disciples to be the best of everyone, the leaders of society.

We were completely covered by darkness, but you opened our eyes and saved us from dumb and useless lives. You gave us a chance to change our lives for the better, to be with Kṛṣṇa, not apart from Him. It is a rare fortune to be in ISKCON. When we will fully appreciate your mission?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength to follow the path you have given us. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength to overcome all the *anarthas* in our hearts. At every moment we need your great and powerful mercy.

Thank you very much.

Your humble servants in Kiev, Ukraine.

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I offer my respectful obeisances at the lotus feet of my dearmost spiritual master.

The lotus feet of my spiritual master are my only hope in this world of shadows. They are the safe boat on which I am trying to cross over the risky ocean of material existence. I grasp these lotus feet with all my strength because I am afraid of the material waves that are continuously tossing me up and down, up and down, from *bhoga* to *tyāga,* from false happiness to distress, from depression to exhilaration. In this state of total exhaustion, I find that the only thing left to me is the strength to hold on to your lotus feet and hold them tight.

These lotus feet are the meaning of my existence, the torchlight that penetrates the fog of my ignorance and shows me the way out of this dark net of material encumbrances. They are the goal of my austerities, of my spiritual practices, of my duly performed daily activities. They are the center of my interests and the thread of my thoughts.

At the end of a busy day, when my limbs are tired and my mind is restless, my greatest solace is to open a volume of your masterpiece, the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Then a miracle occurs: another world opens in front of me, the world of great personalities, elevated sages, and pure devotees, the world to which you belong. At this point all the arguments of the day, all the worries of my fatigued mind, all the thorny relationships that have pierced my heart fade away as if by magic, and I find myself in the mist of very elevated assemblies, where the greatest of all souls reveal to each other their own spiritual realizations. To my amazement, I can listen to their presentation of the finest truths, something that only a few years ago I couldn’t even dream of. But now here I am, a poor small-minded soul with all my *anarthas,* yet I am able to listen to the sublime prayers these exalted personalities offer to the Supreme Lord, expressing their imperishable knowledge, their dedication, and their devotion.

If I meditate for a moment on how all this came about, how I have been elevated so quickly from the lowest plane to the highest, I am struck with wonder at your mystical power! You changed my low and unworthy nature into divine nature, giving me the right instruments for appreciating spiritual subjects and desiring spiritual association. I bow down again and again before this divine power of yours, and I pray constantly to be able, by your grace, to remain attached to your lotus feet until I give up this mortal dress.

One of your disciples once asked you, “If I think of Kṛṣṇa at the moment of death I will go back home, back to Godhead, but what if I think of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?” To that you replied, “You will come to me!”

Yes, this is the exact destination I am heading for in my life, and also the destination I dream of for my afterlife. There are four verses in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that I like in a special way; they depict the moments just prior to the departure of Haridāsa Ṭhākura:

Haridāsa Ṭhākura made Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu sit down in front of him, and then he fixed his eyes, like two bumblebees, on the lotus face of the Lord. He held the lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu on his heart and then took the dust of the feet of all the devotees present and put it on his head. He began to chant the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya again and again. As he drank the sweetness of the face of the Lord, tears constantly glided down from his eyes. While chanting the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, he gave up his air of life and left his body. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā* 11.53–56\]

In the wake of these words I pray for your mercy: let me remember your lotus feet at that crucial moment, let me grasp them and keep them in my heart.

It is said in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that one who understands the transcendental nature of Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, qualities, and activities will not take birth anymore in this world, but personally I would feel perfectly accomplished if in this span of life I could just realize a spark of the majesty of my spiritual master. He turned iron into gold, in no time turning my tasteless life of petty attachments into sublime moments of transcendental association of the highest order. He disclosed to me the most secret knowledge and allowed me to enter the most elevated spiritual circles, where I could enjoy the association of Śukadeva Gosvāmī, Sūta Gosvāmī, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Śrīmatī Kuntī Devī, Nārada Muni, and the *mahājanas,* just to mention a few. His pure words flow into my ears like drops of nectar and open for me countless windows into the spiritual world so that I can have a look into the spiritual nature and become attracted to it.

What else can I say? My fortune is so great that if I could just conceive of a fraction of it I would never, even for a moment, neglect to be grateful to my spiritual master.

I know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I am not a good disciple. I may have sometimes pleased you, but more often you may not have been so pleased with me. Still, in my foolishness I maintain a great hope that one day you will take notice of whatever small service I have been trying to render to your mission, and overlooking my shortcomings and weaknesses, you will kindly place your lotus feet on my head, thus making me the most happy soul in this world. Then I would cry in great joy for having reached the greatest treasure, and following the instruction of Rūpa Gosvāmī, I would strengthen my conviction that I can make it in this lifetime.

Impatience is overcoming me. I know that is not a good quality for spiritual advancement, and somebody may say that I am proud, that I should desire to be the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant. But I hanker for being again in your personal presence, wherever you may be, in order to hear you, serve you, praise you, and pray to you. Forgive me, forgive my impudence. I am only a poor and crazy disciple of yours who am always afraid of losing your mercy.

You are my only hope in life, the only safe shelter in an ocean of uncertainty. Please keep this crazy soul at your feet, and I promise that I will try to serve you without giving you any more trouble.

The devotees serving you in the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Ramaṇ Temple (Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa) and in Govinda’s (Milan), Italy.  
(written by Āli-Kṛṣṇa Devī Dāsī)

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day  
At your soft lotus feet we humbly pray,  
Under your shelter we may ever stay.  
By your guidance please show us the way.  
Your eternal servants may we ever be.  
You gave us the light with which to see.  
We are yours life to life for eternity.  
Please forgive our shortfalls and infirmity.  
Though we have no reason to qualify,  
You’ve lifted us up to the spiritual sky.  
Only by your grace can we justify;  
In the *dhāma* at your feet we prostrate lie.  
Your order upon our heads we shall bear.  
Only by your causeless grace are we here.  
Your satisfaction is our only care;  
Your dissatisfaction our greatest fear.  
Though you know well our inability,  
Somehow you keep us despite our frailty.  
Your instructions mold our reality,  
Your order our life’s one good quality.  
You blessed us with Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name;  
Received from you, it’s our life’s greatest gain:

*aho bata śva-paco ’to garīyān*
