*yaj-jihvāgre vartate nāma tubhyam*  
*tepus tapas te juhuvuḥ sasnur āryā*  
*brahmānūcur nāma gṛṇanti ye te*

**“Oh, how glorious are they whose tongues are chanting Your holy name! Even if born in the families of dog-eaters, such persons are worshipable. Persons who chant the holy name of Your Lordship must have executed all kinds of austerities and fire sacrifices and achieved all the good manners of the Āryans. To be chanting the holy name of Your Lordship, they must have bathed at holy places of pilgrimage, studied the** ***Vedas,*** **and fulfilled everything required.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.33.7)**

“In a holy place like Vṛndāvana, there are many devotees constantly engaged in chanting and hearing the glories of the Lord. If one gets the chance to hear from pure devotees in such a place, allowing the constant flow of the river of nectar to come from the mouths of pure devotees, then the cultivation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness becomes very easy.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.29.39–40, purport)

Though your temple’s been guided by many a hand  
And has sheltered your devotees from many a land,  
They are all just like pearls that are strung on a strand:  
Your Divine Grace in Vṛndāvan, on whose order we stand.  
Hardly a face you would now recognize:  
So many new devotees. From this you may surmise  
Such powerful preaching, from which we realize  
Through your instructions you live: a Vaiṣṇava never dies.  
In the holy dust of Vṛndāvan, we pray at your lotus feet:  
Please bless us that our preaching may never meet defeat.  
We give our life to you, pray we may soon again meet.  
Till then we worship you on Śrī Vyāsadeva’s sacred seat.

Praying for your causeless mercy, we remain  
Your servants at Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir, Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma, India.

## Wiesbaden

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Our easy journey to other planets will happen when we will do the needful by the mercy of Your Divine Grace.

You gave us everything: eternal service, love, mercy, knowledge, and happiness. We have everything we need, spiritually and materially, to overcome our conditioning and go back home, back to Godhead.

The easy journey has just now begun. Thank you very much for showing us the way.

Your humble servants in the Wiesbaden-Rhein-Main-Taunus yātrā, Germany.

## Yogyakarta

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

On this most sacred day, along with all your followers around the world we humbly prostrate ourselves at your divine lotus feet. It is due to your unlimited compassion and mercy that here in Yogyakarta we are able to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and maintain our worship of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi at our Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Ashram.

As “The Student City of Indonesia,” Yogyakarta has more than one hundred colleges and universities, with thousands of students coming from all parts of Indonesia, as well as from overseas. Preaching here is a big challenge for us, for you always stressed the importance of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the students and to youth in general. Under the guidance of His Holiness Bhakti Rāghava Swami and His Grace Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi Prabhu, we established our *āśrama* in 2003. It serves as a student hostel and preaching center. We thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. By your mercy our *āśrama* is now slowly becoming the most important learning center in Yogyakarta for students to learn about the teachings of Vaiṣṇavism and Hinduism. More and more people from different backgrounds visit our *āśrama,* and your books are wonderful resources to satisfy all their spiritual inquiries.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please kindly give us your mercy so that we can continue our devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. May we always be determined to spread the glories of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa to the intellectual community here in Yogyakarta. Our devotees here are confident of the availability of your mercy, which comes in many forms. Please allow us to recognize it, to store it for times of extra need, and to distribute it as well.

Your humble servants at Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Ashram, Yogyakarta Province, Indonesia.  
(written by Suryalocana Dāsa)

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Brazil)

Dearest grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our most humble *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet.

This year marks the fortieth anniversary of your dear ISKCON. When ISKCON was founded, you made clear your desire to see books published and widely distributed as an integral part of its goals. We have been doing our very best at the Brazilian branch of your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust to see to it that this may happen. We treat each and every book printed with the highest respect and care, knowing that your books are nondifferent from you and nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa Himself. Unfortunately, due to our impurities, we do not always obtain the results we desire, neither in terms of final printing quality nor number of books distributed. The story of Bali Mahārāja in the Eighth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* clearly demonstrates that one’s power, both mundane and transcendental, can be acquired only through the blessings of one’s guru and other saintly persons. As our founder-*ācārya*, therefore, you are uniquely qualified to empower and bless us so that, despite our faults, we may be able to do more for you—to print nicer books and sell more and more of them. Please, therefore, be merciful and forgive us for our faults and bless us to be better servants of Your Divine Grace.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Brazil.  
(written by Giridhārī Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (India)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The world’s people, desp’rate for joy to arise,  
Forget that for everyone death is the prize.  
But good fortune comes if they just get your book;  
So let them all get one by hook or by crook!  
Our work for your BBT kindly accept;  
Though insincere, we your instructions have kept.  
Your kindness alone motivates our dull minds;  
We hope with us some satisfaction you’ll find.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in India.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your golden lotus feet!

As a fledgling granddisciple of yours, naturally I was surprised when asked to write this offering to you on behalf of the North American BBT. I lack any qualification and am so junior I feel hesitant to write on behalf of so many advanced devotees. I can only pray that Lord Kṛṣṇa, the source of all intelligence, will supply the necessary intelligence to properly glorify you on this most blessed day. As this intelligence comes through you and my guru mahārāja, I can simply offer Gaṅgā water unto the Gaṅgā. Yet despite this, I pray sincerely for a drop of your mercy so I can compose a suitable offering for you on this most blessed day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed us how to attain the mercy of Kṛṣṇa: please the spiritual master by executing his instructions. You explained this secret of your success in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.22.7):

One has to receive the message of the spiritual master regarding how to act in one’s capacity, for the spiritual master is expert in giving such instructions.

This combination, the instruction of the spiritual master and the faithful execution of the instruction by the disciple, makes the entire process perfect. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura describes in his explanation of the verse in *Bhagavad-gītā*, *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ*, that one who wants to be certain to achieve spiritual success must take the instruction from the spiritual master as to what his particular function is. He should faithfully try to execute that particular instruction and should consider that his life and soul. The faithful execution of the instruction which he receives from the spiritual master is the only duty of a disciple, and that will bring him perfection. One should be very careful to receive the message from the spiritual master through the ears and execute it faithfully. That will make one’s life successful.

At an arrival address in Los Angeles, on June 20, 1975, you explained that our movement is very easy: “We simply follow the predecessor’s instruction.” No new discoveries are required: “We simply repeat the words and the instruction given by the predecessor.” You point out that “\[We are\] singing daily *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. Very simple thing. We are receiving the transcendental knowledge through *guru-paramparā* succession. So we have to simply take instruction from guru, and if we execute that to our heart and soul, that is success. That is practical.” Out of humility, you claim no qualification save trying to satisfy your guru. In that same arrival address you said:

My Guru Mahārāja asked me that “If you get some money, you print books.” So there was a private meeting, talking, some of my important Godbrothers also there. It was in Rādhā-kuṇḍa. So Guru Mahārāja was speaking to me that “Since we have got this Bagh Bazaar marble temple, there has been so many dissensions, and everyone thinking who will occupy this room or that room, that room. I wish, therefore, to sell this temple and the marble and print some book.” Yes. So I took up this from his mouth, that he is very fond of books. And he told me personally that “If you get some money, print books.” Therefore I am stressing on this point: “Where is book? Where is book? Where is book?” So kindly help me. This is my request. Print as many books in as many languages and distribute throughout the whole world. Then Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will automatically increase.

You gave your heart and soul trying to please your guru maharaja and you asked us to do the same. “He used to say whatever money is there was to be used for printing books. He was so happy to print books. So you do it all very nicely. I printed my books, and now you do it.” (Room Conversation — 3 November 1977, Vṛndāvana) Your disciples deeply imbibed your instruction and greatly pleased you with their printing efforts, especially with the famous seventeen-books-in-two-months marathon. You even wrote a letter (to Rāmeśvara & Co — Vrindavana, 1 September 1975) saying:

You have taken seriously this work and I know that my guru maharaj is pleased with you because he wanted this. So by this your endeavor you will all go back home back to Godhead.

You interwove your instruction, your *vāṇī*, throughout your books for the benefit of your disciples and all future generations. You have detailed “all the rules and regulations of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness” (Declaration of Will, June 1977) and given “full directions in \[your\] books” (letter to Satsvarūpa — Calcutta, 13 September 1970). Since your books are *rāja-vidyā rāja-guhyaṁ*, they answer “all questions” (letter to Brian Fleming — Mayapur, 6 March 1974). You explained to Amarendra Dāsa that “the main point is simply to execute everything with complete sincerity and enthusiasm, without deviating from the principles that I have given you in my books” (Calcutta, 31 January 31 1972). You also emphasized that these instructions in your books are personal instructions to everyone: “The instruction given in my books is supposed to be personal instruction. When we read the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*, it is understood that we are receiving personal instructions of Kṛṣṇa.” (Letter to Dhṛṣṭaketu — Bombay 14 October 1973) You have explained Kṛṣṇa consciousness so thoroughly that if there is anything we do not understand, “then \[we\] simply have to read again and again. By reading daily the knowledge will be revealed to \[us\] and by this process \[our\] spiritual life will develop.” (Letter to Bahurūpa — Bombay, 22 November 1974)

In 1977 everyone was in great anxiety because they knew you were about to depart. You were asked on several occasions what we should do when you would leave. You said that “I will never die” because “I shall live \[forever in\] my books, and you will utilize.” (Press Conference — San Francisco, 16 July 16 1975) During a morning walk at Cheviot Hills Golf Course in Los Angeles (13 May 1973) Paramahaṁsa Swami asked, “When you are not present with us, how is it possible to receive instructions, for example, on questions that may arise?” You replied that the “answers are there in my books.” Paramahaṁsa Swami further inquired, “Do you direct us also through the heart? Besides the Paramātmā?”“If your heart is pure,” you assured. “Everything depends on purity.” On one morning walk in Toronto (7 August 1975) you reassured us that “in my absence you read the books. What I talk, I have written in the books. That’s all. . . . You can associate with me by reading my books.”

Now that you that you have left your body, your disciples have held true to your desire. They have acted as proper stewards of your books and increased the printing and distribution of your books. These books form the backbone of your society and our preaching efforts. When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared, He “ushered in the era for the *saṅkīrtana* movement. It is also said that for ten thousand years this era will continue.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.5.23, purport). Your books are the law books to guide us through this most glorious era. The brilliant effulgence of your books will restore the sight of “persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the age of Kali.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.3.43) Everyone who reads your books is eternally, spiritually benefited.

Your disciples are now getting older, and some are leaving their bodies. The next generation must now prepare to take the lead in this mission. Already many aspects of this movement are being organized by second generation devotees. With the training your granddisciples have received from your competent disciples and your immortalized instructions in your books, Kṛṣṇa consciousness will surely flourish for the next ten thousand years. Your books will be printed profusely in every major language and be read in every town and village. Every temple will be fully stocked with all your books. “Provided we keep \[this movement\] uncontaminated” (Morning Walk — Los Angeles, 11 June 1976,), it will go on and grow unimpeded, as Lord Caitanya predicted. Therefore I beg of you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that we can follow exactly as you have instructed in your books, that we can keep your movement uncontaminated, that we can preserve the sanctity of your transcendental books, and that we can please guru and Kṛṣṇa with our insignificant efforts.

Your humble aspiring servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in North America.  
(written by Oṁkāra Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

It was many years ago that you appeared in this world, and many years have passed since your disappearance. Still, your presence is vividly felt by those who have taken the path you showed, the path of devotion to Kṛṣṇa. Why? Because you are still teaching us through your books. When we open any of your books, you talk to us as if you were standing beside us. We can hear you even though you are not physically present. Undoubtedly, your books are the greatest gift you have given us, because through your books you are still here with us. And what could be a greater treasure than the association of a pure devotee who is unique even among pure devotees? To come in contact with a Vaiṣṇava like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is even more rare than seeing the Lord with our own eyes.

And what could be greater than serving or worshiping a dear devotee of the Lord? Lord Śiva says, “Of all types of worship, the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is best, and better than the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is the worship of His devotee, the Vaiṣṇava.” (*Padma Purāṇa*, quoted in *Bhāgavatam* 8.5.49, purport) Isn’t our fortune extraordinary — that we are able to serve you and worship you daily? Certainly it is!

Although you consider all types of devotional service — preaching, worshiping the Deities, cooking, and all the many other services devotees perform — equally “the most important,” we, your granddisciples at the North European ——BBT—, are focusing on publishing books, an activity especially dear to you. You have always encouraged your followers to publish your books, to distribute them, and most importantly, to study them carefully. Why did you so much stress the reading of your books? Because your books contain the transcendental knowledge that has come down in the line of the great Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas*. You translated these books and then commented on them for the single purpose of giving transcendental knowledge to the fallen and the distressed and elevating them to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Besides encouraging us to translate, publish, and distribute your books, you also told us that it is the duty of all Vaiṣṇavas in our line to write transcendental literature:

The Six Gosvāmīs, they engaged themselves in literary work. *Nānā-śāstra-vicāraṇaika*. They are . . . You’ll find in Rūpa Gosvāmīs books. Oh, how many references are there! From *Purāṇas,* from *Vedas,* from this literature, that literature. Highly, great scholars. So that is one of the duties. We have given the list of qualifications of a devotee. One of the qualifications is poetic. Poetic means not write poetry. Poetic means literary man. They must give literature. Naturally they’ll give. That is the nature of devotee. Because without literature, how can we enlighten the people at large? My guru mahārāja used to say that this press is *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga*. *Bṛhat* means bigger, at large, bigger *mṛdaṅga*, bigger. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.12–13, New Vrindaban, 11 June 1969\]

In Vṛndāvana there are *prākṛta-sahajiyās* who say that writing books or even touching books is taboo. For them, devotional service means being relieved from these activities. Whenever they are asked to hear a recitation of Vedic literature, they refuse, saying, “What business do we have reading or hearing transcendental literatures? They are meant for neophytes.” They pose themselves as too elevated to exert energy for reading, writing, and hearing. However, pure devotees under the guidance of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī reject this *sahajiyā* philosophy. It is certainly not good to write literature for money or reputation, but to write books and publish them for the enlightenment of the general populace is real service to the Lord. That was Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatīs opinion, and he specifically told his disciples to write books. He actually preferred to publish books rather than establish temples. Temple construction is meant for the general populace and neophyte devotees, but the business of advanced and empowered devotees is to write books, publish them, and distribute them widely. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 19.132, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga* is heard all over the world. Just as Lord Jagannātha mercifully comes out of His temple during Ratha-yātrā to give His *darśana,* your books come out from the press by the millions to enlighten the people of Kali-yuga. By the hard work of your *saṅkīrtana* devotees, the books find their way into the homes of even the most fallen. People are taking up Kṛṣṇa consciousness simply by reading one of your books. This was your dream; it was your mission to lead all those who think this planet their real home back to their true home, back to Godhead. This was the only reason you appeared in this world.

It is impossible for us to repay our debt to you for giving us the priceless treasure of devotional service, but we hope that we can give you pleasure by carrying on your mission of publishing transcendental literature. We would like to see the bright smile we have only heard about from your disciples, the smile that appeared whenever a new book came off the press. One day we would like to report to you that all your books have been translated into all the forty-five languages for which we are responsible, and that these books are printed and being distributed. And we would like to present you with more and more books written by your disciples and granddisciples — just to see you smile. Many such books already exist, tangible proof of their authors having profoundly understood your teachings and themselves having become elevated Vaiṣṇavas — something you very much desired to see.

We feel fortunate to serve you at your BBT. This service provides us with a unique opportunity to get your constant association. We hope that we will not squander this golden opportunity you have so mercifully given us, and that one day we will be able to offer you not only books or book distribution scores, but our pure hearts filled with *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

Your servants at the North European division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Nalinīkāntā Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Western Pacific)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our fallen obeisances beneath the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to your causeless mercy and continued transcendent presence in our lives!

Time happens. The years roll by and we find ourselves sitting in the front row, witnessing a lively yet horrifying play that depicts quite convincingly what it means to be caught fast within this temporary material world. How many times have you told us that this is a place of suffering, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Sitting in that front row seat, we are witness to the often incomprehensible sufferings of others, and at those times your teachings begin to finally hit home.

And for some of us, your teachings don’t begin to *fully* hit home until *kāla,* time, helps make their truth crystal clear to each and every one of us — personally. As the years go by, events occur in our own lives — and in the lives of those most dear to us — that yank us right out of that sometimes cushy front-row seat and pull us right up onto center stage to taste directly the pains of this world, upfront and personal. It’s not a question of “if” but of “when.” No one likes to hear this. No one wants to experience it. But, most unfortunately, time assures it will happen to all of us.

The greatest fortune for us devotees, however — at these times of great test — is that somehow we were given the eternal blessings of your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your writings, your words — every one of them is meant to console us, comfort us, protect us, enlighten us, and ultimately to thoroughly free us from the fears and pains of this world. And to carry us back to our real, blissful, and timeless home.

We have been made so fortunate by Your Divine Grace. And now, as you have said, we need to make others fortunate. We need to give the soul-saving touchstone of your transcendental books to others.

At the Western Pacific BBT we were surprised (happily!) to discover recently that one reclusive devotee had quietly, over the past twenty years, translated the entire *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* into Arabic. Already, Arabic speakers are being turned into devotees by those books, which are first being made available digitally on krishna.com.

We humbly beg Your Divine Grace to keep us closely in your dear service of publishing, printing, and encouraging the distribution of your transcendental books. In this fallen state, it seems way, way premature to even begin to imagine — yet still we shamelessly and selfishly try to imagine — that distant future when we may be invited up onto the stage where the *Real Play* is going on. Where *you* are, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. Until then, we beg you to please continue to give us your causeless mercy and to keep us engaged somehow in your most important, transcendental BBT service — without any possibility of interruption.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at the Western Pacific division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa Dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Austin Home Temple Gathering Center

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

On your Vyāsa-pūjā day, I’m searching for words to describe the feelings that flood my heart with gratitude and wonder that a tiny fallen soul, like myself, was given such an opportunity and rare gift: to be blessed by even a moment of association with such an exalted Vaiṣṇava as you.

With your expertise, love, and compassion you carefully trained thousands of disciples who carried out your orders and created these beautiful *dhāmas* for us to visit and benefit from! By reading some of the books of your first disciples, like Guru Dāsa, I am understanding and appreciating so much more the magnitude of what you have created; the reawakening of India to Her own glorious culture by attracting those outside! I thank you from the bottom of my heart!

O Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma*,  
The divine place of Rādhā-Śyāma.  
Countless devotees flock there to see  
You, the original desire tree!  
O glorious Śrī Māyāpur *dhāma*,  
O Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya and Nityānanda Rāma,  
By Your beautiful magnanimous glance  
You cause the devotees to sing and dance!  
O Śrīla Prabhupāda, our master divine,  
You embody the teachings so sublime.  
Attracting all with practical love,  
By example you teach as it is above!  
Filled with awe at what you have done,  
Through your disciples somehow a miracle spun.  
Unfathomable mercy always at hand,  
One must take it and beg to understand!  
By begging the heart becomes humble,  
Only then there’s no where to tumble.  
A drop of humility we beg you to share,  
So our steel-framed heart may truly care!  
Your presence there so deeply I felt,  
Otherwise how could my heart begin to melt?  
Of your mercy only I am made,  
Other than that I would simply fade!  
Again and again I beg of you,  
Please help me to my self be true,  
Then fearlessly inviting everyone I meet  
To taste the dust at your lotus feet!

Your servants at the Austin Home-Temple Gathering Center.  
(written by Rasajña Devī Dāsī)

## Back to Godhead Magazine / Krishna.com

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

It was, as usual, an overcast day in Dublin, Ireland. The year was 1976. A young man, disenchanted with material life, was strolling down the street when he noticed one page ripped out of a magazine lying on the ground. Curious, he picked it up.

The magazine was *Back to Godhead.*

Years later, the young man, now a Hare Kṛṣṇa devotee, married and had a son — me.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I grew up hearing of your struggles in starting this magazine, essentially the beginning of everything that was to follow. I heard how you single-handedly wrote, edited, typed, and distributed your message of God consciousness to an increasingly atheistic people. War raged around you, but you did not care. You were determined to fulfill your guru mahārāja’s mission at any cost.

As I heard these legendary stories, I read *Back to Godhead* magazine. My parents kept folders to which they would add each new issue. I looked at the pictures of your *saṅkīrtana* movement around the world again and again, and as I grew older I began to read some of the articles. BTG was part of my life — one of those things that seem like they have always been there.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, to now be actually on the staff of your magazine is nothing short of surreal.

I am trying to help out as best I can among so many incredibly talented and dedicated devotees. Some have steadfastly continued your mission for years, and some are second-generation devotees who, like me, owe a large part of their very being to you and to *Back to Godhead* magazine. Together we are trying to expand BTG to new heights.

You said you wanted BTG to reach as many people as *Time* or *Life* magazine. And we may wonder how this is possible, when people's interests lie elsewhere. But Kṛṣṇa consciousness, ever fresh anyway, has never been more relevant. And we’re seeing the results.

From humble beginnings, our website, krishna.com, which we dub the *bṛhad-bṛhad-mṛdaṅga,* now sees 3,000 visitors a day and over 600,000 unique visitors a year.

Last year, *Back to Godhead* magazine in North America printed 100,000 copies. Our European offices printed 90,000 copies. And our Indian edition printed 600,000 copies, bringing the combined total of magazines to 790,000.

As ever, we continue to take your example and use the latest technology in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have Kṛṣṇa radio and Kṛṣṇa webcams, and are working on introducing *Back to Godhead* in audio format.

And with all this, we have not forgotten what you said in 1970: “Realization means you should write. Every one of you. What is this *Back to Godhead* for? You write your realization — what you have realized about Kṛṣṇa. That is required.” Following this instruction, we are now establishing a network of devotee writers to do exactly that.

The Kṛṣṇa conscious revolution has only just begun. And by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it will flourish.

Your servants at Back to Godhead Magazine and krishna.com.  
(written by Mādhava Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Today begins the thirtieth year after your passing from this world. In your books, tape recordings, films, videos, and photographs you remain with us as fresh as ever. You have said that the spiritual energy is ever fresh and ever expanding, and we are fortunate that, by the mercy of Lord Caitanya, your mission is being spread to every town and village just as He predicted. Your band of enthusiastic followers steadily increases. Perhaps not as explosively as during your time with us, but nevertheless we see the creation of new centers and temples, even in far-flung places of the globe such as Siberia and Paraguay. The holy name is now being heard in so many places as a regular feature of daily life.

In a lecture in Bhuvaneśvara, on 22 January 1977, you said:

Rāmānanda Rāya said that “It doesn’t require of any change. Simply if people giving up his own endeavor to understand what is God, what is life. Simply if he remains humbly and hears from the real authority, then by hearing only he can conquer Ajita.” Ajita is Bhagavān.

So this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is giving chance to everyone to hear from the authoritative sources, from the *śāstra*. *Sādhu śāstra guru*. . . .

So my request is that you people of Orissa, you are ordinarily Vaiṣṇava, so take this seriously. Don’t be misguided by rascal who is not guru. There is no difficulty. Suppose, as Caitanya Mahāprabhu: *yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa*. You become guru. Whomever you meet you simply speak what Kṛṣṇa has spoken. That’s all. It is already there. You have to repeat only, and you become guru. Our request is that you all respectable gentlemen, you develop this center of ISKCON. We have published so many important books. They are being appreciated all over the world by scholars, by universities. You also study them, learn Kṛṣṇa science, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and preach in your country. That is my request.

This remains the basis of everything that you taught and the instruction for all of us to follow, no matter which country we live in. Your example of untiringly preaching is the indication to all of us that you are most dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa and you are His true representative. Advancement in spiritual life depends upon dedicated service to Kṛṣṇa through his representatives in the line of disciplic succession. In your own case, you relate how the commentary of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura on the *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ* verse in the *Bhagavad-gītā* was the inspiration for your service to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. We aspire to follow your example in this regard and remain fixed and enthusiastic to serve your mission by the distribution of your books, the establishment of centers for Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and engaging in preaching work in whatever capacity we can, whether it be Deity worship, *prasādam* distribution, rural development of *varṇāśrama* communities, or the many other types of service that you delineated during your brief visit with us.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!  
Your humble servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta College (Radhadesh)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your lotus feet!

Upon receiving a sacred thread, Śvetaketu, the son of the sage Uddalaka, was sent by his father to a forest *āśrama* to study the *Vedas* at the feet of a guru. Uddalaka knew that that without studying the *Vedas* a *brāhmaṇa* is a *brāhmaṇa* in name only, so he directed Śvetaketu to learn and clearly understand the *Vedas*.

When twenty-four years old, after twelve years of intense study, Śvetaketu returned home, proud of his profound knowledge. However, Uddalaka, seeing his son’s inordinate self-esteem, wanted to examine him to see whether he had truly understood the *Vedas*.

“Tell me, dear boy,” asked Uddalaka, “have you learned the knowledge by which even what is not heard about becomes heard? Can you perceive what cannot be perceived, or do you know what cannot be known?”

Unable even to grasp his father’s questions, Śvetaketu humbly asked him, “O father, I do not know about all this. Will you please enlighten me?”

At this point in the *Chāndogya Upaniṣad,* Śvetaketu’s actual learning begins. Previously, by taking worldly knowledge for wisdom and misidentifying erudition as realization of the truth, he had proudly overlooked the essence of the *Vedas:* Brahman, or that from which the world arises, that by which it is supported, and that into which it returns.

Although the impersonalists equate Brahman with the brilliant, unvariegated effulgence of the spiritual sky, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly taught us that the Supreme Brahman, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is not impersonal, or null and void. In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya-līlā* 8.245) you explain:

Material education aims at increasing the activities of material sense gratification. Beyond material sense gratification is another negative form of knowledge called *brahma-vidyā*, or impersonal transcendental knowledge. But beyond that *brahma-vidyā*, or knowledge of the impersonal Brahman, is knowledge of devotional service to the Supreme Lord, Viṣṇu. This knowledge is higher. And higher still is devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, which is the topmost form of education.

Furthermore, in the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.6, for example, you make it clear that

Kṛṣṇa is the central pivot of living beings, and He is the all-attractive living entity or eternal form amongst all other living beings or eternal forms. Each and every living being has his eternal form in the spiritual existence, and Kṛṣṇa is the eternal attraction for all of them. Kṛṣṇa is the complete whole, and everything else is His part and parcel.

To learn to love Kṛṣṇa, the son of Mahārāja Nanda in Vṛndāvana, is the only goal of life that can completely satisfy the self. Thus the perfection of Vedic study is not flawless knowledge of grammar (*vyākaraṇa*), perfect pronunciation of Vedic hymns (*chandas*) or a great understanding of the linguistic forms (*nirukta*). Rather, it is to become educated in *bhakti,* a learned labor of love. *Karma* and *jñāna* are two branches of the Vedic tree, but *bhakti* is the ripened fruit. This is what Bhaktivedanta College aims to accomplish: educating students in the science of devotional service, so that they can become authoritative, empowered representatives of Your Divine Grace.

Yet you explain in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.1.10 that in the advancement of spiritual knowledge “all progress depends more or less on the mercy of the spiritual master.” This is what Śvetaketu came to realize, too. Thus on this most auspicious anniversary of your appearance, we, the students, staff, and tutors of Bhaktivedanta College humbly beg you: “Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please enlighten us and show us the right way to reach the goal of our studies — unmotivated, uninterrupted devotional service unto the transcendent Lord.”

Bhaktivedanta College at Radhadesh, Belgium.  
(written by Yugala Kiśora Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture (Johannesburg)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

So often we hear it said, “Jesus died for our sins,”  
Yet the desire to sin remains in our consciousness like toxins.  
Atoning and sinning again seems the story of most lives.  
Who will teach them how for real purity to strive?  
The best of you is in your purports, you proclaimed.  
At purging us of the desire to sin they are aimed,  
Alive like the Supersoul to guide one at anytime,  
Purifying and endearing, chanting Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes.  
Lord Buddha came to teach nonviolence.  
In today’s violent world the subject is given less importance.  
How to understand what violence means,  
An understanding of which the consciousness will clean.  
“Do not become *ātmahā,* a killer of the soul,” you teach.  
To distribute this knowledge to others, you preach.  
To not use intelligence to pursue life's goal  
Is spiritual suicide; thus true nonviolence you did extol.  
This year, as always, we humbly thee beseech  
To help us in our meager efforts to practice and preach,  
To imbibe the lessons you taught,  
And to become worthy deliverers of the message you brought.

BCEC Preaching Report for 2005

On the basis of the books you mercifully gave to us, last year we held structured, interactive classes in Cape Town, Durban, Guyana, Houston, Johannesburg, Mauritius, Mexico City, Montreal, Pietermaritzburg, Toronto, and Vancouver. By your kind mercy, 868 students studied in the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture.

Praying to continue serving your mission, we remain  
Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, in Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Mādrī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

I am reading a diary of your last months with us.  
How strongly you spoke against this material world,  
How you scoffed at Darwin and all his derivatives,  
How you were disgusted with the doglike sniffing of the two legged animals  
Who pose as human beings  
How hard it is to pull away from this material world,  
How hard not to be attracted, distracted,  
To not get drawn into to a comfortable illusion.  
Yet you made it so easy for us:  
You gave us the beauty of Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
That can pull our minds  
If we can just allow it to do so.  
You have given everything.  
Can we take it up  
Seriously  
And cross over from this world of birth and death?  
Can we?  
Can I?

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”**

The staff and students at Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School, Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Ānanda Vṛndāvaneśvarī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley and Mumbai)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet!

His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura gave you the order to preach in the Western countries. An ordinary person would have considered it an impossible order to fulfill. Your godbrothers had attempted it and failed. But you had full faith in the words of your guru mahārāja. You meditated on Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s commentary on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41: “As such, to be well versed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, one has to act firmly and obey the representative of Kṛṣṇa, and one should accept the instruction of the bona fide spiritual master as one’s mission in life.” Your Divine Grace had boundless enthusiasm, determination, and patience in working to fulfill the instruction of your guru mahārāja.

In Boston Harbor, you wondered how people so covered by the modes of passion and ignorance could understand the transcendental message of Vāsudeva:

Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it. \[*Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma*\]

But you were determined. In the face of opposition from the police, you advised your disciples to be determined and patient as well:

As far as police problems are concerned, this is going on all over the world. Especially here in Melbourne, they are being persecuted by the police. Even they are going to jail daily, but still they will not stop their chanting and distributing. When they go to jail, they refuse to eat the food, and in this way so much public sympathy is there. So eventually the police will accept us and our Sankirtana activities will go on unchecked. Simply we must have the determination to continue our preaching work under all circumstances. \[Letter to Guru-gaurāṅga Dāsa, 11 February 1973\]

If there is always keen enthusiasm for serving Krishna, and if there is also patience and determination, then everything will be successful. \[Letter to Hariprasāda Dāsa, 23 December 1972\]

Besides enthusiasm, determination, and patience, Your Divine Grace also emphasized the spirit of cooperation. Indeed, Your Divine Grace has declared that “You will show your love for me by cooperating together.” In some letters, you emphasize the idea of unity in diversity in this regard.

Material nature means dissension and disagreement, especially in this Kali yuga. But, for this Krsna consciousness movement its success will depend on agreement, even though there are varieties of engagements. In the material world there are varieties, but there is no agreement. In the spiritual world there are varieties, but there is agreement. That is the difference. The materialist without being able to adjust the varieties and the disagreements makes everything zero. They cannot come into agreement with varieties, but if we keep Krsna in the center, then there will be agreement in varieties. This is called unity in diversity. I am therefore suggesting that all our men meet in Mayapur every year during the birth anniversary of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. With all GBC and senior men present we should discuss how to make unity in diversity. But, if we fight on account of diversity, then it is simply the material platform. Please try to maintain the philosophy of unity in diversity. That will make our movement successful. One section of men have already gone out, therefore we must be very careful to maintain unity in diversity, and remember the story in Aesop's Fables of the father of many children with the bundle of sticks. When the father asked his children to break the bundle of sticks wrapped in a bag, none of them could do it. But, when they removed the sticks from the bag, and tried one by one, the sticks were easily broken. So this is the strength in unity. If we are bunched up, we can never be broken, but when divided, then we can become broken very easily. \[Letter to Kīrtanānanda Swami, 18 October 1973\]

We have been able, by Your Divine Grace’s mercy, to more widely distribute the BI name and the writings of the Institute members among scholarly circles this year. For example, Rasarāja Prabhu was invited by the chief editor of the monthly magazine of the Computer Society of India to guest-edit their December issue, on the theme of *Physics, Philosophy and Information Technology*. The issue prominently mentions the Bhaktivedanta Institute, as founded by Your Divine Grace. It also contained an article presenting our “relational property viewpoint,” based on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. A number of top scientists around the world participated and helped in preparing this special-theme issue for the BI and thus participated in devotional service. The magazine issue was sent to twenty-seven thousand readers.

We offer another significant preaching event for Your Divine Grace’s pleasure. A very prominent Nobel Laureate in Physics, Steven Weinberg, had written an article on “Einstein’s Mistakes” in *Physics Today* (Oct 2005). *Physics Today* is read by all physicists throughout the world. Among the points he discussed was Einstein’s well-known rejection of the contemporary understanding and application of quantum theory. Following majority opinion, Weinberg also argued that Einstein has been shown to be wrong on this matter.

However, our own work in quantum theory at the BI in Berkeley and Mumbai, which is based on Your Divine Grace’s Bhaktivedanta purports in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and which aims to provide an alternative to contemporary understanding and application of quantum theory (thus it is not just a “philosophical” work), regards Einstein’s basic objections as being essentially correct. Thus, Rasarāja Prabhu wrote a letter to the editor of *Physics Today* pointing out that the Nobel Laureate’s presentation of Einstein’s objections to quantum theory was incorrect.

Although the editors of *Physics Today* received over three hundred letters, they selected our letter, along with the letters of seven other distinguished physicists in the US, for publication. The letter was published in the April 1 issue, and the name of your “Bhaktivedanta Institute” got sent out worldwide to one hundred twenty thousand scientists who subscribe to this prestigious magazine. Although we are all totally unqualified, the fact that our correction of such a top Nobel Laureate got published, especially in such a prestigious forum, we take as a signal from Your Divine Grace that our understanding of quantum theory, based on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* is correct and on a sound footing, spiritually and scientifically, again by your mercy only.

We pray for the continued inspiration and strength from Your Divine Grace to make scientific presentations of the philosophy of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* solely for your transcendental pleasure and satisfaction.

Your servants at the branch of the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Berkeley, California, USA, and Mumbai, India.  
(written by Grantharāja Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Kolkata)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We at the Bhaktivedanta Institute beg for your blessings on this most auspicious day, and we pray that we may never forget your transcendental instructions to our international director, His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami. Please bless us that we may always be engaged in assisting him in his efforts to fulfill all of Your Divine Grace’s instructions for the Bhaktivedanta Institute.

In the year 2005, we have seen some good progress in our preaching efforts in India. We are therefore submitting a brief report for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace.

We now have three BI buses traveling to college campuses in all different parts of India. These mobile Institutes are equipped with kitchens, bathrooms, gift shops, and even a retractable stage for cultural and scientific presentations.

In December of last year, we organized an “All India Student Conference on Science and the Spiritual Quest for Ultimate Reality.” Held in Vṛndāvana, this conference attracted a huge gathering of students from all across the country.

Professor V.B. Sharma, Principal of Delhi Engineering College, gave the keynote address, and many students from IIT campuses across India participated. By Your Divine Grace’s blessings, we are planning to make this conference an annual event.

For your transcendental pleasure, we are continuing our publication activities. During the past year, we have published the following books: *Towards a Culture of Harmony and Peace,* a book with submissions by Desmond M. Tutu, the Dali Lama, and President Abdul Kalam. We also published *Savijyanam*, Volume 3-4, and *God, Intelligent Design and Fine-Tuning: A Dialogue between T. D. Singh and Michael Behe*.

We also published the following title: *God Is a Person: Reflections of Two Nobel Laureates: Open Dialogues with Dr. T. D. Singh* (His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami). This is in fulfillment of an instruction that Your Divine Grace gave to Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami thirty years ago.

Here are some letters of appreciation from scholars and students for the preaching work of the Bhaktivedanta Institute:

Dear Dr. Singh,  
Today I had the fortune to meet Sri Jumukta Das and Sri Prasad Das (Volunteers in Motorhome) of your institute and to get myself apprised about the activities and programmes in which your divine institute is currently engaged. I was delighted to scan some of the publications of the institute and was very happy to note that the institute is engaged in creating a better understanding of science, religion, and spirituality. . . . We, at Delhi College of Engineering, shall be delighted to arrange your seminar at our institute so that the members of faculty and students of this premier institute could be benefited from interactions with you. . . . I shall be grateful if you can kindly benefit us by agreeing to deliver a seminar on “Science and Spirituality” on a date convenient to you during your forthcoming visit to Delhi.  
— Prof. P.B. Sharma

I strongly believe that we all have divinity within ourselves. This divinity is the symbol of spiritualism. Integration of science and spiritualism helps us to balance ourselves. Therefore spiritualism is the key to Mental Health.

My hearty congratulations to Bhaktivedanta Institute, Kolkata, for their noble cause in imparting spiritual awareness in different parts of the country, to make this era spiritual in holistic manner.  
— Prof. Abha Singh  
Joint Director, Amity Institute of  
Behavioural and Allied Sciences, Noida, India

Swamiji \[Dr. T. D. Singh\], this morning I have got a few books and journals which are really useful for improving knowledge and quality of life. There is a profundity of thoughts and maybe there are things which ordinary people may not be able to comprehend. There is need to develop this type of literature for the children and the youth. I request that this area may be given adequate attention. If you happen to visit this area, kindly do address our faculty, staff and students.  
— Dr. K. K. Dhir  
Registrar, National Institute of Technology, Jalandhar

It is a matter of great happiness that the Bhaktivedanta Institute is bringing out articles by Nobel Laureates. This authenticates the claim for preaching to layman or blind followers of science who claim themselves as scientists without knowing the fault of their approach. I find these articles very profound and elevating.  
— Prof. Varendu Kumar  
Professor of Commerce, Jalandhar

Dear Dr. T. D. Singh,  
Thank you for the gift of your book *Thoughts on Synthesis of Science and Religion*. . . . I have read almost all articles and feel compelled to reread many articles; I found your articles most interesting, they are superb indeed. If I am fortunate enough to meet you in the future, I would like to discuss your thoughts on the theories of evolution and the origin of life. These topics are very relevant to my own interest as a research scientist in the field of biological chemistry/spectroscopy.  
— Dr. Daulat Patil  
Lawrence Berkeley Lab, Berkeley, CA

Honorable Dr. T. D. Singh,  
I have read it \[*Savijyanam* — Scientific Exploration for a Spiritual Paradigm, Vol.2\] more than three times . . . and I just cannot describe the joy I got every time I read through the pages of any of the articles published therein. As a physicist by profession and also as an interested individual in pursuit of spiritual enlightenment, I find the issues of *Savijyanam* very informative, educative, and real friends to an eager person. Kindly accept my gratefulness for having started this kind of publication based on modern scientific footings from the soil of India, the home of the Vedantic philosophy.  
— Prof. Anil Moharir  
Delhi

As said in the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (6.23):

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of the Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

In this way, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us always be engaged in our humble efforts to assist our International Director, His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami, in fulfilling your instructions for the Bhaktivedanta Institute, so that we may attain some small understanding of the meaning of complete surrender to your divine instructions. Please allow us to make the manifestation of your vision for the Bhaktivedanta Institute our life and soul.

On this most auspicious occasion of your holy appearance day, please bless us so that we will be able to render some insignificant service at your lotus feet.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute branch in Kolkata, India.

## Bratislava Preaching Center

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We are very happy we can celebrate such a really amazing day — this anniversary of your appearance. As with everything, we can do so only because of your mercy. If there were no you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, there would be no Kṛṣṇa consciousness here. Because you are a sincere disciple of your spiritual master, you were able to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world for the benefit of other living entities.

None of us have such power to spread this movement as you did. This is because none of us is as devoted as you to the spiritual master and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. In other words, none of us has such immense love for the spiritual master and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa as you have. And none of us is so fixed up in spreading Lord Caitanya’s movement as you. Everybody is trying to do his or her best, but you are our amazing founder-*ācārya,* and everybody depends on your mercy. We hope we will not lose that mercy, that we will be able to follow the path you showed us, that we will purify our hearts, and that we will be able to please you by our sincere efforts to spread Lord Caitanya’s movement for the benefit of everyone on the planet, according to your wishes.

Thank you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you a thousand times. We are your eternal debtors and the eternal servants of your servants. Thank you for your mercy.

Your devotees in the preaching center in Bratislava, Slovakia.

## Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, let me take the opportunity to express my most profound gratitude to you. I pay my prostrated obeisances to you, and I thank the Lord for sending me to you and for receiving so much divine mercy from you.

Thirty years ago, at the time of our first meeting, I was looking for my identity and for the meaning of life. I was existentially lost, and my only hope was to know God and somehow harmonize with Him. My trip to India was then purposefully aimed at this goal, and the result was reaching your lotus feet.

You are a pure being, compassionate and merciful by nature; since you possess an ocean of exalted spiritual qualities, you are so dear to Śrī Kṛṣṇa that you were able to sow in me the seed of your devotion to Him. What I sensed at the time was your relation to God, your profound devotion, immaculate purity, and exceptional expertise in representing, serving, and loving Him. Thus I accepted you, and from the start you created the connection between me and Him, letting devotion to Him germinate in my heart.

Over the years, I’ve understood with certainty that my spiritual life has been awakened and supported by the devotional seed that you so efficaciously planted in me at the time. I feel that deciding to meet you and then immediately accepting you in my heart as my divine master were the most significant choices in my life. Choices that, though never failing in any circumstance, have been sometimes challenged and revived by life’s events. Through time, more than once I’ve found myself reflecting on what were the external concomitant causes and mental processes by which I trusted myself to you with such faith and dedication from the start.

Looking back, I understood that accepting you as guru, dedicating my life to you, and serving in your project were not certainly fully rational undertakings.

Yet it wasn¹t mere charm, since I was aware of the greatness of your teachings and of how practical were the solutions you suggested to solve individual and collective problems in such contentious years. It is still hard for me to evaluate how much it was the heart and how much the intellect, but certainly at the time I didn’t simply use the discerning faculty of reason. Rather, it was an intuitive way that you opened up for me. Let’s say it wasn’t a rational or a sentimental choice, but rather a fruit of the inspiration you spurred. That’s the way it has been down to this very day, thus confirming your devotional purity and operational genius. I’ve more than once tried to rationalize, wondering and analyzing what at the time happened, how through time the certainty of my choice built up and how the acceptance of the guru-disciple relationship matured, how your teachings structured up in my emotional and cognitive patterns, so much so as to orient the government of my life in various fields: religion, politics, science, family, profession.

Which were the relations between psyche and consciousness, between faith and reason, between spiritual ideal and phenomenal world, are objects of realization still in progress. The comprehension I’ve reached up to now: it has been the perfect combination which in your operational genius you were able to confer upon my relation between spiritual vision and practical application in the world, so as to be always inspired by the first and concrete-coherent by virtue of the second.

When you left this world, only fifteen months had passed since our first meeting and just six months since the sacred *dīkṣā,* and thus I was very soon left a spiritual orphan.

Fortunately, eleven years before disappearing from our sight, you had founded and developed the ISKCON movement, leaving us an inestimable heritage of sacred texts, temples, life testimonies, and theoretical classes on God reality and on the forms of the Absolute, all transcendental instruments by virtue of which I could survive and develop spiritually. But the liveliest and most dynamic part of your work was the creation of a spiritual family on an international basis, a large Vaiṣṇava family with spiritually active members all over the world. I could also grow up spiritually thanks to the care of your wonderful devotees, my godsisters and godbrothers, and for some of them I developed such a strong affection that they still represent the most important affective bonds in my life. After your disappearance, apart from cherishing your written and oral teachings, it was they who educated and engaged me in devotional activities, and it is therefore to them that I address my eternal recognition and gratitude.

Forty years ago in New York you founded the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, known as ISKCON, and I am greatly thankful to you for this. The start was on the quiet, the co-founders being boys who barely knew what they were doing, and the world didn’t notice that you had planted a tree that could potentially extend its beneficial roots and branches everywhere, since *bhakti,* being a universal yearning in the heart of all human beings, knows no limits, neither in time nor in space.

With the desire to always better serve your universal spiritual teaching and extend and consolidate it in time and space, I ask you to grant me the strength to face with faith, joy, and vigor all those challenges that the human adventure sets up for all, including devotees.

Your servants at Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta.  
(written by Matsya Avatār Dāsa)

## Columbus Kṛṣṇa House

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our most humble and prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. On this day we are feeling a most unbearable, intense separation from you. It is with great difficulty that we write these simple words trying to explain and share the most sacred gift you have bestowed on our humble and insignificant existence.

In this desertlike material world, the gift of your loving shelter is the only source of mercy that provides any relief. All elements of relief have your cooling and protective lotus feet within. We are indeed nothing without the salve of your guidance and direction.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are fallen and solely at your mercy. Please be kind upon our fallen selves and drag us back to the cooling shade of your lotus feet. Through your unparalleled kindness and true love, you have shown us the purpose of our lives. You have personally spoken the narration of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Govinda, who is one without a second and has no beginning or end. It is not at all amazing that great, merciful souls such as yourself, whose minds are always absorbed in the infallible Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, show unlimited mercy to the foolish conditioned souls, who are tormented by the problems of material life.

We will, with all our heart and soul and hoping against hope, try again and again to follow your instructions as we struggle in the battle of daily challenges. Please give us the strength to fight this war and push against the forces of *māyā* so that we may somehow or other become fit to serve your mission. Please give us the strength to recognize and overcome the weakness of mind and heart. Help us to deflect and defeat our false pride and the extreme tendency to find faults in others so that we may serve the holy Vaiṣṇava devotees and the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Please help us to see only the faults within ourselves so that we may improve ourselves and fulfill your mission in spreading the glories of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Our most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our hearts are wilting and severely pained because we miss you terribly. Please help us to remember that you are always here in your words and in our hearts, so that one day we will achieve true happiness by seeing your beautiful, divine, glorious lotuslike form again. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless all the living entities, both divine and demonic, that exist within this world and in our hearts. Through your blessings, we can advance to the stage of remembering that our true duty in life is to love the Lord and his blessed devotees.

For insignificant and unqualified souls such as us there is no other way, there is no other way, there is no other way to develop *bhakti* than to fall at your sweet, sweet lotus feet again and again, begging for shelter and service to Your Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda! All Glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! *Hari-nāma saṅkīrtana-kī jaya*! Haribol! Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your lowly fallen servants at Columbus Kṛṣṇa House, Ohio, USA.  
(written by Kāmagiri Dāsa)

## Festival of India (Denmark)

*nama om visnu-pādaya krsna-presthaya bhu-tale*  
*srimate bhaktivedanta-svaminn iti namine*

*namas te sarasvate deve gaura-vani-pracarine*  
*nirvisesa-sunyavadi-pascatya-desa-tarine*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Thank you very much for once again giving us the opportunity to experience how the Supreme Lord arranges yet another festival in Denmark. With the Festival of India, we are trying to pass on your gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the people, and with your blessings we are this year planning to expand the Festival of India to Sweden and Norway as well. During the festival last year, five to six thousand people participated, and we distributed more than seventeen hundred plates of delicious *prasādam*. It was a most wonderful experience for everyone.

Here are some comments on the festival:

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
I was very happy to participate in the festival of India on the Town Hall Square in Copenhagen. We had the best location — right before the town hall — and therefore reached a very big number of people, who watched with great interest. The program was very tastefully and carefully put together: Purely spiritual programs, together with entertaining programs which were catching the interest of a vast audience. There were *bhajans*, *kīrtanas*, lecture, *prasādam,* as well as Bharat Natyam Indian dance, theatre and a very powerful devotee band Dhira, who spoke the language of the young people. The next day in the temple I saw many happy faces: Devotees as well as newcomers were clearly enlivened. I spoke to one person who made his first contact. He was very together — had strong spiritual inclinations — and is just the person who we want to attract to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This was his comment: “I came by and I had to stay, because the festival was so attractive.” I believe that he expressed most genuinely what the festival was: A Festival of Inspiration.  
Wishing you all the best and lots of spiritual strength,  
Śacīnandana Swami

The Festival of India is a beautiful event celebrating life, and I felt privileged being asked to make the opening speech on behalf of the Danish Vegetarian Society. It was a feast for all senses — colors, flowers, *pūjā*, music, mantras, chanting, smells of incense, and the taste of spicy yet wonderful *sattvic* food, which everybody *had* to like. . . .  
Thank you!  
Yours sincerely,  
Britt Søndergaard  
President and Chairman of the Danish Vegetarian Union

Please engage us eternally in this most ecstatic festival promotion.

Your aspiring servants on the Festival of India crew in Denmark.

## Festival of India (USA)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this day we try to glorify you. By your desire to serve and please your spiritual master, and your unflinching faith in and devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you have come and given us hope: hope of an actual tomorrow that doesn’t just bear pain and suffering, hope of something greater than ourselves. You have given us hope of a reality after which we are all hankering, hope of a place comprised of the everlasting blissful understanding of our true self. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given that which cannot be taken away.

No longer fish out of water with no place to go, we bow down to your lotus feet. Now equipped with sword in hand, we stand ready to fight the battle within ourselves and expand one festival at a time.

Your servants on the Festival of India crew in the USA.  
(written by Premāñjana Dāsa)

## Global Varṇāśrama Educational, Social, and Community Organization (GLOVESCO)

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I would like to share some of the developments taking place with our humble efforts at promoting *varṇāśrama* communities and *varṇāśrama* education through the organization of GLOVESCO. Although the topic of *varṇāśrama* remains somewhat clouded and unclear for many devotees, I am always enthused and inspired to meet devotees who show some interest in the subject. I am convinced that in due course of time more clarity and more certainty will come about in this yet-undeveloped aspect of your mission.

We cannot properly glorify you without also glorifying your transcendental vision for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the medium of *varṇāśrama* communities. It is because of your causeless mercy and transcendental wisdom that you have given us so many important instructions on the need to introduce both *varṇāśrama* communities and *varṇāśrama* education, which are intimately connected.

In looking at the VedaBase, we find no less than twenty-three hundred references for the word *varṇāśrama*. For those who don’t have access to the VedaBase, the following breakdown of references helps demonstrate how much emphasis you personally gave to *varṇāśrama*:

*Bhagavad-gītā* 014  
*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 233  
*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* 039  
Other Books 092  
Lectures 353  
Conversations 210  
Correspondence 019  
Writings of Past *ācāryas* 081  
Other Scriptures 032

TOTAL 1073 (the balance from other sources)

GLOVESCO is still in its infancy. However, this last year we have been able to acquire a piece of land in South India that we have registered in the name of GLOVESCO and that is dedicated to promoting the principles of *varṇāśrama*. The name of this project is Sahyādri Śrī Kṛṣṇa Balarāma Kṣetra and is located near the famed Uḍupī Kṛṣṇa temple in South Karnataka.

By your kind mercy we have been able to build up a small team of devotees who understand the need and importance of introducing *varṇāśrama* communities. To help spread more efficiently the understanding of *varṇāśrama*, we have registered a new website under the name www.varnasrama.org, which is dedicated to sharing information about *varṇāśrama* developments around the world. Here are some unsolicited statements about our new website:

Kṛṣṇa dasa

Just found this website by accident! I am an old friend of Paramānanda, who told me to look for a devotee son of Hare Kṛṣṇa Dāsī who is working on *varṇāśrama* in the Southern Americas. I think I found it! Keep up the “good” work! If you would like, visit our Cow Protection Page: www.protectacow.typepad.com. \[Posted 8 March 2006\]

Yogita

Hare Kṛṣṇa to all the devotees who have made an effort to construct the site. It’s a beautiful site full of great knowledge. But anyways if I can be a part of it please let me know. Thank you. \[Posted 8 March 2006\]

Anamika

I am amazed to see a great collection of articles and pictures on *varṇāśrama*. . . . could not find this anywhere on the net. I want to become part of this team. Well done and my best wishes. \[Posted 26 February 2006\]

Rajaram

The Website looks AWESOME! Congratulations to all wonderful devotees involved in making such a lovely website! Haribol!! \[Posted 16 February 2006\]

Ankir Dua

Varnasrama.org is good collection of information, graphically also it’s a nice website. It’s a great work done by Web-team. Hare Kṛṣṇa. \[Posted 11 February 2006\]

Kalyani

A great site. Lucky to have found it. Hare Kṛṣṇa. Kalyani. Posted \[Posted 5 February 2006\]

Rahul

Suvrat groups of individuals who have developed such a vision and understanding. Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Founder-*Ācārya* (Preceptor) of ISKCON, remains the main inspirational force and guide behind our humble attempt to promote *varṇāśrama* development. One of our goals is to provide as much information. \[Posted 7 January 2006\]

The following is but a small section from your most important essay, “Conception of Gītā-nāgarī.” If only more devotees could attentively study this essay, which you wrote even before coming to America, the subject matter of *varṇāśrama* would more easily be understood and applied.

In the following section (portions of which have been omitted), in referring to the personal example of Gandhi, you stress four important activities to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, all of which you have introduced within your ISKCON society: 1. Book distribution and regular hearing of scriptures; 2. Deity worship and temple attendance; 3. *Dīkṣā*, initiation, for those who are more committed; and 4. *Varṇāśrama* social development and education. Of these four, however, *varṇāśrama*, in particular, has yet to flourish. (The full text is available in the VedaBase).

**From** “**Conception of Gītā-nāgarī,” by Śrīla Prabhupāda:**

Leaving aside all \[Gandhi’s\] other activities in the political field, the four following principles, which he adopted sincerely in his mature old age, are in essence derived from the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā* for practical application in the matter of social and spiritual upliftment of the present order of things. They are as follows:

1\. Leaders and politicians may take lessons from the life of Mahatma Gandhi, who was undoubtedly a great and busy politician, with respect to his daily evening prayer meeting and regular recitation of the text of *Bhagavad-gītā*. He followed this principle of life most faithfully, even up to the last moment of his lamented life. . . .

2\. The temple entry movement of Mahatma Gandhi is another attempt to deliver people in general from spiritual degradation, and by such movement, the mass of people can be saved from gliding down to the lowest status of demoniac life. Even when he was at Noakhali during the dark hours of the partition days, he never hesitated to reinstall the deities of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and by such examples, he had done immense good for the people in general. Leaders and politicians may take lessons from this particular incident of his life. . . .

3\. Mahatma Gandhi started the Harijan movement in order to raise the status of the people fallen either by social injustice or otherwise. But such pious movement may not be restricted only amongst the Bhangis and Chamars who are proclaimed as such for their unclean habits or in some cases by social injustice, but such movement may be extended amongst those also who are passing in the society as *brāhmaṇas* and *kṣatriyas,* etc., but in practice their mentality is more degraded than the lowest of the Bhangis and Chamars. Harijan means the associate of God Himself. Chamars and Bhangis, or for that matter, people who are of the mentality of Chamars and Bhangis both in habit and dealings, can never be promoted to the position of Harijans unless and until such persons are helped to go up to the place of the Harijans by the approved method of rules made by actual Harijans such as Arjuna, Prahlāda, Dhruva Mahārāja, Yāmarāja, Bhīṣma, Janaka, Nārada, etc. . . .

4\. Mahatma Gandhi did not approve of the system of birthright castes as prevailing in India, and there is ample support for this movement of casteless society in the *Bhagavad-gītā* in its own way. Casteless society does not mean that there will be no section or subsection of social division. Without such division of social order no living society can be conceived, but there may not be any such section or subsection simply for the matter of birthright accident. A *brāhmaṇa’s* son has the right to become a *brāhmaṇa* provided he has the rightful qualifications of a *brāhmaṇa,* otherwise he cannot be placed in the exalted position of a *brāhmaṇa*. That is the verdict of all revealed scriptures. . . . From the Gītā-nāgarī this universal Truth must be propagated systematically, so that REAL HUMAN SOCIETY may be established for the benefit of all, dividing the categories of men according to natural modes of nature. Such social order on the basis of the *Bhagavad-gītā* all over the world may be called the institution of natural caste system or the casteless society. In that institution of natural caste system all divisions of men will be engaged in one transcendental business of spiritual community with equal status of life and with equal importance of co-operative value as much as the different parts of the one whole body have different functions only but qualitatively they are one and the same. In that institution of spiritual social order everyone will have equal right to promote himself to the higher status of life without any malicious competition, and as such education and culture will be open door for all concerned as much as the varsity of all state is open for all with equal status of educational right. Casteless society will, of course, never allow to place on the same category of a high-court judge and the laborer unless such society will have gone in to madness of culture. On the contrary by establishment of such casteless society as it is stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā* nobody will be grudging if anyone is recognized as the qualified *brāhmaṇa* on the value of merit only and not on the basis of birthright claim. Such casteless society is reasonable and acceptable by one and all in the world.

By gloriying *varṇāśrama* and the profound ideology on which it is based, in particular *daivī-varṇāśrama*, I aim simply at glorifying you directly, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please help us understand more quickly the urgent need to establish *varṇāśrama,* and kindly help us see how this will directly help resolve many of the social ills and shortcomings both of the society at large as well as our own.

Thank you for allowing us to engage in your service of helping to spread the *varṇāśrama* mission.

Your servants at the Global Varṇāśrama Educational, Social, and Community Organization.  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The experience I am about to relate happened on the ISCOWP farm. I was in charge of the cows during the winter when my parents were away in the mall earning enough money to pay our personal bills. They are disciples of yours. They have taught me how to care for cows. I am most thankful to you and them for this cow *sevā* knowledge because of the happiness that I, and others, can experience while caring for the cows and oxen.

When I went to check the cows Saturday morning (December 3), the first thing I noticed was that Balarāma, Gauravāṇī, Viśākhā, and Rādhā-Śyāma were out eating hay in the hay barn and grass in the driveway. As I looked for the break in the fence where they got out, I looked up and saw a cow lying down and not moving in the barn. I cried out to Kṛṣṇa and started praying that whoever was down was just sleeping.

When a cow is dying, we like to give them some holy water and have a tape of you singing *bhajan* 24/7. I did not have time to do either. I am also very attached to all the cows.

I rushed into the new barn where the cow was down and discovered it was Veda. He was barely alive.
