The previous spring the vet had said that Veda had a wasting disease called Johne’s disease and that it would just be a matter of time before his passing. The vet knows we do not put any of our animals down, but I thought he would last longer. The day before, Veda seemed okay, not well but ok — not at death’s door. I started singing to him the Nṛsiṁha prayers and checked to make sure that he was not stuck but was in fact was actually down, and that nothing external was stopping him from getting up. At this stage, he was also very weak and could not lift his head. I went and got bedding to make him more comfortable and also hay and water to see if he would eat or drink, which he did not. I could do nothing more at that moment.

After I did this I gave Vraja his medicine for his arthritis, fed the cows in the loafing shed and old barn, went back outside to fix the fence, and put the four cows back inside. After calling my parents to find out where the CD player and blankets were, I collected these items, covered Veda with the blankets, and turned on the CD player to continuous play. As I was doing this, I noticed that Kṛṣṇa and Viśākhā were sniffing Veda. Then they each went to stand guard on either side of Veda.

I had to run back to the house to get the Yamunā water and a calf’s milk-bottle to help me get water into Veda. As I approached the barn, I noticed a crowd of cows around Veda; some were just standing there, and others were smelling him. My first thought was that he was dead, but then I saw that he had just taken a breath and I was relieved like you would not believe.

I gave Veda Yamunā water, of which he only took a few tablespoons. While talking to him, I looked up and noticed that the gate on the loafing barn was hanging strangely. I went to the loafing barn and discovered that the gate was indeed on the ground, that the inside gate was also broken, and that all the cows with medical problems were not there but down below the barn, hanging with some of the other cows. At this point, I freaked cause I knew I could not move Veda to a better spot, since that would kill him, and I knew that I could not fix the gate and get the cows back inside by myself. I contacted my brother, and he agreed to come early the next morning, since he was already traveling too far away to come right then.

I went back to the new barn and spent time with Veda. At this time, he had about eight cows surrounding him, so I went and starting feeding out more hay. About twenty minutes later Ujjvala started mooing strangely at Veda. I dropped what I was doing to see what was going on. Ujjvala and Kṛṣṇa were trying to get Veda up. Unfortunately it did not work. I spent some more time with them and then finished my chores in the barn.

The next morning we had a coating of ice on everything, about one half inch, and it was extremely cold like it had been for the previous week (in the teens). I suited up and went to the barn. As I walked to the barn, I noticed that Yamunā was standing awkwardly and was acting strangely. I went down to the lower barnyard before I checked everyone in the barn, and helped her get into a better spot. I tried to get her moving toward the barn, hoping to get her up by the silo so once the gate was fixed it would be easier to get her into the loafing barn. She did not want to go up there but kept on going toward my house.

At this time I went up into the barn to check on Veda and discovered that he had passed in the night; he was cold to the touch. I covered his face, turned around, and saw his mother, Gaṅgā, watching me with a sorrowful look on her face. I went over to her and told her how sorry I was about her son. Throughout the day she was always within ten feet of him. At this time, I noticed that the whole herd was also sad, so I took the time to talk with them and to walk around and pet everyone. While I was doing this, the cows kept on checking on Veda by smelling him and trying to uncover him.

While I was waiting for my brother, I did my normal chores of feeding everyone and giving Vraja his grain and medicine and Gītā his grain. Now Gītā was not that happy that Vraja was in the barn with him and pushed him down while Vraja was walking by. This really scared me, since Vraja has bad arthritis in one of his back legs and was recovering from an injury in the other back leg. Some-how, I managed to separate the two of them and get Vraja temporally locked back into the loafing barn, which was not yet that secure. I then heard a vehicle coming, and it was my brother Baladeva. I explained what had hap-pened and showed him what the damage was, and then we decided what we were going to fix and how. While we were doing this and gath-ering the tools we needed, Asha got it in her head that she wanted back into the loafing barn where Vraja was, and she was going to get in there all by herself, thank you very much! When Bala and I got back to the barn after gathering our tools, we discovered that Asha was with Vraja and they were both happy with that. Asha was born with bad hips and walks sideways down hills.

We fixed the loafing barn so that it would be really hard for them to take down the gate. Now we had to get the cows back in the barn. Now you have to remember everything had about half an inch of ice on it, so it was difficult to walk and do anything. Somehow we man-aged. Getting the cows back in is al-ways the hardest part, be-cause you do not want everyone in, only a select few. This took us about an hour, but they were all happy once they realized what we were up to. However, they only realized that once they were in the barn.

It takes at least a tractor or backhoe to move a dead cow, and a backhoe to bury her or him in the cow burial area on the farm. We do not have a tractor or backhoe, but the temple sends one with Ray, who has been doing the tem-ple farm work for over fifteen years. He was not available on Sunday but came up the following morning.

When Ray came up on the tractor, we removed Veda from the barn and took him to the burial spot. We realized it was too slippery for the backhoe to come up, so we talked about it, and Ray believed that he could bury him with the tractor. However, he did not have the bucket on the tractor and would have to go back to the main barn to get the bucket. At this time we decided to restock the barns with round bales of hay. Since he already had the hay bale attachment on the tractor, he needed to do that. We restocked, and it took us about four hours. It was like three o’clock, and he would not have enough time to go back to the barn, come back up, and bury him before dark. It was decided he would do it the next day. Also, by this time I was freezing, since I had been at the barn since 9:30 am, and it was only about twenty degrees, not count-ing the wind.

We buried Veda today, December 6. May he rest in peace and have fun playing with Kṛṣṇa and the cowherd boys.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the opportunity in this lifetime to experience the nectar and transcendental anxiety of taking care of and servicing cows in Kali-yuga.

Your servants at ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture, International Society for Cow Protection.  
(written by Lakṣmī Devī Dasi)

## ISKCON Central Office of Child Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This very special and holy day, Vyāsa-pūjā, gives us an opportunity to reflect upon everything you have given us. Every day the *darśana* of the beautiful forms of Rādhā-Śyāmasundara and Gaura-Nitāi is a source of amazement, gracing our lives with the source of true spiritual relationship. How could we not relish the association of devotees, all unique souls, yet all dedicated to the same principles, the same philosophy, and the same person — Your Divine Grace? The holy name resounds throughout the temple room, fills our hearts, and deeply connects us to that Supreme Lord, who is nondifferent from His name. How can we understand our great fortune in experiencing these gifts every day?

But these transcendental blessings did not somehow occur by chance in the middle of North Florida, or Hungary, or Argentina, or anywhere throughout the world where Kṛṣṇa consciousness is found, any more than the extraordinary and variegated cosmic creation manifested at random, by chance. It was by your will, by your faith in guru and Kṛṣṇa, and by the empowerment of Lord Nityānanda and Lord Caitanya that you brought this gift to us, and we are now privileged to practice the path of pure *bhakti*.

You gave us the holy name and the *Bhāgavatam* and the Deity, but you also gave us a society. You planted the seeds of a culture that would eventually encompass the world. You gave us a way to live as civilized human beings, a path of *dharma*, and a godly life. You taught us the real meaning of loving relationships, and how to conduct our family life and raise our children as service to Kṛṣṇa.

The devotee children were very dear to you. You saw them as the hope for the future, and you delighted in their often clumsy, but always sincere, attempts to worship Kṛṣṇa. In the Child Protection Office, we try to preserve the fledgling devotion of these young Vaiṣṇavas and see that they are raised, taught, and treated in the way that they deserve. To see them as anything other than the great souls that they are, to cause harm and suffering, and to betray the trust that Kṛṣṇa has given us is to commit great *aparādhas*. That we need this office is in some ways an admission of our failure to correctly implement your mission and vision. Nonetheless, we must go forward, correcting our mistakes, working to do better, and continuing our efforts to act as the Vaiṣṇavas you taught us to be.

You alone brought the *saṅkīrtana* movement first to the West, and then all over the world. Your disciples have continued this great service of spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement throughout the world, and thus they further glorify you. Let us also glorify you by creating a Vaiṣṇava society that can give support and strength to the devotees, and where our children can grow and worship Kṛṣṇa with love and protection. Let us create a society of devotees that would make you proud.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON’s Central Office of Child Protection.  
(written by Tamohara Dāsa)

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It was Chicago 1975. You were coming out of the temple, and in front of you on either side were devotees on Emerson St., cheering, laughing, jumping, and crying, and you were smiling. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were smiling so beautifully.

Then flowers. There were flowers falling. The ladies were dropping them from the second floor windows.

Brahmānanda Swami said, “Just see, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the demigods are showering flowers on you.”

At that you became grave and looked at the transcendental crowd, some shouting “*Jaya* Prabhupāda!”, others with their heads on the ground, and you looked from one to another slowly, as if you were looking into the hearts of each devotee, one after the other, relishing their sincere attempts at service in your *saṅkīrtana* army, and you said, “Yes, they are all demigods.”

How fortunate we were to take birth in this place so we could meet you in our adolescence! You are the flower of your society. You told us how you loved us. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for picking us up when we were wandering aimlessly in a tragic prison/slaughterhouse

We were imprisoned by freedom. Yes, we had the free will to make all the wrong choices, and we did.

In court the judge does not accept ignorance as an excuse. But who blessed us with *rāja-vidyā,* that liberating science of the self? Śrīla Prabhupāda, I fall at your feet. You saved me. How kind you are! How hard you work for the conditioned souls. You’re trying everything to save us!

You have given us the keys to real freedom: transcendental knowledge, your dynamic ISKCON family, *prasādam,* Vaiṣṇava *saṅga,* teaching, work, festivals, Deity worship, everything.

You have asked us to take up the minimum discipline in spiritual life — the restrictions you called “the regulative principles of freedom.” Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for relentlessly working on our behalf.

Our service to you is our recreation. We will never stop teaching — *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*.

Every day at the ISKCON Prison Ministry we get letters from inmates who are thrilled at the prospect of growing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Who is responsible for this? *Mahā-patita-pāvana* Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your books transform hearts. You didn’t start this movement for first-class men; you started it for fifth-class and tenth-class men!

Your magic is that you transform them into lotuslike devotees.

We just received these letters:

**My Time to Change**

I just got a packet of books from you with beyond *Birth And Death, Back to Godhead,* and others.

I just got your letter, the *Freedom Newsletter,* and a photo of A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He is special. Life is going good, and I chant and I pray. Like you said in your letter, “A moment is precious; don’t waste any.” This is my time to change. I am doing just that.  
Bhakta Tom Biden  
Raiford, Florida.

The next is from former LA gang member.

**The Perfect Fit**

My story is a lot like yours. I began to ask questions like “Who is God,” and when I got incomplete answers, something deep within me fueled my passions to find the answer.

So yes, from a mixture of unarticulated resentments masked in God’s whispers I ended up on your doorstep.

And here I am. ISKCON Prison Ministry seems to be the perfect fit. There is so much that I don’t know about Lord Kṛṣṇa. But what I know is that all the teachings feel right.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s conversation with Dr. Christian Hauser about “Dirty Illusions” touched my heart. It agrees with my soul. And even though I don’t understand everything about Kṛṣṇa, I am interested in learning more. I hung the Śrī Śrī Śikṣāṣṭaka on my wall.

It reads like a beautiful prayer. The part that says “O almighty lord, I have no desire to accumulate wealth, nor do I want beautiful women, nor do I want any number of followers. I only want your causeless devotional service birth after birth” felt as if it was copied off the tablet of my heart. Thank you for the photographs of Kṛṣṇa’s cows, the Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa Deities, and the Kṛṣṇa devotees chanting the holy names.  
Rodman Brewster  
Alascadero, CA

The next letter is from an inmate who’s never met a devotee face to face in his life.

**Before Prabhupāda**

Before Prabhupāda I knew nothing of Bhagavān. I knew only about Brahman, and then not everything about that. *Mat paraṁ brahma*. Kṛṣṇa is above Brahman. The *brahmajyoti* is the rays emanating from the supreme spiritual plane, Goloka Vṛndāvana. Kṛṣṇa is addressed as *samañjasa,* “O source of opportunities” (SB 6.11.25). Another thing I have observed — *jñāna-yoga*. This wisdom culminates in Kṛṣṇa. I have learned many things. Kṛṣṇa says that from Him comes *jñāna* (BG 15.15). *Nārāyaṇa-paraṁ jñānaṁ,* transcendental knowledge is for getting a glimpse of Nārāyaṇa (SB 2.5.16). This is really an exact quote from the First Canto: *Vāsudeva-paraṁ jñānaṁ* (SB 1.2.29). Two symptoms become manifest by rendering *bhakti: vairāgya* (detachment) and knowledge, according to SB 1.2.7. This same *śloka* is given nearly verbatim in the Third Canto, 3.32.23. *Nārada-bhakti-sūtra* 26 states *phala-rūpatvāt* — *bhakti* is the fruit of all endeavor. In the same text, Sūtra 30, it is confirmed that *bhakti* is its own fruit.

Only those who are uninformed say the devotees are anti-intellectual. In the *śāstras* none of the Lord’s devotees are simple: Rādhā—just see Her exalted position! Uddhava is greatly learned. Nārada, the Kumāras, Śiva, Kṛṣṇa, etc., are all topmost intellectuals.

I have become more knowledgeable being in *bhakti-yoga* than my previous practices.

Look at the worship of the Gosvāmīs. Mantra 14 of the *Īśopaniṣad* states we should know (*veda*) both *sambhūti* (Kṛṣṇa) and *vināśa* (the temporary material manifestation) side by side. According to the *Vedānta-sūtra, sambhūta* is the source of birth and sustenance, as well as the reservoir that remains after annihilation. So, in other words . . . Kṛṣṇa.

This place is full of all sorts of “evil.” I see the effects of materialism and ignorance daily. This life is for *tapa.* Kṛṣṇa says this world is pervaded by the threefold miseries. He also says a person delighting in the senses lives in vain. No happiness is possible here. The word *penitentiary* comes from the root *penance* (*tapa*), so although I am here it does not check Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy. Is Kṛṣṇa’s arm too short to save me here? Kṛṣṇa is not slack concerning His promises. I cannot stress enough how my situation has been both a blessing and a curse. In spite of one’s surroundings, happiness can be had simply by concentrating on Kṛṣṇa (BG 9.22).

One whose happiness is within is the perfect mystic (BG 5.24).  
David Lightsey  
Angola, LA

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t know Bhīma, Arjuna, or Bhīṣma, but I can see you. You’re the most brave spiritual warrior. Your *daṇḍa* is the symbol of knowledge and detachment and is your sword with which you cut the hard knot of attachment. Your example is the inspiration, the shield deflecting illusion. Wherever you go, you conquer. Wherever you go, there is victory, glory, strength, hope, elegance, and majesty. You award freedom. I thank you for that. Hardik Kaswala thanks you, and so does Jeremiah Henthorn, Harry Sullivan, and a thousand other IPM inmates, here and around the world. They love you because they know you love them. You are dear to the ruffians, and you gaze on these rogue demigods with love and compassion. Yes, they are all demigods waiting to go home. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for never giving up on us. We have abandoned you thousands of times, but you never gave up on us, never. You never gave up on us. You never gave up on us.

Your servants at the ISKCON Prison Ministry.  
(written by Candraśekhara Dāsa)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each year is a page  
Dedicated to you, the humble sage,  
Another opportunity to write  
About your glories and your light.  
You illuminated a darkened sky  
And inspired our hardened hearts to cry.  
You’ve given us back a spiritual life,  
Relieved the pangs of material strife.  
Every day when we chant the holy names  
We appeal to you to relieve us of our shame.  
You are the portal of devotion,  
The reservoir of transcendental emotion.  
Many other great devotees have come and left,  
Yet none so far has given such an accessible gift.  
There will always be another great Vaiṣṇava,  
But you will always be the *sampradāya ācārya.*  
We’re worried that your contribution will be minimized,  
Your efforts to preach to *mlecchas* be less emphasized.  
Disciples and granddisciples will continue your mission,  
Though nothing could have happened without your intercession.  
With the legacy of your unquestioned pure association in mind,  
ITV has endeavored to preserve your films and videos for all time.  
We are nearly finished with DVD Library version three,  
Refined, expanded, and with many more languages for all to see.  
Putting aside differences and finding common ground,  
A team of impassioned devotees have assured that your moving images will stay around.  
We already get letters from folks who want more association,  
So we direct them to your books, which are always the foundation.  
What mercy to have gotten a chance to serve you in this way!  
You have provided all the reasons, but we stray from day to day.  
Fortunately you remain the axis of the wheel.  
At least that’s how the devotees at ISKCON Television feel.  
We dedicate to you the first annual Vaiṣṇava Film/Video Competition.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda — Vaikuṇṭha man and executive producer of the exhibition.  
You started a revolution by funding ISKCON Cinema.  
Now the field of Hare Kṛṣṇa filmmakers seems to be unlimited.  
We pray that you continue to guide us in our quest  
To always be of service to you, the honored guest.  
Your appearance day is the mark of a new dawn,  
When material desires fade in the light of your love song:  
*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your fallen servants at ITV.

## Katikati

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and prayerful obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

One time when a devotee asked what pleased you, you answered by saying, “I am happy when I see you struggling. ” You know precisely what it means to be engaged in the battle to save souls, our own and others, from the powerful jaws of the material energy.

To pursue the transcendental path is more or less to declare war on the illusory energy. Consequently, whenever a person tries to escape the clutches of the illusory energy, she tries to defeat the practitioner by various allurements. A conditioned soul is already allured by the modes of material energy, and there is every chance of being allured again, even while performing transcendental disciplines. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 6. 37, purport\]

Your description of struggling is to be understood as the mercy of Kṛṣṇa, and when He is satisfied by your giving energy in this way just to please Him despite all conditions of difficulty, then you need not worry. . . . \[Letter to Tejīyas Dāsa, 2 January 1973\]

One who is struggling against *māyā* is a hero. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4. 29. 82, purport\]

Your own life history of struggling for Kṛṣṇa gives a practical yet powerful example that moves, teaches, and inspires us. You ran the full gamut of struggle — with family, with politics, with business, with godbrothers, with drug-crazed hippies, with health and old age, and ultimately with death itself. You never, ever gave up. Even in Jhansi, when you had to acquiesce to a ladies’ social club, you didn’t take it as defeat, but only a temporary setback. Your teaching by example sustains us. We crave that hope, resolve, and resilience to carry on, to maintain what you established and expand it, gloriously, even in the darkest hours.

You have done what no one else has done, and you continue to radiate Kṛṣṇa’s supreme energy through any and all who take up your charge. Your influence is expanding unlimitedly, and there is no energy (except our own selfish separatist party spirit) that can resist that snowballing effect

We want to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: that is the purpose and the perfection of our lives. And we, your dedicated followers here in the Bay of Plenty, New Zealand, recommit ourselves again to “raising the bar” in our struggles to give up all struggles except those that make us heroes in your army. In spite of our feeling completely unable and unqualified, we accept your firm and loving leadership, and submit ourselves to be your empowered “monkey warriors,” just like those in Śrī Hanumān’s army, performing miracles in the service of the Supreme Lord!

The lines of engagement have been drawn by you. In your instructions you have given us *all* the requisite ammunition to win the war. Your followers are engaged in combat, arrayed across the planet, far and wide, gaining ground on many fronts. We want desperately to be your instruments in winning the hearts and minds of all, starting with our own. We seem to be learning from our blunders, and to be waking to your call for cooperative mutual service to each other and the world.

Every year in this, your resplendent Vyāsa-pūjā book, we offer to you our victories and our defeats, our doubts and our fears, our hopes and our prayers, our re-dedication and our “battle plans”! This precious outpouring is like *śāstra:* it is the inspired realizations of sincere aspiring Vaiṣṇavas who are surrendered to you, struggling to perfect our service to guru and Kṛṣṇa, sharing the hard-earned lessons we learn in that progressive march back to pure devotional service.

When we offer these deeply considered homages, we stand in front of you, vulnerable, humbled, supplicating, prayerful, putting aside pretense and excuses — and we are re-inspired! Even if we try to hide something, we know we can’t, so our offerings are from our hearts, unabashed, honest — eager to respond to your generosity, to reciprocate with the blessings and encouragement you are giving us every day. We know you love us in the most real, complete, and unsentimental way, and we aspire to love you in the same way, through our service. You have moved mountains for us — and we will do the same for you.

That sweet *rasa* of personal relationship in devotional service is what all entities crave. That is what you have given us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Someone who came from Kṛṣṇa’s original place, living at every moment that boundless pleasure of personal exchange with the most beautiful and dearmost friend, Lord Kṛṣṇa. You related with us from that platform, but in a way to which we could respond. Out of full surrender to and love for your spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, you accepted the “terrible” austerity, the burden of love. You came down to the *madhyama* platform to invite the incorrigible and unqualified to become qualified, to give us access to the inaccessible. You walked amongst us, living your love in this dark world, proving that God exists, here and now, and that *anyone* can know Him, free from doubt.

These heartfelt offerings enliven and enthuse us. They remind us that, *yes*, it is possible to not only endure the struggle but to win, to follow and fulfill your instructions, to actually purify ourselves of *all* our *anarthas,* so that we don’t have to give into doubt, or complacency, or pretense. We can become pure devotees and go back to Godhead in this lifetime! At our initiation at Bury Place, London, in 1971, you told us, “If you simply chant sixteen rounds a day and follow the regulative principles, there is nothing that can keep you from going back to Godhead in this lifetime.” You paused, then raised your finger and said, “And that is a fact!”

Such offerings to you expand to become our power in dealing with struggles, which, you taught, begin within, on the subtle platform.

We work not with our body, actually, but with our mind and intelligence. So if the intelligence and the mind are always engaged in the thought of the Supreme Lord, then naturally the senses are also engaged in His service. Superficially, at least, the activities of the senses remain the same, but the consciousness is changed. \[*Bhagavad-gītā*, Introduction\]

This is the way of changing the habit of the mind. The mind is not to be killed. Mind or desire cannot be stopped, but to develop a desire to function for spiritual realization, the quality of engagement by the mind has to be changed. The mind is the pivot of the active sense organs, and as such if the quality of thinking, feeling, and willing is changed, naturally the quality of actions by the instrumental senses will also change. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.1.17 purport\]

Arjuna shows us the way by honestly admitting that he could not control his mind and senses without adjusting his *attitude* toward Kṛṣṇa, accepting Kṛṣṇa’s will instead of his own. As soon as he did that and chose to use his faculties for Kṛṣṇa, then everything became clear, and not just clear, but joyful and easy! Yes, that incredible Battle of Kurukṣetra was, for Arjuna, “simple and joyfully performed”!

He showed us that first we must *be* — be conscious and open to Kṛṣṇa’s hints in any form. *Then* we can do, or act, rather then doing, or acting, first. Of course, we don’t cease acting — we cannot, even for a moment — but we should strive enthusiastically for the stage when we act only on sattvic and, ultimately, śuddha-sattvic principles. You told us, “First be conscious; then be Kṛṣṇa conscious. ”

And our own lives are like that, a battle. Every day we struggle to win the internal battle. First to be aware of, and then honest about, our tendency to occupy Kṛṣṇa’s place as the supreme knower, doer, enjoyer, and to accept our place as subordinate and dependent. Then we can actively struggle to re-enthrone the real Lord in our minds and hearts, through the proven method of *sādhana-bhakti*. You told us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that action in knowledge has some value, and that we have to work, endeavor, to *try* to give up offenses while chanting the holy names. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you cite your guru mahārāja as saying that “if one hears and chants without trying to give up offenses, he becomes materially attached to sense gratification. ” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 19.160 purport)

We say we want to be your sold-out servants, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but when Kṛṣṇa shows us the ugly, naked, terrible truth of our conditioning and attachments, how do we respond? We can keep pretending: I’ve become very good at it, keeping up an impressive front, over the years and lifetimes. Besides it’s too much trouble, and too scary, to do anything about it — and maybe Kṛṣṇa won’t give me all the help, strength, shelter, relief and happiness that I need in dealing with these anarthas honestly.

If we stick with our *kaniṣṭha* ways, thinking that because I practice externally (without inviting Kṛṣṇa to deal with my weeds), I am well situated, then I may be in danger of identifying my *prākṛta* stage as “transcendental.” I can thus avoid pulling those weeds that may be strangling the creeper of devotion within my heart. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you instructed us that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not a one-sided affair — just thinking of Kṛṣṇa — but that there must be personal responsibility, endeavor, and practical action.

If I allow some part to be hidden, unexposed to the sun of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees’ healing love, then how will I be able to serve my friends, spouse, children, or disciples in an effective way. How can I be the transparent medium that you are calling me to be? I know when Kṛṣṇa shows me some *anartha,* or it embarrassingly rears its ugly head unexpectedly, then that’s His way of telling me to pull a weed or two, or three or four. If I lose my temper, or abuse some privilege, or deviate from the regulative principles, or neglect some needy or dependent soul, I can take it that I still have some work to do. I still need to find a way to purify that nasty quality; I need to accept the help by which I can become completely “unalloyed.”

If I pretend that the *anartha* is not there, am I denying Kṛṣṇa’s hint? Do I *really* believe that He’s in control of everything, and that He is my best friend? I can avoid the inevitable for awhile, but is that painful struggle actually going to help me advance? You want us to give up our envious or resentful or critical or angry or begrudging attitudes toward Kṛṣṇa, which become manifest in our dealings with others, and to replace them with honesty, acceptance, gratitude, appreciation, accountability, submission, servitude — and to accept Him as actually our friend, Who cares about and loves us more than we can know.

We want to become whole persons, self-realized, fully aware of *anything* that might stand in the way of fully pleasing and serving you, our dear master. The *Bhagavad-gītā* says that one who learns to choose only Kṛṣṇa, instead of giving in to *anything* else, achieves the level of Brahman. He achieves the concomitant happiness, freedom, and success, instead of the misery, doubt, frustration, and madness that plague us when we avoid that choice and anchor ourselves in repeating the same old mistakes again and again.

So, unless we resist, it is not an empty, begrudging, or painful struggle, but a fulfilling, joyful, voluntary, and simple one. You have said that if one is suffering in spiritual life, then he’s not really engaged in devotional service and is a rascal. So we need the association of those who have achieved that level in the struggle, to reconcile and practically apply this kind of instruction.

Our lives are dedicated to understanding and choosing only your pleasure. In spite of the apparently overwhelming mountain of material desires and pride, and gaping chasms of lethargy and hopelessness, we are praying to you to continue to offer us this opportunity, even though we know that you will in any case. It’s just to remind ourselves that we don’t need to be doubtful about your eternal presence in your instructions, your *vāṇī*. Oh, what an anomaly: the most unworthy and rebellious offered the topmost boon and redemption! You’ll never give in, never abandon us, and we accept your offer, again, in spite of stumbling and letting go so many times.

Recently, we lost one of your stalwart warriors, His Holiness Bhakti Tīrtha Swami. His example and preaching have had, and continue to have, profound effects. At Bhaktivedanta Manor, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, crying, embraced him and told him his life was successful. Surrendered souls like Mahārāja inspire us in that way, because they are sold out to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In their own ways, they are like you. You were ecstatic with Mahārāja’s surrender and service, and in seeing him expand and “surpass” what you had achieved so far.

We see, Śrīla Prabhupāda, another feature of your connecting us with the *paramparā,* in the powerful influence of these surrendered servants of your servants. They expand your teachings in ways that are sometimes even more accessible or understandable by us, according to our time, place and circumstance, and level or type of understanding. The example of the *śuka,* or parrot, is very apt here. When the parrot bites into the already ripened and sweet fruit, it becomes even sweeter by that bite.

So also, when we hear from souls sold out to you and your mission, we are connected to that power line originating from the Supreme Lord Himself. We are surcharged with *divya-jñāna,* transcendental knowledge, and energized in our own struggle to surrender and serve, and to represent and extend that same transcendental conduit, the *paramparā*. Unless we see examples of this, then our achieving the simple and joyful stage may remain only theoretical, “a nice idea, but . . .”

The way to be enthusiastic is to associate with those who are enthusiastic. *The Nectar of Devotion* instructs us to associate with those more advanced than ourselves. So we are inspired and encouraged in our struggles when we see a “peer” and godbrother or -uncle like Mahārāja, becoming a “beggar” on your inspiration, and in being willing to adjust his own mentality and life in order to serve and please you (and us). His glorious departure from this world gives us confidence that we can also achieve that ourselves!

We had a different kind of departure recently amongst our New Zealand devotee community, a most tragic one. The suicide of a member of the second generation has shaken us to the core. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us to feel the pain, anger, and guilt, and also to somehow move forward. We have been shocked into wondering how this could have happened, into becoming deeply introspective. We ask ourselves, What could I have done? and What will I change in my life, today, that will make a difference for those who are suffering so terribly, indescribably, desperately, alone?

How can we open ourselves to Kṛṣṇa’s love, and thus be so accepting and loving with others that they feel accepted, respected, worthy, and worthwhile, that our example and dealings with them will encourage and enliven and inspire them to open themselves to hope and “recovery”? You instructed us to preach to everyone, and we wonder what you would say to see us putting so much energy into outreach preaching but “out the back door” we’re losing those who have already been engaged, especially these young people, our own devotee children, whom you said have come from very elevated positions already. If we aren’t taking full care of our own, how will we care for others we want to join our family?

The steady *gṛhasthas* have much to offer in this regard. We need each other, and can nurture each other. We may even be like unsung heroes: we commit to stay in close, familiar, intimate relationships, learn how to love our dependents unconditionally, learn how to be there for others — to create the mood of an extended family. This built-in austerity cum training in this *āśrama* makes it most valuable, and glorious. To learn how to deal with everything for Kṛṣṇa — including procreation — and not be attached, or *repulsed,* in a hateful, arrogant, material way. To learn to be in the world but not of it, to be the loving steward for the Lord, exactly as you were, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is, as you taught, real renunciation, better than *sannyāsa*!

One example of the potency of this kind of “internal” preaching — taking care of, supporting, and encouraging each other on the long and narrow path to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, is the wonderful wedding festival we created together here at our Katikati community at the beginning of the year for two of our second generation, Rahul and Rama. Everyone worked together cooperatively, spontaneously, in what became a very unifying and fulfilling project. Even some old broken relationships were rekindled, and we ended up on the front page of the local newspaper!

We struggled, and by the mercy of Lord Caitanya, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and all the sincere devotees, we were successful on all levels.

We thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us real life again, and promise that we will continue this glorious struggle to serve you, forever.

The aspiring servants of your servants in the Western Bay of Plenty (the Katikati-Tauranga yātrā) New Zealand.  
(written by Ātmānanda Dāsa)

## Mayapur Institute for Higher Education (MIHE)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on your most holy Vyāsa-pūjā celebration. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and to the ecstatic legacy that you have left your followers in your books, in your other instructions, and in your ISKCON.

On 20 August 1971, you wrote in a letter,

Throughout the whole world there is no institution to impart education in the matter of spiritual understanding. So we are going to open a big center in Mayapur where this education will be internationally imparted. Students from all parts of the world will go there to take education in this important subject.

You also gave many unrecorded instructions to Tamāl Krishna Goswami, Jayapatāka Swami, and Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu on the importance of establishing Māyāpur University and on strategies for doing so. We are trying our best to fulfill your vision.

When we first started the MIHE, we had trouble getting enough students to provide dynamic forums for learning in our courses. Now, in many of our courses we have to turn devotees away because we just do not have enough space in our classrooms — a nice problem to have.

This problem is evidence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that the emphasis you placed on training and education in the seven purposes of ISKCON, contained within the articles of incorporation for your first ISKCON Temple at 26 Second Avenue, is starting to take hold within your movement. We are gathering so much momentum, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that establishing Mayapur University, which appeared to be an unreachable goal seven years ago, now appears to on the verge of happening.

Please empower us to bring your university into being so that we can fully express, by our service, all of the love we have in our hearts for you.

Your servants at the Mayapur Institute for Higher Education.

## Padayātrā America

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*vāñchā-kalpatarubhyaś ca kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

Another year that we have the opportunity to write from the roads of the world. Last year we wrote from Mexico, and we remember asking for your blessings to be able to write this year from South America. Now, after crossing seven countries by your mercy, the *padayātrā* is now in Colombia, South America! After approximately fifty-two hundred miles of walking, chanting, and preaching, the most important thing we are learning is to depend on your mercy. It is a fact: you are the real preacher, and our only qualification is to learn to follow in your footsteps.

During this year we have seen people from all backgrounds receive the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. When we came to Darien Gap in Panama, which is an impassable jungle, we reached the end of the road, without any clear way how to go from North to South America. Then we saw how, by the strong force of your desire and the desire of our authorities to please you by expanding this program, it became possible for the first time in the *saṅkīrtana* mission for a *padayātrā*to cross from North to South America. Now here we are with the Deities, our original four-legged *padayātrīs*, the devotees, and our mobile temple, crossing a country that is plagued with guerrilla and ultra–right-wing armies fighting each other in the struggle for existence, putting the country in dire need of our taking you around to purify the place.

We have been here less that ten days, and already the preaching is wonderful. The most important television channel has done a very nice interview, and the number four newspaper in all of Latin America has done an article called “Hare Kṛṣṇa Monks Traveling around the World.”

The people know us everywhere. A few days ago, even before any television or news crews came, two persons said, “Oh, I saw you on television.” How? We guessed they’d seen us on international television shows broadcast from Mexico or another Central American country, but we don’t know. Still, the fact remains that thousands of people are getting in touch with *padayātrā*, and that is all to your credit.

Every time the Deities are on television and in the news, or people take literature, or they hear the chanting of the holy names, we can see how you are enjoying our journey and preaching mission here on *padayātrā*. Every day we are on the road is to your glorious credit. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for letting us participate in this most wonderful service! Thank you for letting us see how, by doing *harināma-saṅkīrtana* with the desire to please you, we are having all our bodily designations dispelled and seeing the path open up and become clear to proceed.

We thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because we never feel alone: you are here with us. By seeing all these things to be grateful for, we are trying to glorify you, even if we are not qualified for that. Thank you for letting us offer ourselves and this program to you!

It is our most humble petition that you continue to bless us; in this way we can enter into your *saṅkīrtana* family and never come back to the illusion of material designations. We hope next year to write again from the roads of another country further south in South America, maybe Bolivia or Brazil. I am sure that if it is your desire we will be there with you!

We pray to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara to always remain  
The servants of your servants on Padayātrā America.  
(written by Avadhūta Śiromaṇi Dāsa)

## Padayātrā Worldwide

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet on the occasion of the 110th anniversary of your birth. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We are celebrating 2006 as the twentieth anniversary of Vraja Maṇḍala Parikramā and the thirtieth anniversary of Padayātrā Worldwide.

On Rādhāṣṭamī day, 1 September 1976, in New Delhi, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, gave us some specific instructions about Padayātrā. You described again the many defects of Kali-yuga, with its modern machinery and the cumbersome arrangements for its maintenance. Previously you had instructed us to give up the two German buses we were traveling in to preach in India, and instead to use a bullock cart pulled by two Vedic bulls. Wasting no time, we started the preparations right away for the walk from Vṛndāvana to Māyāpur; we got pots, books and banners and recruited devotees. You were pleased that we had taken your instructions seriously.

As our humble offering, we will share with Your Divine Grace and the assembled devotees what you said to members of the *padayātrā* party, encouraging us in our new venture on the occasion of an inaugural function of *padayātrā* on 12 September 1976 in your quarters in Vṛndāvana:

“When entering a village, camp near a well,” Prabhupāda said. In that way we could stay very clean and also preach to the people going there. He began by talking about Gandhi, who had stressed that whatever material resources are available within the radius of five kilometers around the village, the villagers should depend on that and be happy. Gandhi wanted to inspire people to stay in the villages, and he had a whole program to do it. “They have spent so much; did you know that?” asked Prabhupāda. “The first business was toilet. To restrict them to pass stool here and there, they must have.” Even though he appreciated Gandhi’s intentions, Prabhupāda revealed the mistake in his thinking: “Because they had no engagement, so why they should remain in the village? There are so many attractions in the city, and they get money. The factory-*wallas*, they’re inviting, ‘Come here. You shall get twenty rupees per day.’ Why shall he remain in the village? So if you can organize in the villages, they are interested in chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra and getting nice *prasādam*, then . . . One must have some attachment. In the city there are so many artificial attachments. So on what ground they’ll remain in the village? Mind, always remember this. Unless you have got some attachment, there is no possibility.” This was a simple lesson from the *Bhagavad-gītā* that Prabhupāda was applying to the problem. There must be some higher taste, a more attractive alternative. Gandhi had seen the problem of the modern cities, but Śrīla Prabhupāda saw this through the eyes of scripture, *śāstra-cakṣuḥ*. He still gave Gandhi a bit of credit: “Gandhi’s program was very nice, village organize so that they may not come to the city and help the capitalists. Remain satisfied in the village. But where is that satisfaction? That is the failure.”

Prabhupāda paused for a moment, and I proposed the solution: “If they add the holy name to their lives, they will—”

“Yes,” Prabhupāda jumped in, “if they could understand that. That will be helpful if you chant. Therefore Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s statement will never be false. *Ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam.* If they chant Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, then gradually the dirty things accumulated within the heart will be cleansed. But they are trained up to enjoy the so-called facilities of the city. They are not going to village. But if we can introduce this *hari-saṅkīrtana*, and if they have little taste, that is success. It’s a great program, and that taste will come. And if he comes to the understanding, clear idea, that ‘I want to eat something and my necessities . . . So if I get my necessities, primary necessities of life, and satisfaction of mind, then why shall I go to city?’ That they can have very easily. If they follow the instruction of *Bhagavad-gītā*, that ‘Your first necessity is food.’ So produce food here. *Kṛṣi-go-rakṣya-vāṇijyaṁ*. And you’ll find here so much land is lying vacant. Because they have gone to the city for twenty rupees daily to manufacture Goodyear tire.” Prabhupāda exposed the rascal government: on one side it is advertising that the people return to their village, and at the same time it is keeping them engaged in work the whole day and manufacturing wine, which they will require at the end of the day to forget how tired they are. “Just propaganda,” I said.

“No,” Prabhupāda replied, “they’re thinking that so many people are coming to the cities, how we shall provide them? That’s a problem. After all, government has to supply them rations. Where is so much foodstuff? And if they are not supplied, then there will be resentment.

“What they are doing in the city?” Prabhupāda continued. “So many scooter, *put put put,* going here and there. Actually no engagement. The girls are loitering in the street by dressing. It will become more and more problems, city life. So this is the solution, that they must go back to the village. . . . Just keep in mind the psychology. So that simple life will be possible if one is *bhakta*. *Bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir anyatra syāt*. Only by devotion. Not by otherwise. Not by artificial means, by manufacturing toilet. Only *bhakti*. If they get little attachment for Kṛṣṇa, the questions will be solved automatically. And they will be happy. Undoubtedly. That is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s first instruction. *Ceto-darpaṇa*.”

I told Śrīla Prabhupāda, “I come from a village, and I’m happy now as I have taken to the holy name and *prasādam*. So more, other villagers . . . Half of these boys are from villages . . . They’re chanting, and they’re so happy, and they’re going back to the village \[with this bullock cart program\].”

“Very good life,” Prabhupāda said, shaking his head. “Very good life. The village life. Provided he has got proper engagement. Otherwise his mind will be disturbed and he’ll seek after wine, after women, after this, after that. Devil’s workshop.”

Caraṇāravindam spoke up, wanting to know if it was more difficult to grow vegetables and live off the land now that people do not perform sacrifices anymore. Prabhupāda asked, “What is that sacrifice?”

“Chanting the holy name,” Ravi said.

“This is *yajña*,” Prabhupāda agreed, “This is *yajña*. *Yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*. *Su-medhasaḥ*. Those who have got brain substance, they perform this *saṅkīrtana-yajña*. Those who have cow dung, they cannot. One who has got brain substance. . . . Simply chant, and engage others in chanting. That is *yajña*. First class *yajña*, recommended.”

Responding to a question from Ravi, Śrīla Prabhupāda concluded, “Village or no village, wherever you chant, people will come. That is a fact. Either you chant in Calcutta Maidan or in the village, they will be . . . India is still alive in that sense. We have seen it. We have experience. Wherever we have chanted, people have come.”

I glorified Śrīla Prabhupāda by saying that no one outside of his ISKCON movement does this *kīrtana*. Prabhupāda took the comment as an opportunity to bring an Indian gentleman into the conversation and to speak boldly of bogus religionists: “They do not know what is religion. They do not know what is religion. They have no faith in God. This is their position. All bogus. I say it, challenging, do they know what is God? . . . This is the first time, perhaps, in the world we are introducing, ‘Here is God, Kṛṣṇa.’

“So try to present the real fact,” he said. He assured us, “It will be effective. If there is real reality, just like first-class pure ghee . . . If one tastes, he’ll hanker after. Without any advertisement, by the taste of the ghee, it will go on. Is it not? If you put little pure ghee on the rice, it will be so tasteful that he’ll want it again. But give the pure thing. Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Try to give Him to the people. And how to take it? Hare Kṛṣṇa. He hasn’t got to pay anything. God has given him the tongue. Induce him, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. And that is the beginning. *Sevonmukhe hi jihvādau*. It begins from the *jihvā*. So people will be surprised, ‘How God consciousness begins with *jihvā*?’ That they do not know. *Śāstra* says *sevonmukhe hi jihvādau*. *Jihvā*, the tongue is the beginning of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” He quoted the entire *śloka* beginning *ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanaṁ* and gave his purport to the verse. “So chant as much possible and eat *prasādam*. No education required, no philosophy required. But follow Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction and become guru.

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**“If they have got little brain, then** ***kṛṣṇa-upadeśa*****. Otherwise, Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***upadeśa*****, that’s all. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Very simple. The preacher does not require to be very highly educated. If he follows simply Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction,** ***yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa,*** **then he’s successful. . . . So anyway, make your effort sincerely. The basic principle is this. Induce them to chant and take** ***prasādam*****. You’ll be successful.”**

I promised that we would write Śrīla Prabhupāda regularly, that we would keep him informed of our efforts on *padayātrā*. “Yes. Go to village to village. It is very noble attempt. And if you sincerely preach, Kṛṣṇa will . . . *buddhi-yogaṁ dadāmi taṁ yena mām upayānti te*. There is no doubt.”

We, your lowly servants, beg for the intelligence to go on with this noble attempt to preach from village to village, spreading the holy name worldwide.

Your padayātrī servants on worldwide pilgrimage.  
(written by Lokanātha Swami)

## Prabhupāda Manor

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious day we thank you for teaching us so many things by your perfect example. Thank you for teaching us how to be selfless. You didn’t want to come to the West, but your beloved guru mahārāja asked you to do so, and you were a perfect, obedient son. You gave your entire life to push on this movement. Your books contain all the answers for the final exam of death, and we want you to know how much we appreciate this priceless jewel you’ve given us. When we have doubts or questions as to how we should live our lives, we have your law books as a check and balance. Your expertise in striking like a thunderbolt and being as soft as a rose according to time, place, and circumstance is unparalleled. So many of us who struggle with surrender can see your perfect example in the final lesson as you lay in bed, surrounded by your disciples, preaching even though you seem to barely get the words out of your lotus mouth. Every word you speak is dedicated to saving the conditioned souls in this material world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength to overcome the obstacles on the path of devotional service. Please don’t let us fall away from your lotus feet. You say that if we chant sixteen rounds a day, follow the four regulative principals, take *only kṛṣṇa-prasādam*, read your books daily, and associate with devotees, then *māyā* cannot touch us. We have firm faith in your instructions, and we humbly beg you, “Please don’t let us fall down.” You live forever in your books, and we can benefit by taking your association daily. Surrender to guru and Kṛṣṇa is the perfection of our lives, and your life is a testimony to this open secret. Thank you for embracing us as a loving father. Please let us know how we can serve you.

Your servants at Prabhupāda Manor, Cleveland, Ohio, USA.

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All eternal glories unto your lotus feet!

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.14.8), Lord Brahma prays:

*tat te ’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo*  
*bhuñjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam*  
*hṛd-vāg-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jīveta yo mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk*

**“One who seeks Your compassion and thus tolerates all kinds of adverse conditions due to the karma of his past deeds, who engages always in Your devotional service with his mind, words, and body, and who always offers obeisances unto You is certainly a bona fide candidate for liberation.”**

And in the purport (*Bhāgavatam* 8.4.13) you explain:

A devotee who tolerates everything in this material world and patiently executes his devotional service can become *mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk*, a bona fide candidate for liberation. The word *dāya-bhāk* refers to a hereditary right to the Lord’s mercy. A devotee must simply engage in devotional service, not caring about material situations. Then he automatically becomes a rightful candidate for promotion to Vaikuṇṭhaloka. The devotee who renders unalloyed service to the Lord gets the right to be promoted to Vaikuṇṭhaloka, just as a son inherits the property of his father.

You exhibited all these qualities, and in fact you are beyond the simple conception of *mukti*. You are situated on the pure, spiritual, transcendental platform at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. You were put into so many difficult situations. You accepted such adverse conditions of life to be the mercy of the Lord. You came to this Western world with full compassion to deliver the sinful. It is your causeless mercy and compassion that protect and maintain our hesitant steps toward Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

It is our misfortune that you were with us such a short time. This adds emphasis to one of the most important instructions you left us while performing your *vapu*-disappearing pastimes. You pointed out that our love for you will be shown by how much we cooperate together. However, to unite, band together, or join forces means that we have to become personal with each other while sometimes compromising our own individual thoughts and ideas and eliminating fanaticism.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya-līlā* 14.16) you write:

A student of Kṛṣṇa consciousness must receive Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy; then his devotional service will quickly succeed. This was the case with King Pratāparudra. One has to be noticed by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and a little service with sincere efforts will convince the Lord that one is a proper candidate for returning home, back to Godhead. Mahārāja Pratāparudra did not have a chance to meet Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, but when the Lord saw that the King was serving Lord Jagannātha as a menial sweeper, the Lord’s mercy upon the King became a solid fact. When Mahārāja Pratāparudra, in the dress of a Vaiṣṇava, was serving the Lord, the Lord did not even inquire who he was. Rather, He had compassion upon him and began to embrace him.

Similarly, we are in the dress of Vaiṣṇavas and we are trying to perform some service for your preaching movement. In this way we hope to be noticed by you and embraced by you even though we are so fallen. You were so lenient and tolerant of our impersonal natures and showered your mercy upon us. We lovingly tried to reflect your mannerisms and tried to deal with each other with love, affection, and a personal tenderness we never knew we possessed. You compassionately preached the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, trying to purify our steel hearts filled with *nirviśeṣa* and *śūnyavādī* philosophy.

For one to actually engage in pure devotional service, the preliminary qualification is to become a willing cooperator and voluntarily serve the pure devotee who is already engaged in the transcendental devotional service of the Lord. You knew and understood that for us to continue to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and maintain the ISKCON society you founded, we must remain together like a bundle of sticks.

You treated each of us — man, woman, and child — as individuals, understanding our weaknesses and shortcomings. Like a loving father, you personally encouraged and guided us in becoming however minutely useful we could be. Like Lord Rāmacandra, you organized an army of monkeylike humans, transforming our animal tendencies and training us to engage in devotional service of the Lord.

On this 110th anniversary of your appearance day we pray to embrace your instructions and personalize our relationships with our godbrothers and -sisters, forgiving whatever offenses that have been done to us, and praying for forgiveness for whatever offenses we may have done to others.

You wrote in a letter to Upendra Prabhu on 6 August 1970, “Our Society is like one big family and our relationships should be based on love and trust. We must give up the fighting spirit and use our intelligence to push ahead.” By cooperating to serve your lotus feet, we can become proportionately free from the bondage of material nature. We hope to show you how much love we do have for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by continuing to cooperate with each other for your eternal glorification.

Your aspiring devotees in Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.

## Radio Kṛṣṇa Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most dear and beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I beg you to please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which, step after step, are removing all kinds of illusion from the whole material world and destroying our overwhelming false ego, leading us back home, back to Godhead.

Your fame is spread all over the three worlds.

With all good reasons, every fortunate soul in the universe is constantly chanting your glories, and this special recurring day, the anniversary of your appearance, gives everyone the opportunity to participate in the choir of your faithful servants enthusiastically honoring Your Divine Grace.

Certainly, offering the highest honor to Your Divine Grace, especially with chosen words of praise, full respect, and obedience, and manifesting the desire to surrender to your lotus feet, is the natural consequence for a nonenvious soul after observing or hearing about your extraordinary activities and achievements.

Lord Kṛṣṇa, in His form of time, has revealed Your Divine Grace’s greatness so much that even we, who have no spiritual vision, can understand your position as Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Goswami Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda, Jagat Guru.

Failing to recognize Your Divine Grace as a topmost pure devotee in the highest position among Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s representatives is a sign of blindness or envy.

At the moment of His appearance, Pṛthu Mahārāja, a *śaktyāveśa* incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, heard the assembled devotees glorify His qualities and achievements. But even though the offering of prayers was certainly appropriate, since he is an incarnation of Godhead, he gently asked them to stop because he had not yet manifested such extraordinary glories by his actions.

\[Pṛthu Mahārāja said:\] “O gentle reciters, offer such prayers in due course of time, when the qualities of which you have spoken actually manifest themselves in me.” \[Purport:\] He wanted to stress that one who does not actually possess these qualities should not try to engage his followers and devotees in offering him glory for them, even though these qualities might be manifest in the future. If a man who does not factually possess the attributes of a great personality engages his followers in praising him with the expectation that such attributes will develop in the future, that sort of praise is actually an insult. . . .  
\[King Pṛthu continued:\] “My dear devotees, headed by the *sūta*, just now I am not very famous for my personal activities because I have not done anything praiseworthy you could glorify. Therefore how could I engage you in praising my activities exactly like children?” \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.15.23, 26\]

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have already manifested your greatness over and over again, so much so that no amount of praise can properly describe your glories.

Actually, only by your mercy can we have the opportunity to clean our hearts by offering prayers and praise to Your Divine Grace. However expert one may be, he can never describe your glories adequately. Nonetheless, one should try to do so as far as possible. Such an attempt will please the Supreme Personality of Godhead (Śrī Kṛṣṇa) and the whole *paramparā*.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, protect us from falling into Māyās trap and being allured into thinking that we are great personalities. If any glorification is aimed at us, we should know for sure that is actually directed to Your Divine Grace, and we should immediately deliver it to your lotus feet. If we fail to do so, then in no time we will find ourselves surrounded by sycophants who will certainly smooth our way to hell.

You give us the vision  
Above the division.  
You give us a place  
Beyond time and space.  
You give us instructions  
Removing obstructions.  
You give as the science  
To base our reliance.  
You give us attention  
Beyond comprehension.  
You give us the path  
Out of this worldly wrath.  
You give us salvation  
Beyond expectation.  
You give us conclusion  
To our confusion.  
You give us your guide  
To curb our pride.  
You give us dependence  
from your transcendence  
You give us the fire  
To spark the desire.  
You give us your Lords  
By the sound of your words.  
You give us pure *bhakti*  
By means of your *śakti.*  
You give us the chance  
To see the Lord dance.  
You give us the mission:  
Your teachings’ transmission.  
And still from above  
You give us your love.

Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have nothing to offer Your Divine Grace but our own unworthy selves. Please protect us from Māyās allurements, so that we may not fall back into the material condition of life and again become one of the unfortunate persons, deviated by the false ego and hopelessly trying to usurp your “as good as God” position.

You are always the master; we are always the servants.

We wholeheartedly pray to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, to please give us the power to always remember Śrīla Prabhupāda, his instructions, and his pastimes, and to enroll us among the servants of His Divine Grace’s lotus feet, life after life.

Your menial servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi Dāsa)

## Rainbow Gathering Kṛṣṇa Camp

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your life and example! All glories to the long line of teachers before you! All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the golden moon of devotion and the beloved of the devotees!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us the sweetness of devotional service and *bhakti-yoga*. We feel you at the Rainbow Gathering. Devotees are excited, motivated, creative, loving, and teaching, serving lots of *prasādam*, chanting the holy names. We are gathered with tens of thousands of sisters and brothers from all around the planet, gathered for peace in this stunning temple of nature. Kindness and devotion are all around.

Dear spiritual father, you have blessed this seeker with spiritual life and *sādhana*, and you provide constant inspiration with your call for simple living and high thinking. You saved me from material life and blessed me with the sweet nectar of the association of your devotees.

Parama-guru Mahārāja, please bless this gathering with your wisdom and your love, that we may love one another as you love us.

Your loving servants at the Rainbow Gathering Kṛṣṇa Camp.  
(written by Vrajabhūmi Devī Dāsī)

## Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who with boundless compassion continue to lead us through the dark passageway of this material world into the light of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental abode.

As Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E. enters its fifth year, we thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing us to assist those who are dear to you as they prepare for their final journey back home, back to Godhead. We feel blessed to have been given this service by Your Divine Grace, and we pray for the knowledge, compassion, and inspiration to continue caring for those who have given their lives to you. To better serve your devotees worldwide, our team of hospice volunteers has increased this year and now spans twenty-two countries on six continents. We pray that you are pleased with our humble efforts.

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we at Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E. beg to continue to serve you by caring for your dear devotees now and forever.

Your humble servants at Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E.  
(Counseling, Assistance, Resource, and Education for the Terminally Ill and Their Families)
